6. Design Principles

P1. Resources are explicit, typed, and accounted

Statement. Every schedulable unit — CPU cores, memory bytes, GPU devices, network bandwidth, wall-clock time — is a named TRES dimension. No resource is allocated without an explicit entry in ResourceSpec, a bit in CoreBitmap or GpuSet, and a corresponding accrual in hpcsched-acct.

Precise explanation. ResourceSpec carries typed fields: cpus_per_task, mem_per_node, gpus, time_limit. hpcsched-alloc translates these into bitmap set-bits and memory counters on each Node. hpcsched-acct records the Allocation x duration as TRES-seconds. Nothing flows without a record.

Tradeoffs. Explicit typing adds verbosity to the submission API and the allocation path. The payoff: I1 (no double-allocation) and I10 (accounting accuracy) become checkable assertions. Implicit resources (e.g., undeclared scratch disk) become out-of-band and untraceable — accept that tradeoff explicitly or add the TRES dimension.

Operational implications. Adding a new resource type (e.g., RDMA HCAs) requires: new field in ResourceSpec, new bitmap or counter in Node, new accrual logic in hpcsched-acct, new limit enforcement in hpcsched-queue. No structural exception path exists; every new TRES dimension follows the same code path.

Violation scenario. A noded instance autonomously reserves two extra cores "for daemon overhead" without recording them in the controller bitmap. The controller shows those cores as free. A second job is allocated them. Node hits 100% CPU; workloads thrash. Root cause is invisible to sacct because the implicit reservation was never a TRES record.

P2. The controller is the single source of truth

Statement. hpcsched-controld holds the authoritative state of every Job, Node, Allocation, and Reservation. hpcsched-noded is a dumb executor: it launches, monitors, enforces cgroups, and reports. It makes no allocation decisions and caches no authoritative state.

Precise explanation. All decisions — which job runs on which node, when a job is preempted, whether a node is drained — originate in controld and are conveyed to noded via LaunchMsg, CancelMsg, DrainMsg. noded stores only ephemeral process state (PIDs, cgroup handles) sufficient to execute current instructions.

Tradeoffs. Central authority is a throughput bottleneck at extreme scale (millions of nodes). Two-level (Mesos-style) or optimistic (Omega-style) models avoid the bottleneck but risk conflicting allocations. For HPC cluster sizes (hundreds to tens of thousands of nodes), central authority with a fast scheduling pass is correct and operable.

Operational implications. Controller loss is a cluster-wide scheduling pause (Phase 12 mitigates with standby). Node daemon crashes lose no authoritative state; on restart noded re-registers and re-receives LaunchMsg for running jobs. Debugging tool: dump controld state at any time and fully reconstruct cluster reality.

Violation scenario. A noded instance locally decides to "help" by releasing a core early when a process exits before controld processes the completion event. Controller bitmap still marks that core allocated. Scheduler skips it for new jobs. Utilization drops silently. The freed core is invisible until the job's Completing→Completed transition is processed by controld.

P3. Never double-allocate

Statement. A resource unit (one bit in CoreBitmap, one entry in GpuSet, one memory byte range) is held by at most one job at any instant. Allocation is a compare-and-set transaction over the bitmaps, WAL-committed before the job transitions to Configuring.

Precise explanation. hpcsched-alloc acquires a write lock on the target node's CoreBitmap, checks that the required bits are clear, sets them, appends a JobAllocated WAL record, flushes, then releases the lock and advances job state. Partial failures roll back: if WAL flush fails, the bits are cleared and the allocation is retried or rejected.

Tradeoffs. Transactional bitmaps serialize allocation. Parallel allocation for independent jobs is possible if they target disjoint node sets (separate per-node locks). Start with a single allocation lock per node; shard by NUMA domain at scale. Bitmap CAS is O(N/64) where N = cores per node — negligible at current hardware scales.

Operational implications. hpcsched-alloc holds a write lock on the affected node's CoreBitmap for the duration of the compare-and-set + WAL append. Readers (squeue, sinfo) take read locks or snapshot the bitmap. The WAL record for JobAllocated encodes the exact bitmap diff (node_id, core_mask, gpu_mask, mem_bytes); replay re-applies it atomically.

Violation scenario. Controller crashes after setting CoreBitmap bits but before writing the WAL record. On restart, replay sees no JobAllocated record; those bits are cleared. Both the original job and a new job can be allocated those cores. Two processes compete for the same hardware. P6 (durability before ack) prevents this: the bit-set is never exposed to the user before WAL flush.

P4. Backfill must not delay the top job

Statement. The highest-priority pending job always gets a Reservation: a (start_time, node_set) pair computed from current allocations' projected end times. No backfilled job may start on any node in the reservation's node_set if its projected completion time exceeds the reservation start_time. I4 is the machine-checkable form.

Precise explanation. hpcsched-sched computes the top-job reservation at the start of every backfill pass (Phase 7). For each lower-priority job considered for backfill, it verifies: job.time_limit + now <= reservation.start_time on all required nodes. Any job that fails this check is skipped (not launched) and noted in the backfill report metric.

Tradeoffs. Computing the reservation requires projecting job end times from declared time limits, which users may underestimate. Over-conservative estimates shrink the backfill window; optimistic estimates can delay the top job. Use declared time_limit as the upper bound; if a job runs over its limit it is killed (enforced by hpcsched-noded), ensuring projection accuracy. Never use historical average runtimes as projections without an explicit policy flag.

Operational implications. BackfillPlan entries are validated against the current reservation before dispatch; a job whose time-limit projection would now violate the reservation is dropped from the plan. Metric: backfill_reservation_violation_total should be 0. Reservation is logged with (job_id, reserved_start, reserved_nodes) for auditability.

Violation scenario. Backfill loop launches a job estimated to finish at T+4h. Top job reservation starts at T+3h on the same nodes. Top job waits 1h extra. SLO violated. Users lose trust in fairness. The invariant I4 check at plan validation time catches this before dispatch; the backfill entry is dropped.

P5. Fair-share is hierarchical and decays

Statement. Each user belongs to an Account, accounts nest in a tree, each tree node has a shares weight. Usage decays with half-life T_half so that recent overuse matters more than historical overuse. Fair-share factor = (shares / sum_sibling_shares) / (decayed_usage / cluster_usage). This is an explicit, documented, reproducible formula (I8).

Precise explanation. hpcsched-fairshare walks the Account / Association tree bottom-up, accumulating usage_decayed at each level. Decay: usage_decayed[t] = usage_decayed[t-1] * exp(-ln(2) * dt / T_half) + usage_current. Priority = weighted sum: age_w * age_factor + fairshare_w * fs_factor + qos_w * qos_priority + size_w * size_factor + partition_w * partition_tier.

Tradeoffs. Decay smoothing introduces a lag: a burst user retains priority penalty for ~T_half after stopping. Shorter half-life = faster recovery, more variable ranking. Typical T_half = 14 days; configurable per association. Hierarchical accumulation means a group's overuse penalizes all members — intended behavior, documented explicitly.

Operational implications. hpcsched-fairshare recomputes decayed usage on a background timer (period = config fairshare_decay_interval, default 5 min) independently of the scheduling pass. The Account / Association tree is loaded from config + acct records. Changes to shares require tree reload, not restart. PriorityFactors breakdown is persisted per job for sacct -o Priority auditability.

Violation scenario. Usage accumulator is never decayed (timer bug). High-volume group from week 1 has permanent lowest priority forever. New users in the same account get no throughput. Fair-share purpose defeated. Decay timer correctness is tested by Phase 14 conformance fixtures seeded with synthetic historical usage and expected priority rankings.

P6. Durability before acknowledgement

Statement. A submit RPC returns success to the client only after the WAL record (JobSubmitted, including JobId and full ResourceSpec) is fsync-ed to durable storage. Controller restart after the ack never loses the job. This is I5's enforcement mechanism.

Precise explanation. hpcsched-state batches WAL records (up to wal_flush_batch_ms milliseconds or wal_flush_batch_count records), calls fdatasync(), then sends acks to all waiting submit RPCs. No ack is sent before the batch flush returns successfully. On startup, WAL replay re-inserts all acked jobs into the pending queue before accepting new RPCs.

Tradeoffs. WAL fsync on the critical path adds latency (typically 1–5 ms on NVMe, 5–20 ms on HDD). Batching amortizes this: 64 submits per flush costs the same as 1. Batch size is a config knob (default: 5 ms or 64 records). Never skip fsync for "performance" without setting an explicit durability = relaxed config flag with documented data-loss risk.

Operational implications. Metrics: wal_flush_latency_p50_ms, wal_flush_latency_p99_ms, wal_records_per_flush histogram. Checkpoint frequency reduces replay time on restart; checkpoint itself is fsync-ed before old WAL segments are truncated. Monitor wal_segment_count to alert on checkpoint staleness.

Violation scenario. WAL write is async (no fsync). Controller crashes 2 ms after acking submit. Job is missing on restart. User resubmits. Original job may have been partially dispatched to noded before crash. Duplicate job execution. If job output is non-idempotent, data corruption results. P3 violation also possible if bitmap bits were set without WAL.

P7. Graceful degradation

Statement. Node loss (heartbeat timeout → NodeState::Down) requeues affected requeueable jobs (JobState::Requeued) and fails non-requeueable jobs (JobState::Failed). The scheduling loop continues without the lost node. No panic, no wedge, no manual intervention required. This is I6's operational enforcement.

Precise explanation. hpcsched-controld heartbeat monitor runs on a dedicated timer, independent of the scheduling loop. On timeout: atomically set Node.state to Down; release its CoreBitmap/GpuSet/mem bits with a NodeDown WAL record encoding the bitmap diff; iterate affected running jobs, set Requeued or Failed per job's requeueable flag; enqueue Requeued jobs back into PendingQueue.

Tradeoffs. Requeue inflates queue depth transiently. Jobs with --no-requeue are permanently lost on node failure; operators must document this per partition. Drain (NodeState::Draining) is a softer path: new jobs excluded, running jobs finish, then node transitions to Drain for maintenance. Drain preserves running jobs at the cost of delayed maintenance.

Operational implications. heartbeat_timeout = 3x heartbeat_interval (configurable). Metric: node_fault_events_total, jobs_requeued_total, jobs_failed_on_node_fault_total. Alert: if node fault rate > threshold, may indicate network partition — escalate before auto-requeue flood. Phase 10 details node-return reconciliation (node re-registers, bitmap diff is reconciled).

Violation scenario. Node goes silent but heartbeat monitor timer is misconfigured to 30 minutes. Jobs appear Running but processes are dead. Accounting accrues wasted wall-time. Other jobs wait for those nodes. Late detection = large blast radius. I6 is violated for the detection gap duration. Conformance fixture: verify Down transition within 3 * heartbeat_interval.

P8. Scheduling is a deterministic function of state

Statement. Given identical controller state (pending queue, node inventory, fair-share factors, current time), a scheduling pass produces identical decisions: same jobs allocated, same nodes assigned, same priority ordering. No randomness, no hidden mutable globals. I8 is the accounting-level expression; P8 is the scheduler-level expression.

Precise explanation. hpcsched-sched takes a ScheduleCtx { now: Instant, state: &ControllerState } parameter. All state influencing scheduling is captured in this struct. No call to rand::random() inside scheduling logic. Tiebreaks use deterministic comparators (job_id ordering, submit_time). Tests inject now explicitly to reproduce any past scheduling decision.

Tradeoffs. Determinism requires that all scheduling inputs are explicit and WAL-captured. Wall-clock time is a scheduling input (age factor, backfill projections); it must be injected, not read from SystemTime::now() inside scheduling code. Side-effect: can replay any historical decision given a WAL snapshot.

Operational implications. Phase 14 conformance fixtures record (input state, expected allocations) pairs. A scheduled CI job replays fixtures to detect regressions. Debugging: replay WAL to a point in time, run scheduling pass, compare to historical decisions recorded in job event log.

Violation scenario. Scheduler reads rand::random() to break ties. Two identical pending jobs produce non-deterministic relative ordering. Conformance test flaps. User A starves intermittently with no explainable reason. Audit trail cannot reconstruct why job B was chosen over job A. Fair-share fairness audits fail.

P9. Bounded scheduling latency

Statement. A scheduling pass over the pending queue completes within max_sched_pass_ms (default 500 ms). Backfill is depth-limited to max_backfill_jobs (default 200). The scheduling loop yields to heartbeat processing between passes to prevent node timeouts.

Precise explanation. hpcsched-sched tracks elapsed time during the backfill scan; if elapsed > max_backfill_time_fraction * max_sched_pass_ms, the scan terminates early. Main scheduling pass is similarly time-bounded. After each pass, the event loop processes any queued heartbeats and RPC responses before starting the next pass.

Tradeoffs. Depth limit means backfill misses low-priority jobs beyond position max_backfill_jobs in the queue. Intentional: unbounded backfill scan at 10k+ pending jobs exceeds the latency budget. Increase depth limit only with measured pass latency headroom. P9 and P4 are in tension: deeper backfill improves P4 quality but risks P9 violation.

Operational implications. hpcsched-sched emits sched_pass_duration_ms histogram. Alert if p99 > 0.8 * max_sched_pass_ms. Backfill depth is a runtime config knob; raise it during off-peak windows if needed. Phase 14 load test: 10k pending jobs, P99 pass duration < 500 ms, heartbeat processing not starved.

Violation scenario. Backfill depth is unbounded. At 50k pending jobs, backfill scan takes 8 seconds. Heartbeat messages queue up. Three nodes time out during the scan, are marked Down, and trigger requeue cascades. The next pass is also slow. Cluster enters a degradation spiral that requires manual restart.

P10. Crash-only controller with failover

Statement. The controller has no graceful shutdown path that is necessary for correctness. Kill -9 at any point; the standby (Phase 12) resumes from the last WAL record + checkpoint with no manual repair. Fencing ensures the old primary cannot re-awaken and allocate (I9).

Precise explanation. Fencing mechanism: primary holds a lease (TTL = 2x heartbeat interval) in a consensus store (etcd or equivalent). Standby monitors lease expiry; on expiry, acquires lease and promotes. Before issuing any allocations, new primary writes a fence token to all noded instances; noded rejects any LaunchMsg not bearing the current fence token.

Tradeoffs. Crash-only design requires all in-flight operations to either commit to WAL before taking effect or be idempotent on re-execution. Partially-written WAL records are detected by trailing checksum and truncated on replay; the corresponding operation is re-attempted by the new primary. Lease TTL is a tradeoff between failover latency and false-positive fencing.

Operational implications. Metrics: failover_events_total, failover_duration_ms, fence_token_reject_total. Operators test failover monthly via chaos kill of the primary. WAL segments and checkpoint are stored on a shared durable volume accessible to both primary and standby. Phase 12 documents the full fencing protocol and lease store configuration.

Violation scenario. Fencing is skipped to speed up failover. Old primary recovers from a transient network partition, finds its state stale, and issues a LaunchMsg for a job the new primary already allocated to different nodes. Two noded instances launch different jobs on the same cores. I9 is violated. Workloads corrupt each other's output. Data loss is likely non-recoverable.

P11. Idempotency at every external boundary

Statement. Every operation a client or peer daemon can retry — submit RPC, WAL replay, noded re-registration, launch dispatch — is safe to repeat: the same input produces the same observable outcome, never a duplicate side effect. This is I11's design-level statement.

Precise explanation. client_token deduplicates SubmitJob (Phase 3); replay_record is pure and idempotent per WAL record (crash recovery and standby tailing share the same function, Phase 12); noded's StepId and fence-token checks make LaunchMsg/CancelStepMsg safe to re-deliver (Phase 6, Phase 12). Every retry path in the system reduces to "apply the same deterministic function to the same durable input."

Tradeoffs. Idempotency requires threading a dedup key or sequence number through every mutating call, which adds a field to nearly every RPC and WAL record. The alternative — exactly-once delivery — is not achievable over unreliable networks without consensus overhead disproportionate to an HPC control plane's needs; at-least-once delivery plus idempotent handlers is strictly simpler and matches P6/P10's crash-only philosophy.

Operational implications. Any new RPC or WAL record type added post-launch must carry a natural dedup key (job/step/record id) before it can be safely retried; code review gate: "what happens if this message is delivered twice?" must have a documented answer. Metrics: duplicate_launch_attempts_total, submit_duplicate_token_total should track (not alert on) expected retry volume.

Violation scenario. A future CLI feature adds a "bulk resubmit" RPC without a dedup key. A flaky network causes the client to retry; the controller creates two Jobs for one user intent. The user is billed twice and sees duplicate output directories. Root cause: the new RPC was added without applying P11.

P12. Observability is a first-class design constraint

Statement. Every state transition, invariant check, and scheduling decision emits both a durable WAL record and an operator-visible metric or structured log event. Diagnosing production behavior must never require attaching a debugger or reading source code. This is I12's design-level statement.

Precise explanation. Every phase's N.10 Operational Notes defines the metrics, logs, and traces for that phase's subsystem (Prometheus counters/gauges/histograms, tracing spans, structured JSON logs); §7's invariants each carry a Telemetry row mapping the invariant directly to a counter that "must be 0." The Operational Readiness back-matter section aggregates these into dashboards, SLOs, and runbooks. No scheduling decision is made through a code path that lacks a corresponding span or counter.

Tradeoffs. Comprehensive instrumentation adds a small constant overhead per operation (span creation, counter increment) and a design tax: every new feature must specify its telemetry alongside its logic, not after. The alternative — instrument only after an incident — means the first occurrence of any bug class is undiagnosable; for a system whose failure modes include split-brain and double-allocation, that tradeoff is unacceptable (see Threat Model, Failure Modes & Recovery).

Operational implications. Code review gate: a PR introducing a new state transition or RPC without an accompanying metric/span/log is incomplete. Metric cardinality is bounded per the Metrics Strategy label-cardinality budget and Phase 13.6 Pitfalls (do not label by job_id or user); dashboards (Operational Readiness D1–D4) are the primary on-call interface, not greping raw logs.

Violation scenario. A future preemption-tuning feature ships without a preemption_events_total{mode} counter. Preemption storms during a QoS misconfiguration go unnoticed until users escalate; the on-call engineer has no dashboard signal and must reconstruct the incident from WAL replay alone, tripling mean time to resolution.

7. System Invariants

I1 — No double-allocation of resource units

Attribute Detail
Statement Every allocatable unit (core / GPU / mem byte) is assigned to at most one running job at any instant
Why it matters Prevents resource contention and correctness violations in running workloads; I1 is the primary safety guarantee
Enforced in hpcsched-alloc: compare-and-set on CoreBitmap/GpuSet before WAL commit; hpcsched-state: WAL record integrity
Detection Integrity scan at startup and after failover: sum all live Allocation bitmaps per node; assert pairwise disjoint
Telemetry allocation_conflict_total counter — must be 0 in production; alert on any non-zero value
Recovery Halt new scheduling on the affected node; alert ops; trigger controlled failover for manual bitmap audit
Debugging Replay WAL to conflict timestamp; diff CoreBitmap at each JobAllocated record; identify overlapping commit sequence

I2 — Node TRES sum never exceeds capacity

Attribute Detail
Statement Sum of a node's live allocations never exceeds node capacity in any TRES dimension (cores, mem, GPUs)
Why it matters Prevents kernel-level OOM kills, CPU oversubscription, and GPU memory corruption on compute nodes
Enforced in hpcsched-alloc: capacity pre-check before bitmap commit; hpcsched-controld: heartbeat ACK handler re-verifies node TRES sum
Detection Per-node: assert popcount(CoreBitmap) <= node.cpus; sum(alloc_mem) <= node.mem; popcount(GpuSet) <= node.gpus
Telemetry node_tres_overcommit_total{node,tres} gauge — must be 0; also track node_utilization_pct per TRES dimension
Recovery Refuse new allocations on that node; schedule a drain; investigate bitmap inconsistency via WAL replay
Debugging Compare CoreBitmap popcount against Node.cpus field; cross-reference against all JobAllocated records for that node

I3 — Running job has Allocation; Pending job has None

Attribute Detail
Statement A Job in Running state has a valid non-empty Allocation; a Job in Pending state has Allocation = None
Why it matters Prevents phantom running jobs (appear busy, no resources actually reserved) and orphaned allocations (bitmaps held with no job)
Enforced in hpcsched-controld: JobState FSM transition gate — Running transition requires Allocation.is_some(); Pending requires Allocation.is_none()
Detection FSM guard returns Err on illegal transition; audit scan on WAL replay checks (state, alloc) consistency
Telemetry job_state_allocation_mismatch_total{from_state,to_state} — must be 0; log job_id on any mismatch
Recovery Running job with no allocation: transition to Failed + free any partial bitmaps; Pending job with stale alloc: clear alloc, keep Pending
Debugging Scan WAL for JobAllocated record preceding Running transition; missing record = crash between allocation and state advancement

I4 — Backfilled job does not delay top pending job

Attribute Detail
Statement For every job in BackfillPlan: job.submit_time + job.time_limit <= reservation.start_time on all nodes in the reservation's node_set
Why it matters The highest-priority pending job is never delayed by lower-priority backfill; this is the EASY backfill correctness condition
Enforced in hpcsched-sched: pre-dispatch validation loop in backfill_pass(); any entry violating the condition is dropped before launch
Detection Assertion in backfill_pass() before dispatching each BackfillPlan entry; logged if dropped
Telemetry backfill_reservation_violation_total — must be 0; backfill_jobs_dropped_total tracks dropped entries
Recovery Drop violating backfill entry from plan; do not launch; recompute reservation at next pass
Debugging Log: (backfill_job_id, projected_end, reservation_start, delta_ms, affected_nodes) for any drop event

I5 — WAL replay reconstructs exact pre-crash state

Attribute Detail
Statement Controller state after WAL replay + checkpoint equals the committed state at the moment of crash, with no uncommitted records applied
Why it matters Crash-only restarts lose no committed work; I5 is the correctness guarantee for P6 and P10
Enforced in hpcsched-state: WAL record trailing checksum; idempotent record application; checkpoint CRC stored alongside snapshot
Detection Startup self-test: replay WAL; compare final state hash against checkpoint's expected-hash field
Telemetry wal_replay_records_total, wal_replay_duration_ms, checkpoint_crc_mismatch_total — alert on non-zero CRC mismatches
Recovery On CRC mismatch: refuse to start; alert ops; restore from last verified backup checkpoint
Debugging Enable WAL trace log (record-level diff logging); replay record-by-record; binary-search for state divergence point

I6 — Down/Drain nodes receive no new allocations

Attribute Detail
Statement A node in Down or Drain state holds no newly-scheduled jobs; jobs on a Down node are requeued or failed; Drain node lets running jobs finish
Why it matters Prevents wasted allocations to unreachable nodes; enables safe maintenance without disrupting the scheduling loop
Enforced in hpcsched-controld: scheduling pass skips nodes not in {Idle, Allocated, Mixed}; allocate() checks Node.state before bit-set
Detection Pre-allocation node-state gate; post-allocation audit asserts no Down/Drain node in any new Allocation
Telemetry alloc_to_down_node_total — must be 0; node_state_transitions_total{from,to} for operational tracking
Recovery If allocation lands on Down node (race): immediately requeue or fail the job; remove node from allocation in WAL
Debugging Check Node.state at time of allocation commit in WAL; correlate with NodeStateChange records for timeline

I7 — Job FSM transitions are strictly ordered

Attribute Detail
Statement Job state transitions follow the documented FSM (see Phase 1); no transition skips Completing/cleanup step
Why it matters Prevents resource leaks (unreleased CoreBitmap bits) and accounting errors (missing epilog, incomplete TRES accrual)
Enforced in hpcsched-core: JobState FSM with explicit transition table; illegal transitions return Err; gate implemented as match in job_mgr
Detection FSM gate returns Err on illegal transition; error is logged with (job_id, from_state, to_state, caller)
Telemetry job_illegal_state_transition_total{from,to} — alert on any non-zero; job_state_duration_ms{state} histogram
Recovery Reject illegal transition; leave job in prior state; alert ops for manual inspection and potential bitmap audit
Debugging WAL trace: sequence of JobStateChange records for the job; compare against FSM adjacency table; identify missing intermediate state

I8 — Priority is a documented, reproducible function

Attribute Detail
Statement Fair-share priority is a monotonic function of PriorityFactors with fixed, documented weights; same inputs always produce the same output
Why it matters Enables auditable, contestable scheduling decisions; prevents unexplained starvation and supports SLA compliance reporting
Enforced in hpcsched-fairshare: pure function compute_priority(f: PriorityFactors, w: Weights) -> u32; no side effects, no global reads
Detection Unit tests assert monotonicity per factor (increase age → increase priority); conformance fixtures check ranking against expected order
Telemetry priority_recompute_duration_ms histogram; priority_factor_breakdown{factor} gauge per job (for sacct analog)
Recovery If weights are changed: recompute all pending jobs' priorities before next pass; log old/new values with timestamp
Debugging Log PriorityFactors breakdown per job per scheduling pass; correlate ranking change with specific factor delta

I9 — No double-allocation across controller failover

Attribute Detail
Statement Across a controller failover, no resource unit is allocated by both old and new controller; the fencing protocol holds
Why it matters Prevents I1 violation specifically across failover boundary; loss of fencing makes Phase 12 HA unsafe
Enforced in hpcsched-state + Phase 12: lease-based fencing in consensus store; noded rejects any LaunchMsg not bearing current fence token
Detection noded reports fence_token_reject_total counter; new primary verifies no pending allocations from previous primary before issuing new ones
Telemetry failover_events_total, fence_token_reject_total, failover_duration_ms — alert on any fence rejection
Recovery If noded receives stale-primary launch: reject, report to new primary, do not start job step; new primary reconciles
Debugging Inspect fence token in LaunchMsg vs. current noded token; compare WAL on both primary instances to identify dual-allocation window

I10 — Accounting TRES-seconds equals allocated TRES times duration

Attribute Detail
Statement Accounting TRES-seconds for a completed job = (allocated TRES per dimension) × (end_time − start_time), within clock-rounding tolerance
Why it matters Ensures fair-share decay is based on accurate usage; prevents gaming via short-jobs that under-report; billing accuracy
Enforced in hpcsched-acct: usage record written at Completing transition; fields: alloc_cpus, alloc_mem_mb, alloc_gpus, start_time, end_time
Detection Post-completion audit: recompute TRES-seconds from Allocation + timestamps; compare against stored acct record; log discrepancy
Telemetry acct_tres_seconds_discrepancy_total{tres_type} — must be 0; acct_job_records_total, acct_write_latency_ms
Recovery Flag discrepant record for manual audit; do not silently adjust; re-derive from WAL if record is corrupt
Debugging Cross-reference WAL JobAllocated (start of billing) and JobCompleted (end of billing); recompute expected value; diff against acct record

I11 — Retried operations are idempotent

Attribute Detail
Statement Re-delivering any client or peer-daemon message (SubmitJob, WAL record replay, LaunchMsg, CancelStepMsg, noded re-registration) produces the same resulting state as delivering it exactly once
Why it matters At-least-once delivery is the only realistic network model between CLI, controld, and noded; without this invariant every retry is a latent double-effect bug (P11)
Enforced in hpcsched-controld: client_token dedup map (Phase 3); hpcsched-state: replay_record pure-function application (Phase 3, Phase 12); hpcsched-noded: StepId-keyed step table and fence-token check (Phase 6, Phase 12)
Detection Property test: apply any operation sequence twice (once with duplicates injected), assert identical final state
Telemetry submit_duplicate_token_total, duplicate_launch_attempts_total, wal_replay_duplicate_skipped_total — nonzero is expected (retries happen), but must never correlate with a state-divergence alert
Recovery N/A by design — the retried call is a no-op; if divergence is ever observed, treat as an I1/I5 emergency (idempotency guard itself failed)
Debugging Replay the exact duplicate call against a WAL snapshot in a test harness; compare state before/after the second delivery

I12 — Every state transition is externally observable

Attribute Detail
Statement Every JobState/NodeState transition and every invariant check produces both a WAL record (or acct record) and a corresponding metric, span, or structured log line within the same operation (P12)
Why it matters Operators must diagnose production incidents from dashboards and logs alone; a transition with no telemetry is a diagnostic dead end during an incident
Enforced in Each phase's N.2 implementation couples the state mutation and its telemetry emission in the same function (e.g., handle_submit, node-fault handler, scheduling_pass) — never in a separate, skippable step
Detection Code-review checklist item per PR; a lint (grep-based CI check) flags new match arms on JobState/NodeState with no adjacent metric or log call
Telemetry Coverage metric: state_transitions_total vs. state_transitions_logged_total — the two counters must be equal at all times
Recovery If a transition is found missing telemetry, back-fill the metric/log and treat the gap period's incidents as under-diagnosed (documented in postmortem)
Debugging Any incident review that cannot be reconstructed from Prometheus + structured logs alone is itself evidence of an I12 gap; file a follow-up to instrument the missing path

8. Domain Model

8.1 JobState Finite State Machine

                        submit RPC
  (none) ──────────────────────────────► Pending
                                           │
                   scheduling pass alloc   │  cancel (scancel)
                   ◄───────────────────────┤──────────────────► Cancelled
                   │                       │
                   ▼                       │ requeue (node down, preemption)
            Configuring ◄─────────────────-┘
                   │
                   │ noded acks step launch
                   ▼
               Running ──────────────────► Preempted
                   │   (preempt trigger)       │
                   │                           │ suspend/requeue
                   │ last step exits            ▼
                   ▼                        Requeued ──► Pending
             Completing
                   │
                   │ epilog finishes
                   ├──── exit 0 ─────────► Completed
                   └──── exit ≠ 0 ───────► Failed

State-transition table (authoritative; no other transitions are valid per I7):

From To Trigger / Condition WAL Record
— Pending submit RPC accepted + WAL flushed JobSubmitted
Pending Configuring scheduling pass: alloc succeeds, noded notified JobAllocated
Pending Cancelled scancel before scheduling JobCancelled
Configuring Running all allocated nodes' noded ack step-launch JobStarted
Configuring Pending configuring timeout (noded unreachable) → requeue JobRequeued
Running Completing final step exits (all nodes report step done) JobCompleting
Running Preempted higher-priority job preemption decision JobPreempted
Running Requeued node hosting job goes Down and job is requeueable JobRequeued
Running Cancelled scancel while running → noded sends SIGTERM/SIGKILL JobCancelled
Completing Completed epilog exits 0 on all nodes JobCompleted
Completing Failed epilog non-zero or step exit-code non-zero JobFailed
Preempted Requeued preemption grace period expires + checkpoint done JobRequeued
Requeued Pending inserted back into pending queue with preserved priority (state update)

Terminal states: Completed, Failed, Cancelled. No transition out of terminal states.

8.2 NodeState Machine

State Accepts new jobs Existing jobs Transition triggers
Idle Yes None first alloc → Mixed or Allocated
Allocated Yes (if space) Running normally last job finishes → Idle; partial free → Mixed
Mixed Yes (if space) Running normally all slots taken → Allocated; all free → Idle
Drain No (I6) Run to completion admin scontrol update node drain; finish → Down
Draining No Run to completion implicit when Drain+jobs present; all done → Down
Down No (I6) Requeued or Failed heartbeat timeout; admin mark; resume → Idle

8.3 ResourceSpec + CoreBitmap Semantics

ResourceSpec {
  nodes:         u32,          // number of nodes required (1..=max_nodes)
  tasks:         u32,          // total MPI ranks / processes
  cpus_per_task: u16,          // logical CPUs per task
  mem_per_node:  u64,          // bytes
  gpus:          u16,          // GPUs per node (GRES)
  time_limit:    Duration,     // wall-clock; checked at admission
  partition:     PartitionId,
  qos:           QosId,
  account:       AccountId,
}

Derivation rules:

  • cpus_needed_per_node = ceil(tasks / nodes) * cpus_per_task
  • Admission gate: cpus_needed_per_node <= node.cpus, mem_per_node <= node.mem, gpus <= node.gpus
  • CoreBitmap: fixed-width bit vector (max 1024 bits); set-bit = allocated core.
  • GpuSet: u64 bitmask (max 64 GPUs per node); set-bit = allocated GPU.
  • Allocation is transactional: all-or-nothing; partial failure rolls back all node bitmaps (P3, I1).

8.4 Allocation Record Layout

Allocation {
  job_id:  JobId,
  nodes:   Vec<NodeAlloc>,
}

NodeAlloc {
  node_id:  NodeId,
  cores:    CoreBitmap,    // which cores assigned on this node
  gpus:     GpuSet,        // which GPU indices
  mem:      u64,           // bytes reserved on this node
}

Invariants per allocation record:

  • I1: ∀ (n, c) : ¬∃ two live Allocations sharing any bit in c on the same n
  • I2: node.alloc_cores.count() * cpu_frequency + alloc_mem ≤ node capacity
  • I3: job.state == Running ↔ job.alloc.is_some()
  • On job completion: bitmap bits freed before WAL JobCompleted record is appended.

8.5 WAL + Checkpoint Record Taxonomy

WAL record variants (hpcsched-state; serialized as MessagePack framed by u32 length + CRC32):

Variant Key fields When appended
JobSubmitted job_id, user, spec, submit_time before submit RPC ack (P6)
JobAllocated job_id, allocation, start_time after bitmap commit, before launch
JobStarted job_id, start_time after all noded ack step-launch
JobCompleting job_id when final step exits
JobCompleted job_id, end_time, exit_code, tres_usage after epilog exits 0
JobFailed job_id, end_time, exit_code, reason after epilog non-zero or step error
JobCancelled job_id, cancel_time, cancelled_by before SIGTERM dispatch
JobRequeued job_id, reason (NodeDown Preempted
JobPreempted job_id, preempt_mode, preemptor_id on preemption decision
NodeStateChange node_id, old_state, new_state, reason on every node state transition
CheckpointRef checkpoint_file, lsn inlined after checkpoint flush
AccountUsageDecay tree snapshot, decay_epoch periodic fair-share decay event

Checkpoint: full controller state snapshot serialized as bincode; compressed (zstd level 3). WAL is truncated after checkpoint is durable (fsync). Replay = load checkpoint + apply WAL records since CheckpointRef.lsn.

Replay correctness invariant (I5):

  • All bitmap operations are idempotent under re-apply given the snapshot baseline.
  • JobAllocated re-applied: set bits, update node maps. JobCompleted re-applied: clear bits.
  • Duplicate record detection: each WAL record carries a monotone lsn: u64; skip lsn <= checkpoint_lsn.

8.6 Control-Plane Wire Protocol (hpcsched-proto)

Transport: TCP + TLS 1.3; framing: u32 big-endian length prefix + MessagePack body.

Message taxonomy:

Message Direction Key fields Response
SubmitJob CLI → controld spec, script_path, env, stdin JobSubmittedAck(id)
CancelJob CLI → controld job_id, signal Ok / Err
QueryJobs CLI → controld filter (user, state, partition) Vec<JobSummary>
QueryNodes CLI → controld filter (state, partition, features) Vec<NodeSummary>
QueryAcct CLI → controld filter (user, account, time range) Vec<AcctRecord>
RegisterNode noded → controld node_id, cpus, mem, gpus, features, hostname RegisterAck
Heartbeat noded → controld node_id, load, alloc_cores, alloc_mem, alloc_gpus HeartbeatAck
StepStatusUpdate noded → controld job_id, step_id, state, exit_code Ok
LaunchStep controld → noded job_id, step_id, allocation_slice, script, env LaunchAck
KillStep controld → noded job_id, step_id, signal, grace_sec Ok
SuspendStep controld → noded job_id, step_id Ok
ResumeStep controld → noded job_id, step_id Ok

Heartbeat interval: 30 s (default). Timeout threshold: 3 missed heartbeats → NodeStateChange(Down). noded must respond to LaunchStep within 5 s or controld marks launch failed and requeues.

8.7 Scheduling Pass Semantics

Main pass (priority order): main_pass(queue: &PendingQueue, nodes: &NodeMap) -> SchedulePassResult walks queue.eligible_iter() (priority-descending, ineligible jobs already filtered) and for each job attempts alloc::try_allocate(job.spec, nodes). Every successful attempt is accumulated into the result set and its bits are applied tentatively to nodes so later jobs in the same pass see them as used — this tentative-apply step is what prevents double-allocation within a single pass. The result set is what gets committed: a JobAllocated WAL record followed by the real bitmap commit, per accumulated (job, alloc) pair.

Contract invariants:

  • Pass is a deterministic function of (queue snapshot, node snapshot) → P8.
  • Tentative bitmap apply prevents double-allocation within a single pass.
  • Pass bounded at O(pending × nodes); depth-limited via max_sched_iters config → P9.

EASY Backfill semantics: easy_backfill(top_job, queue, nodes, now) first computes a Reservation for the top pending job — the earliest time T at which it fits, reserving those nodes for [T, T+top_job.time_limit]. The backfill candidate set is every lower-priority job whose projected_end(now) is no later than the reservation's start. Candidates are considered in priority order, depth-limited by the backfill_depth config; a candidate is allocated immediately only if it fits within currently-free nodes outside the reservation's node set before the reservation start — I4 is exactly the constraint that no candidate may be allocated in a way that overlaps the reservation window.

Worked example — normal backfill:

now = T0
Nodes: N0(4cpu), N1(4cpu), N2(4cpu)
Queue (priority desc): J1(8cpu, 2h), J2(4cpu, 30m), J3(4cpu, 1h)

main_pass: J1 needs 8 cpu → not enough free (only 12, but J1 needs 2 nodes, possible)
  → J1 alloc: N0+N1; reservation = [T0, T0+2h] on N0+N1.
  J2: needs 4cpu on 1 node; N2 free, projected_end = T0+30m < T0+2h ✓ → backfill J2 on N2 now.
  J3: needs 4cpu; N2 will free at T0+30m; projected_end = T0+30m+1h = T0+1.5h < T0+2h ✓ → schedule.

Failure-path example — backfill violation rejected:

J3 has time_limit = 1h30m; projected_end = T0+2h = reservation.start → NOT < reservation.start.
J3 blocked; top job J1's reservation is not delayed (I4 enforced).

Edge cases:

Case Behavior
Top job never fits (infinite time) Reservation set to now + top_job.time_limit; backfill only below that window
Two jobs tie in priority Tiebreak by job.submit_time ascending; deterministic per P8
Node goes down mid-pass Pass sees pre-snapshot node state; reconciled next cycle; no partial alloc
All nodes drain simultaneously Eligible set = empty; pass produces empty result; no error
Job time_limit = 0 Rejected at admission; not inserted into queue
Job requests 0 nodes Rejected at admission; ResourceSpec validation enforces nodes >= 1
Backfill candidate over limits Filtered by eligible_iter; QoS/association limit checked before backfill attempt

8.8 Multifactor Priority Model

Priority formula (P5, I8):

priority(job) =
  W_age      * age_factor(job)       +
  W_fairshare * fairshare_factor(job) +
  W_partition * partition.priority_tier +
  W_qos      * qos.priority_factor  +
  W_jobsize  * job_size_factor(job)

age_factor(job)        = min(job_age_seconds / age_scale, 1.0)
fairshare_factor(job)  = 2^( -effective_usage(acct) / shares(acct) )
job_size_factor(job)   = 1 - (job.spec.nodes / max_nodes_in_partition)

Fair-share tree traversal:

Association tree (rooted at "root"):
  root  (shares=100)
  ├── dept_A (shares=60)
  │   ├── user_alice (shares=40)
  │   └── user_bob   (shares=20)
  └── dept_B (shares=40)
      └── user_carol (shares=40)

effective_usage(user_alice) = usage_alice + Σ usage_children_alice
                             propagated to parent: dept_A usage includes alice+bob

Decay: usage_decayed(t) = usage_decayed(t-1) * decay_factor + raw_usage(interval). decay_factor default 0.9 per accounting interval. Accounts with zero recent usage approach usage_decayed → 0, boosting their fairshare_factor → 1.

8.9 Preemption Semantics

Preemption modes (ordered by disruption level):

Mode Effect on victim job Job state transition Requires checkpoint
Suspend SIGSTOP to job step processes Running → Preempted No
Checkpoint App-level checkpoint + SIGTERM Running → Preempted Yes
Requeue Kill + reinsert into Pending Running → Requeued No
Cancel SIGTERM/SIGKILL; job lost Running → Cancelled No

Preemption eligibility:

  • victim.partition.preemptible == true
  • victim.qos.priority < preemptor.qos.priority
  • victim.job.priority < preemptor.job.priority (anti-cycle guard)
  • Grace period: SIGTERM → SIGKILL after preempt_grace_sec (default 30 s)

Gang scheduling: time-sliced co-location of jobs on shared nodes; scheduler marks job groups; noded suspends/resumes on tick (default 30 s); configured per partition.

8.10 Accounting + TRES Model

TRES (Trackable Resources): cpu, mem, node, gpu, energy (extensible via plugin registry).

Job accounting record:

AcctRecord {
  job_id:       JobId,
  user:         UserId,
  account:      AccountId,
  alloc_tres:   TresMap,       // TRES allocated (not used, not sampled)
  elapsed_secs: u64,
  tres_seconds: TresMap,       // alloc_tres * elapsed_secs (I10)
  exit_code:    i32,
  node_list:    Vec<NodeId>,
  submit_time:  Epoch,
  start_time:   Epoch,
  end_time:     Epoch,
}

Limit enforcement (admission + scheduling):

Limit type Scope Enforcement point Action on exceed
MaxJobs Association Submit Reject with error
GrpTRES Association Submit + Schedule Reject / hold in queue
MaxTRESPerJob QoS Submit Reject with error
MaxWall QoS Submit Reject with error
GrpWall Association Schedule Hold until under limit

8.11 Durable State Replay Semantics

Sequence for replay after crash:

1. Load latest checkpoint file: deserialize into ControllerState { jobs, nodes, queue, accounts }
2. Open WAL; seek to checkpoint_lsn
3. For each record with lsn > checkpoint_lsn:
   a. JobSubmitted  → insert job into queue (idempotent if already present)
   b. JobAllocated  → apply allocation bitmaps; set job state = Configuring
   c. JobStarted    → set job state = Running
   d. JobCompleted  → free bitmaps; set state = Completed; write AcctRecord
   e. JobFailed     → free bitmaps; set state = Failed
   f. JobCancelled  → free bitmaps (if allocated); set state = Cancelled
   g. JobRequeued   → free bitmaps; set state = Pending; re-insert queue
   h. NodeStateChange → update node state map
4. Reattach to live nodeds: send re-registration challenge; noded responds with current step status
5. Reconcile: any Running job whose noded reports no active step → transition to Completing or Requeued

Post-replay invariant checks (I5, I1, I2, I3): assert no double-allocated bits; assert Running jobs have allocations; assert node alloc sums do not exceed capacity. Fail-fast if any invariant violated (corrupt WAL).

8.12 Heartbeat Timeout + Node Fault Handling

noded heartbeat period: 30 s
missed_heartbeat_threshold: 3 (configurable)
timeout_detection_period: 90 s

on timeout(node_id):
  1. NodeStateChange(node_id, prev_state, Down)        → WAL append
  2. for job in jobs_on_node(node_id):
       if job.spec.requeueable:
         free_bitmaps(job.id); transition Running → Requeued → Pending
       else:
         free_bitmaps(job.id); transition Running → Failed
  3. node removed from eligible set; no new allocations

Drain sequence:

admin issues: drain node_id reason
1. NodeStateChange(node_id, prev, Drain)
2. No new jobs allocated to node (I6)
3. Running jobs complete normally (epilog runs)
4. When last job finishes: NodeStateChange(node_id, Drain, Down)
5. Admin un-drains: NodeStateChange(node_id, Down, Idle) after noded re-registers

9. Tech Stack ADR Table

ADR # Decision Locked Choice Rejected Alternatives Rationale When the Rejected Choice Wins
ADR-01 Implementation language Rust (stable toolchain) C, C++, Go, Java Memory-safe systems language; no GC pauses in scheduling loop; strong type system enforces invariants Go wins for teams with stronger Go expertise and less strict latency-tail requirements (GC pause < 10 ms acceptable); C wins when the codebase must run on embedded or legacy kernels without Rust toolchains; Java wins for organizations with existing JVM HPC tooling and tolerance for heap-tuning
ADR-02 Build system Cargo workspaces Bazel, CMake, Makefile Native Rust tooling; workspace unifies all crates; reproducible via Cargo.lock Bazel wins in large polyglot monorepos (C++/Python/Go alongside Rust) where hermetic cross-language builds and remote caching are required; CMake wins when shipping libraries that must integrate into existing HPC C++ build chains
ADR-03 Dependency policy Minimal curated crates; cargo-deny Unrestricted crates.io; vendored subtrees Supply-chain control; cargo-deny enforces license + advisory policy at CI gate Unrestricted wins during rapid prototyping before security hardening; vendored subtrees win in air-gapped HPC environments with no outbound internet access from build servers
ADR-04 Wire serialization MessagePack (rmp-serde) Protobuf, Cap'n Proto, JSON, bincode Compact binary; schema-free for iterative development; serde integration; human-debuggable with msgpack tools Protobuf wins once the protocol stabilizes and multiple language clients (Python job submitters, C++ monitoring agents) must interoperate with strong schema contracts; Cap'n Proto wins if zero-copy parsing on the hot heartbeat path becomes the dominant bottleneck
ADR-05 WAL + checkpoint format Bincode (checkpoint) + MessagePack (WAL records) SQLite WAL, RocksDB, custom flat-log bincode is zero-copy fast for full snapshots; msgpack for WAL allows record introspection; no external DB dependency SQLite WAL wins when the scheduler must support ad-hoc SQL queries over job history without a separate sacct store (embedded analytics); RocksDB wins at 100k+ concurrent jobs where random-access job-state lookups dominate and append-only log compaction becomes a bottleneck
ADR-06 WAL storage backend Append-only file on local (primary) or shared POSIX FS (HA) etcd, PostgreSQL, Kafka Simplest durable log; shared FS (NFS/GPFS) sufficient for HPC HA; eliminates external service dependency etcd wins when the HA deployment requires distributed consensus across 3+ replicas in geographically separated data centers (NFS-less WAN clusters); Kafka wins when WAL records must fan-out to multiple downstream consumers (audit systems, billing, real-time analytics) at high throughput
ADR-07 Checkpoint compression zstd level 3 lz4, gzip, brotli, none Best ratio/speed tradeoff for large state snapshots; zstd-rs has safe Rust bindings lz4 wins when checkpoint write latency dominates and compression ratio is secondary (NVMe with ample space, latency-critical failover RTO < 5 s); no compression wins on memory-constrained head nodes where decompression CPU cost at startup exceeds I/O savings
ADR-08 Async runtime Tokio (multi-thread scheduler) async-std, smol, blocking threads Mature; broad ecosystem; handles thousands of concurrent noded connections; timer precision for heartbeat smol wins for minimal-footprint embedded controllers where binary size and dependency count matter more than ecosystem breadth; blocking threads win when the scheduling logic must use synchronous system calls that don't have async equivalents in nix crate
ADR-09 RPC / transport Raw TCP + TLS 1.3 (rustls) + hand-rolled framing gRPC/tonic, tarpc, quinn (QUIC) Minimal dependencies; full control over flow; gRPC overhead unnecessary for internal cluster control plane gRPC/tonic wins if external cloud orchestrators or Kubernetes must call the scheduler API directly (standard JSON/proto over HTTP/2); QUIC wins on lossy or high-RTT InfiniBand over-subscription paths where TCP head-of-line blocking causes heartbeat storms
ADR-10 TLS implementation rustls (no unsafe, no openssl dep) OpenSSL via openssl-sys, native-tls Memory-safe; no C FFI in security path; simpler audit surface OpenSSL wins on FIPS-140-2-certified deployments where the compliance body requires an already-approved cryptographic module; native-tls wins on macOS-native deployments needing OS-managed certificates via Keychain
ADR-11 CoreBitmap / GpuSet fixedbitset (or custom const-generic array) Vec<bool>, roaring-bitmap, u128 Fixed upper bound (1024 cores); no heap alloc per allocation; fast set-difference / popcount roaring-bitmap wins when nodes have sparse, non-contiguous core allocations with thousands of cores and utilization < 30% (sparse sets compress 10×); u128 wins for nodes with ≤ 128 cores as a single-word register-width operation
ADR-12 Concurrency model (controller) Single-threaded scheduling loop + Tokio async I/O Fully concurrent shared state, actor model Deterministic scheduling (P8); no races on bitmaps; Tokio handles I/O concurrency outside the loop Actor model (e.g., Actix) wins when scheduling passes are partitioned by cluster region with no cross-region resource contention, enabling true parallelism; fully concurrent shared state wins when scheduling-pass duration is < 1 ms and lock contention never materializes at the target scale
ADR-13 Inter-thread state sharing Arc<RwLock<ControllerState>> read by I/O tasks; scheduling loop holds exclusive write lock during pass Lockless MPSC, channels only Simple correctness model; scheduling pass duration is bounded (P9); no starvation from brief lock hold Lockless MPSC channel-only wins when the scheduling pass exceeds 200 ms and RPC handlers need to drain large query results without waiting for the lock; sharded per-partition locks win at >100k pending jobs where different partitions never share nodes
ADR-14 Logging tracing crate (structured, leveled) log crate, slog, env_logger tracing integrates with Tokio spans; structured fields queryable by log aggregators slog wins in deployments where log aggregators are incompatible with tracing's subscriber model and a custom drain chain is simpler; log crate wins in no_std embedded contexts where hpcsched-core must be compiled without the tracing machinery
ADR-15 Metrics metrics crate + metrics-exporter-prometheus OpenTelemetry, statsd, InfluxDB client Prometheus is HPC cluster standard; pull model; no external agent needed on head node OpenTelemetry wins when the site already uses OTLP-native backends (Grafana Cloud, Datadog) and wants a single telemetry SDK for traces + metrics; statsd wins in HPC sites with legacy Graphite infrastructure predating Prometheus adoption
ADR-16 Distributed tracing tracing-opentelemetry → OTLP exporter Jaeger native, Zipkin, manual spans Single instrumentation point; vendor-neutral; works with Grafana Tempo in HPC setups Jaeger native wins if the site uses Jaeger exclusively and wants to avoid the OTLP translation layer's additional hop; manual spans win in ultra-low-overhead deployments where the tracing SDK overhead on the hot scheduling path is measurable
ADR-17 Configuration format TOML (serde + toml crate) YAML, JSON, INI, Slurm .conf syntax Human-editable; strongly typed via serde; unambiguous (no YAML parsing pitfalls) YAML wins when configuration is generated programmatically by Ansible/Helm and operators prefer the ecosystem tooling (yq, jsonschema for validation); Slurm .conf syntax wins in migration deployments where existing site configurations must be imported without conversion
ADR-18 CLI argument parsing clap v4 (derive macros) structopt (deprecated), argh, pico-args Standard Rust CLI; rich help generation; consistent across all hpcsched-cli binaries pico-args wins for single-purpose auxiliary binaries (the WAL dump tool, state-inspect) where binary size and compile time matter more than help-text quality; argh wins for simpler command surfaces that don't need subcommand nesting
ADR-19 Unit + integration testing cargo test + in-process controller harness pytest + subprocess, Go testing In-process harness allows deterministic scheduling-pass tests without networking; fast CI pytest + subprocess wins when non-Rust test engineers own the conformance suite and must write black-box tests against the binary API without understanding Rust internals; Go testing wins in polyglot shops where the test infrastructure is already Go
ADR-20 Property-based testing proptest quickcheck, arbitrary Structured generators for ResourceSpec / queue states; shrinking on failure; derives Arbitrary quickcheck wins when tests require a simpler API surface and shrinking is not critical (tests are fast enough to re-run with smaller seeds manually); arbitrary wins when fuzzing and property tests share the same generator (unified corpus for cargo-fuzz)
ADR-21 Protocol fuzzing cargo-fuzz (libFuzzer) AFL++, honggfuzz Native Rust integration; persistent mode; covers wire deserialization (msgpack decode paths) AFL++ wins when the fuzzer must instrument C code in hybrid Rust+C bindings (e.g., if libcgroup C FFI is used in noded) for full coverage; honggfuzz wins for network-traffic-aware fuzzing (structure-aware mutation of framed TCP streams)
ADR-22 Benchmark harness criterion.rs iai-callgrind, divan, custom bench binary Statistical rigor; HTML reports; regression detection for scheduling-pass latency (P9) iai-callgrind wins when deterministic instruction-count benchmarks are needed in CI (wall-time noise on shared runners exceeds 10%; iai removes timing variance entirely); divan wins for micro-benchmarks requiring less boilerplate with simpler reporting needs
ADR-23 CI pipeline GitHub Actions (or GitLab CI) Jenkins, CircleCI, Buildkite Declarative YAML; matrix builds (stable + beta Rust); cargo-deny + cargo clippy + test gate Jenkins wins in air-gapped on-premise HPC sites with no GitHub/GitLab access and existing Jenkins infrastructure; Buildkite wins for large-scale parallel CI with agent pooling on on-premise hardware (e.g., running the 24-hour soak on real cluster hardware)
ADR-24 Code coverage cargo-llvm-cov grcov, tarpaulin LLVM-native; accurate line + branch coverage; integrates with CI artifact upload grcov wins when LLVM profdata files are difficult to collect in cross-compilation environments (aarch64 cross-build on amd64 CI); tarpaulin wins when the project avoids LLVM toolchain entirely and needs a pure-Rust coverage tool
ADR-25 Conformance test fixtures JSON fixture files + custom test runner Cucumber, robot framework Lightweight; fixtures encode Slurm semantics (backfill, fairshare, preemption) as input/expected-output pairs Cucumber wins when non-technical HPC site administrators must author or validate conformance tests using natural-language Gherkin steps without writing Rust; robot framework wins for integration with existing HPC site test infrastructure that uses robot
ADR-26 Container packaging Distroless Debian base + multi-stage Dockerfile Alpine, Ubuntu full, Nix flake Small attack surface; multi-stage separates build from runtime; no shell in prod container Alpine wins when binary size and minimal layer count are critical for frequent container pulls over slow management network; Nix flake wins when strict reproducibility of the entire build closure (including glibc version) is required for compliance audits
ADR-27 Reproducible builds Cargo.lock committed; pinned toolchain via rust-toolchain.toml Nix, Guix Sufficient for Rust binary reproducibility; rust-toolchain.toml locks exact channel + version Nix wins when the entire build environment (OS, compiler, libc, system deps) must be bit-for-bit reproducible across machines for SLSA Level 3+ provenance; Guix wins in GNU-stack HPC sites already using Guix as the package manager
ADR-28 Licensing Apache-2.0 OR MIT (dual) GPL-2.0, BSL, proprietary Standard open-source HPC tooling expectation; dual license maximizes compatibility GPL-2.0 wins when the project must guarantee that all derivative work remains open source and the community prioritizes copyleft over commercial adoption; BSL wins for commercial deployments where a time-delayed open-source release model funds ongoing development
ADR-29 Cgroup resource enforcement (noded) cgroups v2 via nix crate + direct sysfs writes libcgroup C bindings, systemd transient units Direct kernel interface; no C lib dependency; Rust nix crate covers Linux syscall surface libcgroup C bindings win on kernels < 4.5 where cgroup v2 is incomplete and v1 subsystem paths differ from v2 (RHEL 7 compatibility); systemd transient units win when the site's security policy mandates that all cgroup management go through systemd's D-Bus API to maintain audit trails
ADR-30 Shared durable store (HA) POSIX shared filesystem path (NFS/GPFS/Lustre) etcd, PostgreSQL, S3 HPC clusters universally have shared FS; no additional service to deploy; fsync guarantees suffice for WAL durability etcd wins for cloud-native deployments where no shared POSIX FS exists and the team is comfortable operating a 3-node etcd cluster; S3 wins for checkpoint archival in cloud HPC (AWS, Azure) where durable object storage is cheaper than EFS/NFS; PostgreSQL wins when the scheduler must support complex accounting queries that exceed what sacct's linear scan can handle

11. Core Schema and Core Types

All types in this section are normative. Implementations in later phases must use exactly these names, field names, and variant names. Signatures follow Rule 10: struct/enum headers, field types, and trait declarations only — no function bodies.

11.1 hpcsched-core

Identity types

// job.rs
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord, Hash, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct JobId(pub u64);

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct StepId { pub job: JobId, pub step: u32 }

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct NodeId(pub u32);
Type Ownership Mutation Concurrency Persistence Compat
JobId Copy immutable after creation send/sync freely WAL field, stable u64 monotone; never reused
StepId Copy immutable send/sync WAL field composite key
NodeId Copy immutable send/sync config + WAL stable across restarts

JobState FSM

// job.rs
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub enum JobState {
    Pending, Configuring, Running, Completing,
    Completed, Failed, Cancelled, Preempted, Requeued,
}

State transition table (I7 — no transition skips Completing/cleanup):

From To Trigger
Pending Configuring allocate + LaunchMsg sent
Configuring Running first noded step-started report
Running Completing exit detected or cancel/preempt issued
Completing Completed epilog finished, exit 0
Completing Failed epilog finished, exit != 0
Running Preempted preemption granted
Preempted Requeued requeue policy active
Requeued Pending re-inserted into queue
Pending Cancelled scancel before allocation
Running Cancelled scancel after allocation, completing path
Running Failed node Down event, non-requeueable

Terminal states: Completed, Failed, Cancelled. No outbound transitions.

Job

// job.rs
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct Job {
    pub id:          JobId,
    pub user:        String,
    pub account:     String,
    pub spec:        ResourceSpec,
    pub state:       JobState,
    pub priority:    u32,
    pub submit_time: SystemTime,
    pub start_time:  Option<SystemTime>,
    pub end_time:    Option<SystemTime>,
    pub alloc:       Option<Allocation>,
    pub exit_code:   Option<i32>,
    pub requeue:     bool,
    pub array_id:    Option<JobId>,   // non-None for array element jobs
}
Aspect Semantics
Ownership heap-allocated; owned by ControllerState under RwLock
Mutation via explicit state-transition methods; never mutate fields directly across module boundary
Concurrency ControllerState holds Arc<RwLock<HashMap<JobId, Job>>>; write lock held only during transition + WAL append
Persistence serialized as WAL record payload (bincode); full snapshot in Checkpoint
Compat additive fields only; unknown fields ignored on deserialize with #[serde(default)]
Invariant I3: alloc.is_some() iff state == Running or Configuring

ResourceSpec

// resource.rs
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct ResourceSpec {
    pub nodes:         u32,            // number of nodes requested
    pub tasks:         u32,            // total MPI tasks / processes
    pub cpus_per_task: u16,
    pub mem_per_node:  u64,            // bytes
    pub gpus:          u16,            // GPUs per node
    pub time_limit:    Duration,       // wall-clock limit; enforced by controld timer
    pub partition:     String,
    pub qos:           Option<String>,
    pub account:       Option<String>,
    pub features:      Vec<String>,    // node feature constraints (AND-list)
    pub exclusive:     bool,           // whole-node allocation
}
Aspect Semantics
Ownership value type cloned into Job; not shared
Mutation immutable after job submission
Persistence embedded in Job WAL record
Validation cpus_per_task * tasks <= nodes * node_cpus checked at submit
TRES dimensions nodes, cpus (tasks * cpus_per_task), mem (mem_per_node * nodes), gpus (gpus * nodes)

Node and NodeState

// node.rs
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct Node {
    pub id:          NodeId,
    pub hostname:    String,
    pub cpus:        u16,
    pub mem:         u64,            // bytes total
    pub gpus:        u16,
    pub features:    Vec<String>,
    pub state:       NodeState,
    pub alloc_cores: CoreBitmap,
    pub alloc_mem:   u64,
    pub alloc_gpus:  GpuSet,
    pub last_seen:   SystemTime,
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub enum NodeState { Idle, Allocated, Mixed, Down, Drain, Draining }
NodeState alloc_cores accepts new jobs existing jobs
Idle all clear yes —
Allocated all set no running
Mixed partial yes (remaining) running
Down any no requeued (I6)
Drain any no allowed to finish
Draining any no finishing
Aspect Semantics
Ownership owned by ControllerState node map; noded holds a local copy updated by heartbeat
Mutation alloc_cores/alloc_gpus/alloc_mem mutated only inside hpcsched-alloc transaction
Concurrency mutation under ControllerState write lock; heartbeat updates via write lock
Persistence snapshot in Checkpoint; reconstructed from WAL allocation records
Invariant I2: alloc_cores.count() * cpus_per_core + alloc_mem + alloc_gpus never exceeds capacity

CoreBitmap and GpuSet

// allocation.rs
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct CoreBitmap(pub u128);   // up to 128 cores per node; bit i = core i allocated

#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct GpuSet(pub u64);        // up to 64 GPUs per node; bit i = GPU i allocated

impl CoreBitmap {
    pub fn allocate(&mut self, count: u16) -> Result<CoreBitmap, AllocError>;
    pub fn free(&mut self, mask: CoreBitmap);
    pub fn free_count(&self) -> u16;
    pub fn intersects(&self, other: &CoreBitmap) -> bool;
}
impl GpuSet {
    pub fn allocate(&mut self, count: u16) -> Result<GpuSet, AllocError>;
    pub fn free(&mut self, mask: GpuSet);
}
Aspect Semantics
Ownership value type; embedded in Node and Allocation
Mutation atomic bit-manipulation; free() only after job leaves Running/Completing
Concurrency always mutated under ControllerState write lock; no interior mutability
Persistence serialized as u128/u64; stable layout
Invariant I1: for all nodes, Node.alloc_cores & Allocation.cores == Allocation.cores (bits not double-set)
Limit 128 cores per node; extend to Vec<u64> only if needed (ADR-11)

Partition

// partition.rs
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct Partition {
    pub name:           String,
    pub node_set:       Vec<NodeId>,
    pub max_time:       Duration,
    pub max_nodes:      u32,
    pub priority_tier:  u8,           // additive to multifactor score
    pub preemptible:    bool,
    pub default:        bool,
    pub allow_accounts: Option<Vec<String>>,
}
Aspect Semantics
Mutation config-driven; updated via admin RPC only; immutable during scheduling pass
Persistence config file + Checkpoint
Validation node_set elements must exist in node inventory at load

Allocation and Reservation

// allocation.rs
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct Allocation {
    pub job_id: JobId,
    pub nodes:  Vec<NodeAllocation>,
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct NodeAllocation {
    pub node_id: NodeId,
    pub cores:   CoreBitmap,
    pub gpus:    GpuSet,
    pub mem:     u64,
}

// reservation.rs
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct Reservation {
    pub job_id:    JobId,             // top pending job this reserves for
    pub start:     SystemTime,        // earliest the top job can start
    pub node_set:  Vec<NodeId>,       // nodes reserved
    pub window:    Duration,          // duration held
}
Aspect Semantics
Allocation ownership owned by Job.alloc; NodeAllocation.cores also mirrored in Node.alloc_cores
Reservation lifetime single scheduling pass; recomputed each EASY backfill pass (I4)
Persistence Allocation serialized in WAL JobAllocated record; Reservation is transient
Invariant I3: Job.alloc.is_none() when Pending/Completed/Failed/Cancelled

Account and Association

// account.rs
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct Account {
    pub name:          String,
    pub parent:        Option<String>,
    pub shares:        u32,           // relative share weight
    pub usage_decayed: f64,           // TRES-seconds decayed
    pub children:      Vec<String>,   // child account names
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct Association {
    pub user:          String,
    pub account:       String,
    pub qos:           Vec<String>,
    pub max_jobs:      Option<u32>,
    pub max_tres:      Option<TresLimits>,
    pub grp_tres:      Option<TresLimits>,
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct TresLimits {
    pub cpus:  Option<u64>,
    pub mem:   Option<u64>,     // bytes
    pub gpus:  Option<u32>,
    pub nodes: Option<u32>,
}
Aspect Semantics
Account mutation usage_decayed updated each fair-share recompute interval (Phase 8)
Association lookup (user, account) pair → Association; enforced at submit + schedule
Persistence stored in hpcsched-acct persistent store; snapshotted in Checkpoint

PriorityFactors

// priority.rs
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct PriorityFactors {
    pub age:       f64,        // clamped [0,1]: age / age_max
    pub fairshare: f64,        // [0,1]: from fair-share tree
    pub partition: f64,        // partition priority_tier / max_tier
    pub qos:       f64,        // QoS priority factor
    pub job_size:  f64,        // log-normalized requested TRES
    pub weights:   PriorityWeights,
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct PriorityWeights {
    pub age:       u32,
    pub fairshare: u32,
    pub partition: u32,
    pub qos:       u32,
    pub job_size:  u32,
}

// priority = sum(factor_i * weight_i) normalized to u32; I8
Aspect Semantics
Mutation recomputed each scheduling pass; old value replaced atomically
Determinism P8, I8: identical inputs → identical u32 output; weights from config
Persistence weights in config file; computed value not persisted (recomputed on replay)

SchedulePass and BackfillPlan

// hpcsched-sched (uses core types)
pub struct SchedulePass {
    pub started:    Vec<JobId>,        // jobs transitioned Pending → Configuring
    pub blocked:    Vec<(JobId, BlockReason)>,
    pub pass_time:  Duration,
}

pub struct BackfillPlan {
    pub reservation:   Reservation,
    pub backfilled:    Vec<(JobId, SystemTime, Allocation)>,  // (job, projected_start, alloc)
}

pub enum BlockReason {
    InsufficientNodes, TimeLimitExceeds, QosLimit, AssocLimit, PartitionLimit, NoFit,
}
Aspect Semantics
SchedulePass lifetime ephemeral; created + consumed each scheduling tick
BackfillPlan invariant I4: all backfilled[i].0 projected end <= reservation.start
Depth limit backfill scans at most BACKFILL_DEPTH (config, default 500) candidates (P9)

11.2 hpcsched-proto

// messages.rs — ControlMsg discriminants (no method bodies)
#[derive(Debug, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub enum ControlMsg {
    Submit(SubmitReq),      SubmitAck(SubmitAck),
    Cancel(CancelReq),      CancelAck(CancelAck),
    StatusReq(StatusReq),   StatusResp(StatusResp),
    LaunchStep(LaunchMsg),  LaunchAck(LaunchAck),
    StepEvent(StepEventMsg), NodeRegister(NodeRegisterMsg),
    Heartbeat(HeartbeatMsg), HeartbeatAck,
    DrainReq(DrainReq),
    QueueQuery(QueueQueryReq), QueueResp(QueueResp),
}

Payload structs for the key messages:

#[derive(Debug, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct SubmitReq   { pub script: String, pub spec: ResourceSpec, pub client_token: u64 }
#[derive(Debug, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct SubmitAck   { pub job_id: JobId, pub client_token: u64 }
#[derive(Debug, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct LaunchMsg   { pub job_id: JobId, pub step_id: StepId,
                         pub alloc: Allocation, pub script: String }
#[derive(Debug, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct HeartbeatMsg {
    pub node_id: NodeId, pub free_cores: CoreBitmap,
    pub free_mem: u64,   pub free_gpus: GpuSet,
    pub running_steps: Vec<StepId>, pub timestamp: SystemTime,
}
#[derive(Debug, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct StepEventMsg {
    pub step_id: StepId, pub event: StepEvent,
    pub node_id: NodeId, pub exit_code: Option<i32>,
}
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub enum StepEvent { Started, Completed, Failed }
#[derive(Debug, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct NodeRegisterMsg {
    pub node_id: NodeId,  pub hostname: String,
    pub cpus: u16,        pub mem: u64,
    pub gpus: u16,        pub features: Vec<String>,
}
Aspect Semantics
Encoding MessagePack (rmp-serde) with length-prefix u32 framing (ADR-04)
Versioning message enum; unknown variants return ProtocolError::UnknownVariant
client_token idempotency key: controld deduplicates by (user, client_token) for SubmitReq
Heartbeat interval configurable; default 10 s; missed 3 → NodeDown (Phase 10)
Transport TCP; TLS optional via rustls feature flag

11.3 hpcsched-state

// record.rs
#[derive(Debug, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub enum WalRecord {
    JobSubmitted  { job: Job },
    JobAllocated  { job_id: JobId, alloc: Allocation },
    JobState      { job_id: JobId, state: JobState, time: SystemTime },
    NodeState     { node_id: NodeId, state: NodeState, time: SystemTime },
    CheckpointRef { offset: u64 },       // WAL pointer to latest checkpoint
}

// wal.rs
pub struct StateLog { /* path, fd, crc_state */ }
impl StateLog {
    pub fn append(&mut self, record: &WalRecord) -> Result<u64, StateError>; // returns offset; fdatasync before return (P6)
    pub fn replay(&self, from: u64) -> impl Iterator<Item = Result<WalRecord, StateError>>;
}

// checkpoint.rs
pub struct Checkpoint { /* serialized ControllerState snapshot */ }
impl Checkpoint {
    pub fn write_atomic(path: &Path, state: &ControllerState) -> Result<(), StateError>; // write tmp, rename
    pub fn read(path: &Path) -> Result<Checkpoint, StateError>;
}
Aspect Semantics
WAL append atomicity write record + CRC, fdatasync, then return; I5, P6
Checkpoint atomicity write to .tmp, fsync, rename; never a partial snapshot visible
Replay algorithm load latest Checkpoint, apply all WAL records with offset > CheckpointRef.offset
HA mode checkpoint written to shared durable path (NFS/S3); standby tails WAL (Phase 12)
Compat WalRecord enum: additive variants only; unknown variants skipped with warning

11.4 hpcsched-acct

// record.rs
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct JobAcctRecord {
    pub job_id:      JobId,
    pub user:        String,
    pub account:     String,
    pub partition:   String,
    pub submit_time: SystemTime,
    pub start_time:  SystemTime,
    pub end_time:    SystemTime,
    pub state:       JobState,
    pub exit_code:   i32,
    pub tres:        TresUsage,
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct TresUsage {
    pub cpu_seconds:  u64,
    pub mem_byte_sec: u64,   // mem_per_node * nodes * wall_seconds
    pub gpu_seconds:  u64,
    pub node_seconds: u64,
}
Aspect Semantics
Invariant I10: cpu_seconds = cpus_per_task * tasks * wall_seconds ± 1 s rounding
Mutation written once at job terminal state; append-only store
Usage feed JobAcctRecord.tres fed into Account.usage_decayed for fair-share (Phase 8)
Query sacct filters by user/account/time range/state; O(N) scan acceptable for Phase 13

11.5 Key traits (hpcsched-core)

pub trait Schedulable {
    fn job_id(&self) -> JobId;
    fn spec(&self) -> &ResourceSpec;
    fn priority(&self) -> u32;
    fn submit_time(&self) -> SystemTime;
}

pub trait ResourceAllocator {
    fn try_allocate(&mut self, spec: &ResourceSpec, nodes: &[Node]) -> Result<Allocation, AllocError>;
    fn free(&mut self, alloc: &Allocation);
    fn fits(&self, spec: &ResourceSpec, node: &Node) -> bool;
}

pub trait PriorityModel {
    fn compute(&self, job: &Job, tree: &Account) -> PriorityFactors;
    fn score(&self, factors: &PriorityFactors) -> u32;
}

pub trait DurableLog {
    fn append(&mut self, record: &WalRecord) -> Result<u64, StateError>;
    fn replay(&self) -> Box<dyn Iterator<Item = Result<WalRecord, StateError>>>;
}
Trait Implementors Purpose
Schedulable Job abstraction for scheduling pass; testable with mock types
ResourceAllocator hpcsched-alloc::Allocator swap real/mock allocator in tests (P8)
PriorityModel hpcsched-fairshare::MultifactorModel injectable for determinism tests
DurableLog hpcsched-state::StateLog, in-memory mock WAL abstraction; mock used in unit tests

11.6 Error taxonomy

// hpcsched-core::error
#[derive(Debug, thiserror::Error)]
pub enum CoreError {
    #[error("invalid job state transition {from:?} -> {to:?}")] InvalidTransition { from: JobState, to: JobState },
    #[error("resource spec invalid: {0}")] InvalidSpec(String),
}

// hpcsched-alloc
#[derive(Debug, thiserror::Error)]
pub enum AllocError { InsufficientCores, InsufficientMem, InsufficientGpus, NoEligibleNode, FeatureMismatch }

// hpcsched-state
#[derive(Debug, thiserror::Error)]
pub enum StateError { Io(#[from] std::io::Error), Corrupt { offset: u64 }, Crc { expected: u32, got: u32 } }

// hpcsched-proto
#[derive(Debug, thiserror::Error)]
pub enum ProtoError { Encode(#[from] bincode::Error), Decode(#[from] bincode::Error), UnknownVariant, Io(#[from] std::io::Error) }
Rule Detail
No panic in production paths all error paths return Result; no .unwrap()/.expect() outside tests (clippy.toml enforces)
Error context use anyhow::Context in daemon crates; typed errors in library crates
WAL corrupt StateError::Corrupt at offset N → replay stops, operator alert, do not silently skip

Phase 1 — Core model + controller skeleton

1.1 Objectives

  • Define all canonical types in hpcsched-core: Job, JobId, JobState, ResourceSpec, Node, NodeId, NodeState, Partition, CoreBitmap, GpuSet, Allocation.
  • Implement JobState FSM with validated transitions; panic on invalid transition in debug builds.
  • Build in-memory controller state registry: node map, job map, partition map.
  • Stand up event loop skeleton in hpcsched-controld: tokio runtime, single scheduling-loop tick interval, graceful shutdown on SIGTERM/SIGINT.
  • Parse TOML cluster config (nodes, partitions, resource capacities, limits); validate at startup.
  • Constraints: no networking, no persistence, no scheduling logic yet; only model correctness.

1.2 Detailed Implementation Steps
