hpcsched-core/src/job.rs — applies P1 (R1: slurmctld/job_mgr.c), I7 (ADR-12 deterministic FSM)

  • JobId(u64) newtype; StepId(u64) newtype; Display, Hash, Eq, Ord derives.
  • JobState enum with transition(next: JobState) -> Result<(), FsmError> signature; encode valid edges as a const lookup table. [I7; P1 architecture; ADR-12]
  • Job struct: id, user: Arc<str>, account: Arc<str>, spec: ResourceSpec, state: JobState, priority: u32, submit_time: Instant, start_time: Option<Instant>, end_time: Option<Instant>, alloc: Option<Allocation>.
  • ResourceSpec: all fields as per spine; time_limit: Duration; validate() -> Result<(), SpecError> — rejects zero-node, negative mem, missing partition. [P1; S1 (JSDL resource-spec lineage)]

hpcsched-core/src/node.rs

  • CoreBitmap: [u64; N] backed fixed-width bitset; allocate(count) -> Option<CoreBitmap> (first-fit within bitmap), free(bits), available() -> u32, intersects(other) -> bool.
  • GpuSet: same pattern as CoreBitmap but narrower width (max 64 GPUs per node).
  • Node: fields per spine; available_cores() -> u32, available_mem() -> u64, available_gpus() -> u32.
  • NodeState enum; is_schedulable() -> bool (true only for Idle/Mixed/Allocated when capacity remains).

hpcsched-core/src/partition.rs

  • Partition: fields per spine; contains_node(id: NodeId) -> bool; enforces_time_limit(d: Duration) -> bool.

hpcsched-controld/src/state.rs

  • ControllerState: HashMap<NodeId, Node>, HashMap<JobId, Job>, HashMap<String, Partition>, AtomicU64 id counter.
  • new_job_id() — fetch-and-increment; monotonically increasing, never reused.
  • All fields behind a single tokio::sync::RwLock<ControllerState> (read-heavy workload assumption).

hpcsched-controld/src/config.rs

  • ClusterConfig: serde-deserialized TOML; node stanzas expand to Node records with bitmaps sized to declared cpus/gpus.
  • Validation: partition references only declared nodes; max_time not zero; no duplicate node IDs.

hpcsched-controld/src/main.rs

  • Tokio multi-thread runtime; periodic scheduling tick via tokio::time::interval; shutdown_signal() watches SIGTERM + SIGINT; graceful drain on signal.
  • Event loop: acquire write lock, call scheduling_loop_tick() (stub returns Ok(())), release; log tick latency.

1.3 Deliverable

  • cargo build --workspace succeeds with zero warnings (deny(warnings) enforced).
  • In-memory state initialized from TOML config; node list and partition list queryable via unit-test helpers.
  • Controller process starts, logs tick heartbeats, shuts down cleanly on signal.
  • JobState FSM table covered by unit tests.

1.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
cargo test -p hpcsched-core all tests pass
FSM invalid-transition test returns Err(FsmError::InvalidTransition)
Config parse of 128-node fixture < 10 ms cold
CoreBitmap allocate/free round-trip zero bits leaked (property test, 10k iterations)
Controller start → tick × 10 → SIGTERM clean exit, no panics
Zero clippy warnings (-D warnings) enforced in CI

1.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected output
fixtures/config/cluster_8node_2part.toml baseline cluster config 8 nodes, 2 partitions parsed
fixtures/config/bad_partition_ref.toml unknown node in partition ConfigError::UnknownNode
fixtures/config/zero_time_limit.toml max_time = 0 ConfigError::ZeroTimeLimit
fixtures/fsm/valid_transitions.json all legal FSM edges all Ok
fixtures/fsm/invalid_transitions.json illegal FSM edges all Err(InvalidTransition)
fixtures/bitmap/alloc_free_roundtrip proptest seed corpus zero bits leaked

1.6 Pitfalls

  • Bitmap width mismatch: node declared with 192 cpus but CoreBitmap backed by [u64; 3] — allocation silently truncates. Fix: derive bitmap array size from node cpu count at construction; assert at config-parse time.
  • FSM table as runtime HashMap: initializing a HashMap<(JobState,JobState), bool> on every transition check causes allocation pressure. Fix: encode as const 2D array indexed by discriminant.
  • RwLock starvation under writer contention: scheduling tick holds write lock; many readers (CLI queries) queue behind it. Fix: snapshot state for scheduling inside the lock, do computation outside; use try_read() with backoff for CLI paths.
  • new_job_id reuse across restart: if counter resets to 0 on restart, old WAL records collide. Fix: persist the high-water mark in checkpoint; Phase 3 WAL replay must restore counter.
  • Config hot-reload races: reloading config mid-tick with a new partition set creates transient inconsistency. Fix: config reload is not supported live; document that restart is required.

1.7 Performance Concerns

  • RwLock<ControllerState> is a single global lock; acceptable at Phase 1 but shapes future sharding. Profile lock hold time each tick; instrument with tracing::Span.
  • CoreBitmap first-fit scan is O(width); for 256-core nodes with [u64; 4], branch-free trailing_zeros loops are fastest.
  • HashMap lookups for job/node by ID: pre-size at startup from config node count to avoid rehash during soak.
  • Config TOML parse runs only at startup; serde derive is sufficient; no streaming parser needed.

1.8 Edge Cases

  • Node declared with 0 cpus → validation error, not a later arithmetic underflow.
  • Partition with empty node set → allowed at parse (nodes may register later); scheduling simply finds no candidates.
  • JobId counter at u64::MAX → saturating behavior must be detected and controller must refuse new submissions with an explicit error rather than wrapping to 0.
  • Duration zero time_limit on ResourceSpec → validate() returns Err; zero is nonsensical and would break backfill reservation math.
  • Clock skew between nodes and controller → submit_time uses controller wall clock exclusively; node clocks are not trusted for priority.

1.9 Failure Analysis

  • Config parse failure at startup: controller exits with non-zero status and structured error to stderr; no partial state left.
  • Panic in FSM transition (debug build): test-caught; in production builds transitions return Result; unwrap-in-production is a bug, not an operator action.
  • Tick interval drift: if scheduling tick overshoots its interval under load, tokio's MissedTickBehavior::Delay prevents cascade; log warns if tick exceeds 2× interval.
  • SIGKILL with no WAL (Phase 1 only): state is fully lost; expected and documented; Phase 3 adds durability.

1.10 Operational Notes

  • Log level: RUST_LOG=hpcsched_controld=info; tick duration logged at debug.
  • Metrics stub: tick_duration_us counter exported via in-process registry (wired to Prometheus exporter in Phase 13).
  • Config file path: --config <path> CLI flag; defaults to /etc/hpcsched/cluster.toml.
  • No migration concerns at Phase 1; state is ephemeral.
  • Health endpoint stub: TCP port configurable; responds 200 OK after successful config load.

Phase 2 — Control-plane RPC + node daemon

2.1 Objectives

  • Define all Phase 2 wire messages in hpcsched-proto: RegisterMsg, HeartbeatMsg, NodeStatusReport, AckMsg.
  • Implement hpcsched-noded: registers with controller on startup, sends periodic heartbeats, reports live cpu/mem/gpu/features.
  • Implement controller-side RPC handlers: handle_register, handle_heartbeat; update ControllerState node inventory.
  • Controller detects node failure via heartbeat timeout; transitions node to NodeState::Down.
  • No job launch yet; no submission yet; only liveness and inventory.
  • Constraints: use tonic (gRPC over HTTP/2) for transport; TLS optional (flag-gated); proto schema versioned.

2.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

hpcsched-proto/proto/control.proto — applies P1 (R1: wire protocol from slurmctld/slurmd split), ADR-04 (MessagePack framing), ADR-09 (TCP+TLS transport)

  • Service ControlPlane: Register(RegisterRequest) -> RegisterResponse; Heartbeat(HeartbeatRequest) -> HeartbeatResponse. [P1; ADR-09; S6]
  • RegisterRequest: node_id, hostname, cpus, mem_bytes, gpu_count, features (repeated string), daemon_version.
  • HeartbeatRequest: node_id, timestamp_unix_ms, alloc_cores_bitmap (bytes), alloc_mem_bytes, alloc_gpu_mask (u64), load_1m (f32).
  • Build via tonic-build in build.rs; generated code in hpcsched-proto/src/generated/.

hpcsched-noded/src/main.rs

  • Startup: parse --controller-addr, --node-id, --cpus, --mem, --gpus, --features flags; build RegisterRequest.
  • register_loop(): connect to controller gRPC endpoint with exponential backoff (initial 1s, max 30s, jitter); call Register; log success with assigned controller-side version stamp.
  • heartbeat_task(): tokio task; interval from config (default 10s); reads live /proc/loadavg; reads local allocation bitmaps from shared state; sends HeartbeatRequest; on transport error, reconnect via register_loop.
  • Local state: Arc<Mutex<NodeLocalState>> holding current alloc bitmaps; updated by job-launch logic in Phase 6.

hpcsched-controld/src/rpc/register.rs

  • handle_register(req): validate node_id unique or re-registration (same node restarted); upsert into ControllerState.nodes; set NodeState::Idle; record last_seen = Instant::now(); return RegisterResponse { ack: true, controller_epoch: u64 }.
  • Re-registration of a known node while jobs are allocated: mark node Configuring (jobs may have survived); reconciliation deferred to Phase 10.

hpcsched-controld/src/rpc/heartbeat.rs

  • handle_heartbeat(req): update last_seen; update alloc_cores_bitmap, alloc_mem, alloc_gpu_mask on the node record (controller trusts these as advisory; authoritative bitmap lives in controller state per P2); detect divergence (node reports less allocated than controller expects) and log warning.
  • Heartbeat timeout watcher: separate tokio task; scans all nodes every heartbeat_interval / 2; any node with last_seen > 2 × heartbeat_interval → transition to NodeState::Down; emit NodeDown { node_id, last_seen } event to internal event bus.

hpcsched-controld/src/events.rs

  • Internal EventBus: tokio::sync::broadcast channel; subscribers: scheduling loop, fault handler (Phase 10), metrics sink.
  • Events at this phase: NodeUp { node_id }, NodeDown { node_id }.

2.3 Deliverable

  • hpcsched-noded binary registers with hpcsched-controld; controller logs the node in inventory.
  • Heartbeat flow running at configurable interval; controller inventory reflects live node count.
  • Kill a noded process → controller logs NodeDown within 2 × heartbeat_interval.
  • Restart noded → controller logs NodeUp and re-registers (idempotent).
  • sinfo analog (stub in hpcsched-cli) prints node list with state.

2.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
Register 128 simulated nodes (integration test) all visible in controller inventory < 5s
Heartbeat round-trip latency (p99) < 5 ms on loopback
Node timeout detection < 3 × heartbeat_interval after last packet
Re-registration idempotency second Register from same node_id does not duplicate entry
Disconnect + reconnect cycle × 100 no memory leak (valgrind / heaptrack baseline)
Proto schema version check mismatched version returns FAILED_PRECONDITION

2.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected output
fixtures/proto/register_valid.json well-formed RegisterRequest node appears in inventory
fixtures/proto/register_duplicate_id.json second register same id idempotent upsert, no error
fixtures/proto/heartbeat_stale.json timestamp 10× interval in past controller logs clock-skew warning
fixtures/proto/heartbeat_timeout_sim simulate 3 missed heartbeats node transitions to Down
fixtures/proto/bad_version.json daemon_version mismatch FAILED_PRECONDITION gRPC status

2.6 Pitfalls

  • Controller-trusts-node bitmaps divergence (P2 violation): if controller uses noded-reported bitmaps as authoritative, a crashed noded reporting zero allocation can trigger double-allocation. Fix: controller bitmaps are authoritative; noded reports are advisory diagnostics only.
  • Heartbeat interval too short under load: 1s interval × 10k nodes = 10k RPC/s on a single controller thread. Fix: default 10s; batch heartbeat processing inside a single write-lock acquisition.
  • gRPC reconnect storm: all nodeds reconnect simultaneously after controller restart. Fix: jitter in reconnect backoff seeded from node_id hash.
  • Node ID collision across racks: node_id derived from hostname; duplicate hostnames in misconfigured clusters cause state corruption. Fix: controller rejects duplicate registration from different socket addresses; operator must fix config.
  • Clock skew in last_seen comparison: Instant is monotonic per-process; controller uses its own Instant, not the noded-reported timestamp for timeout detection. Always correct.
  • TLS cert expiry silently drops heartbeats: gRPC transport error surfaces as timeout, not a cert error, unless tonic error codes are inspected. Log the underlying hyper error string.

2.7 Performance Concerns

  • Heartbeat handler: per-request write-lock acquisition on ControllerState is O(1) but contends with scheduling tick. Mitigate: heartbeat handler takes a write lock on a separate NodeInventory lock; scheduling tick reads node inventory via its own snapshot.
  • 10k nodes × 10s interval = 1000 heartbeat RPC/s; each gRPC request allocates protobuf decode buffer. Pool decode buffers with bytes::BytesMut reuse.
  • Timeout watcher scan: linear O(nodes); at 10k nodes with 64B per entry, fits in a single cache line group. Use a sorted BTreeMap<Instant, NodeId> for O(log n) earliest-expiry lookup.
  • broadcast event bus: unbounded channel risks memory growth if slow subscribers. Size channel at 8× node count; slow subscribers get Lagged error, logged as warning.

2.8 Edge Cases

  • noded starts before controld is up: backoff loop must not spin-loop; must respect shutdown signal during backoff sleep.
  • Network partition heals: node resumes heartbeat; controller transitions Down → Idle only after confirmed re-registration + heartbeat pair.
  • Controller receives heartbeat from unknown node_id (stale after controller restart): log warning, respond with UNKNOWN_NODE; noded must re-register on receiving this status.
  • Node reports alloc_gpu_mask with bits set that controller has not allocated: advisory mismatch; log metric node_bitmap_divergence_count; do not override controller authoritative state.
  • IPv6 addresses in --controller-addr: tonic endpoint must parse [::1]:port form; test both IPv4 and IPv6 in integration tests.

2.9 Failure Analysis

  • controld restart while nodeds are running: nodeds detect connection drop via gRPC keepalive timeout; trigger re-register loop; controller rebuilds inventory from re-registrations (full rebuild in < heartbeat_interval × 2 for all nodes). Jobs already allocated need reconciliation (Phase 10).
  • noded OOM / SIGKILL: heartbeat stops; controller declares node Down within 2 × heartbeat_interval; Phase 10 handles job requeue.
  • Network partition (not process death): identical to noded death from controller perspective; Down transition fires; when partition heals, re-register flow restores Idle state.
  • gRPC server port conflict: controld fails to bind → logged and exits; operator must free port.
  • Blast radius of spurious Down: jobs on that node may be requeued (Phase 10); false-positive Downs waste compute. Tune heartbeat_interval and timeout multiplier conservatively (3×).

2.10 Operational Notes

  • RUST_LOG=hpcsched_noded=info,hpcsched_controld=info; heartbeat events at trace.
  • Metric: heartbeat_rpc_duration_us histogram; nodes_registered_total gauge; nodes_down_total counter.
  • --heartbeat-interval <secs> flag on noded; controller reads same value from cluster config for timeout computation; they must agree.
  • gRPC reflection enabled in debug builds for grpcurl introspection.
  • Deployment: noded runs as a systemd service on each compute node; controld runs on management node(s). Restart policy: always. No state loss on noded restart (stateless daemon at this phase).

Phase 3 — Submission + pending queue + durable state

3.1 Objectives

  • Implement sbatch-analog submit flow: CLI → hpcsched-proto SubmitJob RPC → controller validates, assigns JobId, appends WAL::JobSubmitted record, inserts job into pending queue, acks with JobId.
  • Implement hpcsched-queue: priority-ordered pending queue, partition-filtered eligible set, O(log n) insert/remove.
  • Implement hpcsched-state: WAL (append-only log of StateRecord variants) + periodic snapshot checkpoint; replay on restart reconstructs exact committed state.
  • Idempotent submit: client-supplied client_token deduplicates; duplicate submit returns original JobId.
  • Constraints: P6 (durability before ack); no scheduling yet; no launch yet.

3.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

hpcsched-proto/proto/control.proto (additions) — applies P6 (B9 Gray&Reuter Ch.9 WAL-before-ack), ADR-05 (WAL format), I5 (replay correctness)

  • SubmitJob(SubmitJobRequest) -> SubmitJobResponse; CancelJob(CancelJobRequest) -> CancelJobResponse. [P6; ADR-05; B9 ARIES force/steal]
  • SubmitJobRequest: ResourceSpec (embedded proto), script_path, env (map), client_token (uuid string for idempotency).
  • SubmitJobResponse: job_id: u64, was_duplicate: bool.

hpcsched-queue/src/lib.rs

  • PendingQueue: internal BTreeMap<(u32 /* priority */, JobId), JobId> (descending priority → lowest priority key = highest priority job via key negation or Reverse); O(log n) insert, remove, peek.
  • push(job_id, priority), pop_highest() -> Option<JobId>, remove(job_id), peek_highest() -> Option<JobId>.
  • eligible_set(partition: &str, node_inventory: &NodeInventory) -> Vec<JobId>: filters pending jobs whose ResourceSpec.partition matches and whose resource request does not exceed any single node's total capacity (quick pre-filter, not full allocation attempt).
  • SchedulePass: newtype wrapping an ordered Vec<JobId> snapshot for a single scheduling cycle; consumed by hpcsched-sched in Phase 5.

hpcsched-state/src/wal.rs

  • StateRecord enum: JobSubmitted { job: Job, seq: u64 }, JobAllocated { job_id, alloc: Allocation, seq }, JobStateChange { job_id, from: JobState, to: JobState, seq }, JobCompleted { job_id, exit_code, seq }, CheckpointMarker { seq }.
  • WAL file: append-only, each record length-prefixed (4-byte little-endian) + bincode-serialized + CRC32 suffix.
  • Wal::append(record: &StateRecord) -> Result<u64, WalError>: fsync after write (or fdatasync on Linux); returns assigned seq.
  • Wal::read_from(seq: u64) -> impl Iterator<Item = Result<StateRecord, WalError>>: streaming read; truncates at first CRC mismatch (partial write recovery).

hpcsched-state/src/checkpoint.rs

  • Checkpoint: serialized snapshot of full ControllerState at a given seq; stored as a separate file checkpoint-<seq>.bin.
  • write_checkpoint(state: &ControllerState, seq: u64) -> Result<()>: atomic write (write to temp file, fsync, rename).
  • load_latest_checkpoint() -> Option<(ControllerState, u64)>: finds highest seq checkpoint file.
  • Checkpoint interval: configurable (default every 1000 WAL records or 5 minutes, whichever first).
  • Checkpoint file rotation: keep last 2 checkpoints; delete older.

hpcsched-state/src/replay.rs

  • replay(checkpoint: Option<(ControllerState, u64)>, wal: &Wal) -> Result<ControllerState>:
    1. Start from checkpoint state at seq_start (or empty state if no checkpoint).
    2. Replay WAL records with seq > seq_start in order.
    3. Apply each record to state via apply_record(state, record).
    4. Validate final invariants (I1, I3, I5) after replay; abort if violated.

hpcsched-controld/src/rpc/submit.rs

  • handle_submit(req):
    1. Validate ResourceSpec (call spec.validate()).
    2. Look up client_token in idempotency map (HashMap<String, JobId> in ControllerState); if present, return existing JobId with was_duplicate: true.
    3. Assign JobId via new_job_id().
    4. Construct Job with state Pending.
    5. Append WAL::JobSubmitted → get seq; do not ack until fsync returns.
    6. Insert job into ControllerState.jobs and PendingQueue.
    7. Store client_token → job_id in idempotency map.
    8. Return SubmitJobResponse { job_id, was_duplicate: false }.

hpcsched-queue integration in hpcsched-controld

  • ControllerState holds one PendingQueue per partition.
  • On submit: insert into correct partition queue.
  • Priority at submit time: static formula base_priority(qos, account) + age_factor = 0 (age grows each scheduling pass per Phase 8).

3.3 Deliverable

  • sbatch CLI submits a job script; returns JobId printed to stdout.
  • Job persists across controller restart: kill -9 controld → restart → squeue shows the same job in Pending state.
  • Idempotent submit: retry with same client_token → same JobId, no duplicate entry.
  • WAL file visible on disk; human-readable dump tool (--dump-wal) outputs seq, record type, job_id per line.

3.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
Submit 10k jobs, kill -9 controller, restart, verify all 10k Pending all present, correct job_ids
Duplicate submit (same client_token) × 100 exactly 1 job per token
Submit RPC p99 latency (fsync on local SSD) < 5 ms
Submit RPC p99 latency (fsync on tmpfs for CI) < 1 ms
WAL replay correctness (property test: random submit/cancel sequences) state identical to in-memory baseline
WAL truncation recovery (injected partial write at byte N) replay stops at truncation, no panic
PendingQueue insert/pop with 10k entries O(log n) verified by benchmark (< 500 µs for 10k ops)

3.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected output
fixtures/wal/submit_10_jobs.wal 10 JobSubmitted records replay yields 10 Pending jobs
fixtures/wal/partial_write.wal last record truncated mid-byte replay recovers 9 jobs, warns on truncation
fixtures/wal/crc_mismatch.wal record 5 has bad CRC replay stops at record 5, returns 4 jobs
fixtures/submit/idempotent_token.json same client_token twice second call returns was_duplicate: true
fixtures/submit/invalid_spec.json zero nodes requested INVALID_ARGUMENT gRPC status
fixtures/submit/unknown_partition.json partition not in config NOT_FOUND gRPC status
fixtures/checkpoint/checkpoint_seq1000.bin valid snapshot replay from checkpoint merges correctly with tail WAL

3.6 Pitfalls

  • Acking before fsync: if the WAL record is buffered in OS page cache and controller crashes before fsync, the job is lost but the client was told it was accepted. Fix: fdatasync (Linux) before returning from Wal::append; never use O_WRONLY without explicit sync.
  • JobId counter not persisted: after restart, counter resets to 0; replayed WAL has job_id = 5, new submit gets job_id = 1 — collision. Fix: replay apply_record must update id_counter to max(current, record.job_id + 1) for each JobSubmitted record.
  • Idempotency map not in checkpoint: after restart from checkpoint, old client_token entries are lost; duplicate submit creates second job. Fix: include idempotency map in checkpoint serialization; rebuild from WAL replay for tokens not in checkpoint.
  • Priority queue holding stale priorities: if a job's priority is recomputed in Phase 8, the BTreeMap key is stale. Fix: PendingQueue::reprioritize(job_id, new_priority) must remove-then-reinsert; do not mutate the key in place.
  • WAL file unbounded growth / atomic checkpoint rename across filesystems: without checkpointing, replay time grows linearly with cluster uptime; and if the checkpoint temp file is on a different filesystem from the destination, rename() fails with EXDEV. Fix: enforce checkpoint interval and truncate/archive WAL segments before the checkpoint seq; keep the temp file on the same filesystem (same directory) as the destination.

3.7 Performance Concerns

  • fsync per submit is the dominant latency contributor. Batch fsync: accumulate up to N records (configurable, default 32) or until 1ms deadline, then single fsync; wake all waiting submitters. Trade-off: slightly higher p99 but massively higher throughput under burst load.
  • Checkpoint serialization with bincode: for 10k jobs × ~256 bytes each = ~2.5 MB; serialization in < 50ms expected; measure with criterion benchmark.
  • PendingQueue per-partition: if cluster has 100 partitions, 100 BTreeMaps each with O(n/100) entries; scheduling pass iterates all partitions — keep per-partition queue small by design.
  • Idempotency map: HashMap<String, JobId> with UUID string keys; consider HashMap<u128, JobId> (parse UUID to u128 at insertion) for 2× smaller memory footprint. WAL segment file: pre-allocate file blocks (fallocate) to avoid fragmentation; write sequentially.

3.8 Edge Cases

  • Controller restart during checkpoint write: rename is atomic; partial temp file is detected by missing final rename; load falls back to previous checkpoint. Submit during checkpoint write: WAL append proceeds normally; checkpoint is taken of a consistent state snapshot while new appends queue behind; no deadlock because checkpoint holds a read lock and WAL append holds WAL mutex separately.
  • Empty WAL (brand new cluster): replay returns empty ControllerState; valid; no error. WAL record seq wraps u64: in practice impossible (10k submits/s × 100 years < 2^63); assert seq < u64::MAX - 1 in Wal::append.
  • client_token is empty string: treat as non-idempotent (do not deduplicate empty tokens); document this behavior.
  • Job submitted to a partition defined in config but with no registered nodes: job enters Pending; scheduler finds no candidates; job remains Pending until nodes register. This is correct behavior, not an error.

3.9 Failure Analysis

  • Disk full during WAL append: write() returns ENOSPC; Wal::append returns Err(WalError::DiskFull); handle_submit returns RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED to client; job is NOT inserted into memory state; controller continues operating (existing jobs unaffected). Alert: wal_write_errors_total counter.
  • Corruption of checkpoint file (bit rot, partial overwrite): load_latest_checkpoint returns Err; fallback to previous checkpoint; if both corrupt, replay from empty state using full WAL (monitor WAL file size). Replay of unknown StateRecord variant (schema evolution): skip-unknown strategy using #[serde(other)] on the enum; log unknown variant type; continue replay for forward compatibility.
  • Submit RPC timeout at client: client retries with same client_token; idempotency map returns same JobId; no duplicate. This is the intended behavior — document it in CLI help.
  • Concurrent submits from multiple clients: handle_submit holds write lock for duration of WAL append; serialized; no race. Contention under high submit rate is mitigated by batched fsync (3.7).

3.10 Operational Notes

  • WAL directory: --wal-dir <path> flag (default /var/lib/hpcsched/wal/); checkpoint directory: --checkpoint-dir <path> (default /var/lib/hpcsched/checkpoints/).
  • Log: every submit logs job_id, user, partition, cpus, mem, time_limit at info level for audit trail.
  • Metrics: jobs_submitted_total counter (labeled by partition); wal_append_duration_us histogram; checkpoint_duration_ms histogram; pending_queue_depth gauge per partition.
  • WAL dump tool: hpcsched-state --dump-wal <path> prints records as JSON lines; useful for postmortem.
  • Upgrade path: WAL format version embedded in file header; incompatible version triggers WalError::IncompatibleVersion and clean error message; operator must migrate or replay from an empty state.

Phase 4 — Resource Allocation Engine

4.1 Objectives

  • Implement hpcsched-alloc: per-node CoreBitmap, GpuSet, mem tracking; allocate/free as atomic transactions.
  • Realize allocate(spec: &ResourceSpec) -> Result<Allocation, AllocError> satisfying I1, I2, I3.
  • Support constraint filtering: node features, partition membership, contiguous socket/NUMA topology.
  • Fragmentation-aware best-fit node selection; deferred free on job teardown.
  • Expose allocation state to scheduling loop (Phase 5) and WAL (Phase 3).

4.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

hpcsched-alloc crate — types and API — applies P3 (I1 bitmap transaction), B5 (McKenney CAS memory ordering), B10 (Herlihy CAS-safe bitmap), ADR-11, ADR-12

Symbol Shape Invariant
CoreBitmap [u64; N_WORDS] fixed array, 1 bit = 1 logical CPU set bits = allocated
GpuSet u64 bitmask (max 64 GPUs/node) set bits = allocated
NodeAlloc { cores: CoreBitmap, mem_bytes: u64, gpus: GpuSet } sum ≤ node capacity (I2)
AllocatorState HashMap<NodeId, NodeAlloc> guarded by Mutex<AllocatorState> in controld single owner
Allocation { job_id: JobId, nodes: Vec<(NodeId, CoreBitmap, GpuSet, mem_bytes)> } populated iff Running/Configuring (I3)

Allocation algorithm (hpcsched-alloc::allocate)

  1. Filter node pool: partition membership → features superset → not Down/Drain.
  2. Score candidates with best-fit: prefer node with fewest free cores satisfying the per-node need; minimizes fragmentation.
  3. Topology-aware: if spec.nodes > 1 prefer same rack/switch (node feature tags rack=, switch=); fall back to any if unavailable.
  4. Contiguous socket: within a node, allocate physically contiguous core ranges using CoreBitmap::first_fit_range(n).
  5. For each selected node, lock AllocatorState, verify capacity still holds (re-check under lock), set bits transactionally with CoreBitmap::try_set_range / GpuSet::try_set_mask; rollback all bits on any node failure.
  6. Return Allocation; caller (controld) appends JobAllocated WAL record before mutating job state (P6).

Free path: free(alloc: &Allocation) clears bits; called on Completing→Completed/Failed/Cancelled; also on requeue (Phase 10).

CoreBitmap operations (signatures only)

impl CoreBitmap {
    fn first_fit_range(&self, n: usize) -> Option<usize>;        // first aligned run of n free bits
    fn try_set_range(&mut self, start: usize, n: usize) -> bool; // atomic CAS over word boundary
    fn clear_range(&mut self, start: usize, n: usize);
    fn free_count(&self) -> usize;
}

Instrumentation: alloc_attempt_total, alloc_success_total, alloc_fail_reason{reason} counters; node-level free_cores, free_mem_bytes, free_gpus gauges updated on every alloc/free.

Tests: unit — CoreBitmap boundary conditions; property — allocate then free returns identical state; integration — multi-node spec fills cluster and detects over-capacity.

4.3 Deliverable

  • hpcsched-alloc compiles as library; allocate/free pass unit + integration tests.
  • AllocatorState snapshot matches node inventory reported by Phase 2 noded registrations.
  • Fragmentation metric (average free-core run length per node) queryable via internal API.

4.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Threshold
try_set_range correctly handles word-boundary spans 100% of property tests
Double-allocate of same core rejected assertion in all test modes
free restores exact pre-alloc state byte-identical bitmap in unit tests
Alloc + free 1 000 000 ops/s single-threaded ≥ 1M ops/s on reference hardware
Topology-aware selection picks same-rack nodes when available fixture coverage
I1 / I2 never violated under concurrent alloc/free (loom test) 0 violations in 10 000 schedules

4.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected Output
alloc_basic.toml 4-node cluster, submit 2-node job valid Allocation with 2 NodeIds
alloc_overcommit.toml request more cores than available Err(AllocError::InsufficientCores)
alloc_feature_mismatch.toml job requires gpu=v100, no such node Err(AllocError::NoEligibleNode)
alloc_topology.toml 8-node cluster, 2 racks, 2-node job both nodes same rack
alloc_partial_rollback.toml 3-node job, third node full mid-alloc full rollback, no bits set
alloc_gpu_exclusive.toml 2 jobs competing for 4 GPUs total second job gets disjoint GpuSet
alloc_fuzz.toml randomized submit/cancel stream I1 holds throughout

4.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root Cause Debug Signal Recovery
TOCTOU free-core count race: check under no lock, alloc under lock stale read before acquiring Mutex<AllocatorState> alloc_fail_reason{stale} spike always re-verify under lock
Word-boundary CoreBitmap overflow silent double-alloc at bit 64 boundary off-by-one in try_set_range I1 violation in loom test unit-test every boundary explicitly
Topology tag absent in config all jobs land on same node missing rack= features node utilization skew metric document mandatory feature tags
GpuSet > 64 GPUs not supported silent truncation if cast to u64 bitmask width limit compile-time assert gpus ≤ 64 document limit; use Vec<u8> bitmap for extension
Free called twice (double-free) bits cleared on already-free resource epilog races cancellation handler debug assert on clear_range no-op idempotent free: assert bit was set before clearing
Rollback leaves partial state on panic permanently lost resources unwind without rollback guard free_cores gauge drift over time wrap alloc transaction in a RAII guard with rollback on drop

4.7 Performance Concerns

  • CoreBitmap word-scan hot path: keep N_WORDS ≤ 8 (512-CPU nodes); all words fit in a cache line pair.
  • AllocatorState lock is per-pass: acquire once per scheduling pass, batch all alloc decisions, release; avoid per-job lock round-trip.
  • first_fit_range: scan from hint offset (last alloc) to avoid re-scanning allocated prefix each pass.
  • free_count cached as atomic u32; recomputed lazily after free, avoids full bitmap scan on eligibility filter.
  • Topology sort is O(n log n) on candidate count; memoize rack/switch groupings at node registration time.
  • Avoid heap allocation inside hot alloc path: Allocation.nodes pre-allocated to spec.nodes capacity.

4.8 Edge Cases

  • Zero-CPU job (monitoring tasks): allowed; CoreBitmap empty range, memory-only allocation.
  • Node with 0 free GPUs but job requests 0 GPUs: must succeed — GpuSet comparison must test requested == 0 before bit-check.
  • spec.nodes exceeds live node count: detect early in filter step, return AllocError::InsufficientNodes.
  • Node removed mid-alloc (concurrent Down event): lock ordering — AllocatorState lock held before node state read; Down transition must acquire same lock.
  • mem_per_node overflow: validate spec.mem_per_node * spec.tasks ≤ u64::MAX at submission.
  • Reservation interaction (Phase 7): allocator must skip reserved nodes for backfill jobs; reservation mask passed as exclude: Option<&NodeSet> parameter.

4.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Containment Recovery
Allocator lock poisoned (panic inside) Mutex::lock() returns PoisonError all subsequent alloc fail catch poison, log, reload state from WAL Phase 3 WAL replay reconstructs AllocatorState
Bitmap corruption (hardware/memory) I2 violation detected by periodic audit miscounted resources mark node Down, requeue jobs audit task runs every 60s; log discrepancy
WAL record missing for alloc restart sees job Running without Allocation job treated as Failed on replay accept and requeue Phase 3 replay handles missing alloc by transitioning to Requeued

4.10 Operational Notes

  • Metric alloc_free_cores_pct{node}: per-node utilization gauge; alert if cluster-wide avg < 5% for > 5 min (queue depth may be hiding pending jobs).
  • Trace span alloc_request{job_id} with attributes: nodes_requested, nodes_eligible, nodes_selected, topology_satisfied.
  • Log level INFO: every successful Allocation (job_id, nodes, cores, mem, gpus). DEBUG: candidate scoring per node.
  • No schema migration needed for bitmaps (fixed-width per node capacity); node capacity changes require node re-registration and WAL NodeUpdated record.
  • Deploy note: CoreBitmap N_WORDS is a compile-time const; changing it requires recompile + full state wipe (document as breaking change).

Phase 5 — Priority Scheduling Loop

5.1 Objectives

  • Implement hpcsched-sched: the main scheduling pass iterating PendingQueue in priority order, calling hpcsched-alloc::allocate, transitioning jobs Pending→Configuring.
  • Enforce partition limits (max_nodes, max_time) before each allocation attempt.
  • Static priority + submit-time tiebreak (Phase 8 adds full multifactor; this phase uses job.priority as opaque u32).
  • Pass must be deterministic given identical state (P8).
  • Integrate with hpcsched-queue for eligible-set computation and hpcsched-controld event loop.

5.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

hpcsched-queue crate — applies P8 (P4 Feitelson deterministic scheduling), P9 (depth limit), ADR-12, ADR-13

Component Description
PendingQueue BinaryHeap<PriorityEntry> (priority desc, submit_time asc tiebreak); heap rebuilt on priority recompute
PriorityEntry { priority: u32, submit_time: Instant, job_id: JobId } — Ord impl: priority desc, then submit_time asc
eligible_set() filter: job.state == Pending, partition active, user not over job-count limit; returns ordered iterator
promote(job_id) remove from heap, caller transitions job state

hpcsched-sched crate — main pass

Signature:

pub fn scheduling_pass(queue: &mut PendingQueue, alloc: &mut AllocatorState, nodes: &NodeInventory, partitions: &PartitionMap, state_log: &mut StateLog, clock: &dyn Clock) -> SchedulePass;

Algorithm (pseudocode in terms of types, not code):

  1. Snapshot eligible set from PendingQueue::eligible_set() into a local Vec<PriorityEntry> (deterministic order).
  2. For each entry (highest priority first): fetch Job, check partition limits (ResourceSpec.time_limit ≤ partition.max_time, ResourceSpec.nodes ≤ partition.max_nodes), then call allocate(spec, alloc, nodes) from hpcsched-alloc.
  3. On Ok(alloc): WAL-append JobAllocated{job_id, alloc, timestamp} → transition job.state = Configuring → remove from PendingQueue → accumulate in SchedulePass.allocated. On Err: record reason in SchedulePass.skipped; continue (do not short-circuit entire pass).
  4. Return SchedulePass { allocated, skipped, pass_duration }.

Scheduling pass rate: invoked by controld event loop — triggered on: job submit, job complete, node state change, or 30s periodic tick. Coalesced: multiple triggers within 100ms fire one pass.

SchedulePass carries allocated: Vec<JobId>, skipped: Vec<(JobId, SkipReason)>, pass_duration: Duration. Partition limits are checked before allocate; SkipReason::PartitionTimeLimitExceeded, SkipReason::PartitionNodeLimitExceeded.

Observability hooks: emit trace span scheduling_pass with attributes jobs_considered, jobs_allocated, pass_duration_us; increment counters sched_pass_total, sched_allocated_total, sched_skipped_total{reason}.

Test harnesses: determinism (same state twice → equal SchedulePass), priority ordering (higher-priority job allocated first when resources permit), partition limit rejection (max_time violation appears in skipped with correct reason), and starvation detection (job stuck > N passes logs job_id, skip_reasons_seen).

5.3 Deliverable

  • hpcsched-sched + hpcsched-queue compile and link into controld.
  • Scheduling pass runs on trigger; allocated jobs appear in Configuring state.
  • Pass duration logged and exported as metric.
  • squeue --state=Configuring (Phase 13 CLI) shows newly allocated jobs.

5.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Threshold
Determinism: same state → same pass output 100% across 10 000 randomized states
Priority ordering respected unit test: high-priority job always allocated first when resources permit
Partition max_time rejection correct fixture sched_partition_limit.toml
Pass over 10 000 pending jobs < 200ms on reference hardware (Phase 14 target)
No job starved > 1000 passes without diagnostic log assert in integration test
Pending→Configuring transition WAL-appended before state change verified by restart-replay fixture

5.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected
sched_basic.toml 10 jobs, priority descending, ample resources all allocated in priority order
sched_tiebreak.toml 3 jobs equal priority, different submit_time allocated in submit_time asc order
sched_partition_limit.toml job time_limit > partition.max_time SkipReason::PartitionTimeLimitExceeded
sched_insufficient_nodes.toml job requests 100 nodes, 50 available SkipReason::InsufficientNodes
sched_determinism.toml replay same state twice byte-identical SchedulePass
sched_coalesce.toml 10 rapid job submits exactly one pass fires
sched_empty_queue.toml no pending jobs SchedulePass with empty allocated
sched_priority_inversion.toml low-resource high-priority job must not be blocked by resource gap allocated; lower-priority large job stays Pending

5.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root Cause Signal Recovery
Non-deterministic HashMap iteration different allocation decisions across restarts hash randomization determinism test fails iterate NodeInventory via sorted Vec<NodeId> snapshot
Pass fires too frequently CPU spin, starvation of I/O threads coalescing timer too short sched_pass_total rate > 100/s enforce 100ms minimum inter-pass interval
Partition limit checked after alloc attempt wasted alloc attempts check ordering error sched_skipped_total{PartitionLimit} near zero despite limit violations enforce partition checks first in eligibility filter
Priority heap not rebuilt after recompute; starvation of large jobs stale order / small jobs always win multifactor recompute (Phase 8) writes job.priority but heap not re-sorted; priority tie resolved by size (wrong) wrong job allocated; sched_skipped_total grows for specific job_ids PendingQueue::rebuild() after every priority recompute; tiebreak by submit_time only, age factor (Phase 8) resolves long-run starvation
WAL append failure halts pass no jobs allocated on disk I/O error flush error not handled wal_append_error_total spike surface error, skip that job, continue pass; alert operator

5.7 Performance Concerns

  • BinaryHeap pop is O(log n) (~14 comparisons/job at 10k pending — acceptable); eligible-set snapshot into Vec is one allocation per pass (not per job), pre-allocated with queue.len() capacity.
  • allocate inner loop dominates pass time; Phase 4 batches lock acquisition — no per-job lock cycle.
  • Coalescing: use tokio::time::sleep debounce; avoid Arc<AtomicBool> ping-pong across threads.
  • Pass duration histogram buckets: 1ms, 5ms, 10ms, 50ms, 100ms, 200ms, 500ms, 1s.
  • Priority recompute (Phase 8) cost amortized: recompute once per pass, not per job; total O(n) recompute + O(n log n) heap rebuild.

5.8 Edge Cases

  • Job priority updated (by admin or fair-share recompute) while job is mid-iteration in current pass: use snapshot; updated priority applies next pass. PendingQueue empty between coalesced triggers: pass runs with 0 jobs, returns empty SchedulePass; O(1) early return.
  • All nodes Down: every job skipped with InsufficientNodes; pass must not crash; log cluster-wide warning. Job submitted with unknown partition: caught at submit validation (Phase 3); never reaches scheduling pass.
  • Clock skew between submit_time records (Phase 12 failover): use monotonic logical timestamps (WAL sequence number) for tiebreak under failover. Job transitions from Pending to Cancelled mid-pass (concurrent cancel RPC): job removed from heap; scheduling pass skips missing job gracefully (lookup by job_id returns None).

5.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Containment Recovery
Scheduling loop panic thread crash, no more allocations all pending jobs stall controld catches panic, logs, restarts loop loop is a supervised task; exponential backoff restart
WAL disk full JobAllocated cannot be written jobs not promoted; queue backs up wal_append_error_total alert operator clears space; queue drains automatically
Priority heap corruption jobs allocated out of order fairness violation determinism fixture mismatch rebuild heap from job.priority map on detection
controld event loop blocked pass not triggered for > 60s jobs accumulate in Pending sched_last_pass_age_seconds alert > 60 watchdog timer triggers pass directly

5.10 Operational Notes

  • Metric sched_pass_duration_seconds histogram: P50/P95/P99; alert P99 > 500ms.
  • Metric pending_queue_depth: gauge; alert > 10k with utilization > 90%.
  • Log at INFO: every pass — {allocated: N, skipped: M, duration_ms: D}.
  • Log at WARN: job skipped > 100 consecutive passes (starvation risk).
  • squeue -t PENDING --sort=-p shows priority-ordered queue; output driven directly from PendingQueue snapshot.
  • Rolling deploy: scheduling pass is stateless function over shared AllocatorState; no migration needed across binary updates.
  • Disable periodic pass via config flag sched.periodic_interval = 0 for test environments relying only on event-driven triggers.

Phase 6 — Job Launch + Steps + Lifecycle

6.1 Objectives

  • Implement job launch: controld sends LaunchMsg to each allocated node's hpcsched-noded instance.
  • noded spawns job step processes, enforces resources via cgroup, monitors exit, reports back.
  • State machine: Configuring→Running→Completing→Completed/Failed/Cancelled.
  • Capture exit codes; epilog cleanup per node; srun interactive step support.
  • Integrate with WAL: JobLaunched, StepStarted, StepCompleted, JobCompleted WAL records.

6.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

hpcsched-proto additions (Phase 6 messages) — applies P1 (R1: slurmd prolog/epilog integration), P7 (B11 Barroso graceful degradation at node level), ADR-08 (resource isolation model), I2 (TRES non-overcommit via cgroup enforcement), I10 (TRES-seconds accounting from cgroup reports)

Message Direction Fields
LaunchMsg controld→noded job_id, step_id, allocation_slice: (CoreBitmap, GpuSet, mem_bytes), argv, env, cwd, uid, gid, prolog_path, epilog_path
StepStatusMsg noded→controld job_id, step_id, state: StepState, exit_code: Option<i32>, cpu_time_us, mem_peak_bytes
CancelStepMsg controld→noded job_id, step_id, signal: i32
InteractiveConnectMsg srun→noded job_id, step_id, pty: bool, stdin/stdout/stderr forwarding metadata

hpcsched-noded — launch path

  1. Receive LaunchMsg over control-plane RPC connection (Phase 2); run prolog before exec (blocking); create cgroup hierarchy hpcsched/<job_id>/<step_id>/ — set cpuset.cpus from CoreBitmap, memory.limit_in_bytes, devices.allow for assigned GPUs.
  2. Fork-exec job step: set UID/GID, bind-mount working dir, exec argv with env; place child in cgroup before exec completes (clone + CLONE_INTO_CGROUP or write PID to cgroup.procs immediately after fork); register in noded step table (HashMap<StepId, StepHandle>, StepHandle = { pid, cgroup_path, start_time }).
  3. Spawn async monitor task: waitpid (or pidfd_open + epoll) on child PID; on exit collect exit_code, peak mem from memory.max_usage_in_bytes, run epilog (async, cgroup cleanup after), send StepStatusMsg to controld.
  4. On CancelStepMsg: send signal to cgroup (cgroup.kill v2 or kill(-pgid)); if SIGKILL follow up after grace_period_ms (default 30s).

controld — launch orchestration

  1. On job.state = Configuring: for each (node_id, slice) in Allocation.nodes, send LaunchMsg; wait for StepStatusMsg{state: Running} from all allocated nodes → transition job Running, WAL-append JobLaunched.
  2. On any StepStatusMsg{state: Failed/Completed}: collect all node reports; when all received, transition to Completing; run epilog acknowledgment from all nodes; transition to Completed/Failed based on any non-zero exit code.
  3. Release Allocation bits (call hpcsched-alloc::free), WAL-append JobCompleted, trigger next scheduling pass.

StepId: StepId(job_id: JobId, step_index: u32); interactive srun steps have step_index ≥ 1; batch step is step_index = 0.

srun interactive steps:

  • srun CLI sends InteractiveConnectMsg to noded directly (after controld issues interactive allocation).
  • noded allocates a sub-slice of job's cgroup; spawns process with PTY if requested; forwards stdin/stdout/stderr over the RPC stream.
  • Controld tracks interactive steps in job record; job transitions Completing only after all steps (batch + interactive) exit.

State machine summary:

From Event To WAL Record
Configuring all LaunchMsg sent, Running ack from all nodes Running JobLaunched
Configuring noded unreachable / timeout Requeued or Failed JobRequeued / JobFailed
Running all steps exited (0 or non-0) Completing JobCompleting
Running CancelStepMsg sent Completing JobCancelling
Completing all epilog ACKs received Completed or Failed JobCompleted
Completing epilog timeout Failed JobFailed

Tests: unit — cgroup path construction; integration — fork+exec a /bin/true step, observe StepStatusMsg{Completed, exit_code: 0}; cancellation — send SIGTERM, verify cgroup killed within grace period; epilog failure — epilog exits non-zero → job Failed.

6.3 Deliverable

  • controld sends LaunchMsg; noded spawns processes in cgroups; exit observed and reported.
  • Job progresses Configuring→Running→Completing→Completed end-to-end in integration test.
  • Exit code captured and stored on Job.times.exit_code.
  • Epilog runs; cgroup hierarchy cleaned up after every job.
  • srun sends interactive step and receives stdout; step tracked in job record.

6.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Threshold
/bin/true → Completed exit_code=0; /bin/false → Failed exit_code=1 integration test
Cgroup removed after job completion, including after SIGKILL cancel within grace_period + 5s assert!(!Path::new(cgroup_path).exists()) post-job; integration test
Memory limit enforced: OOM kill triggers Failed cgroup OOM integration test
Configuring→Running latency < 2s on loopback benchmark
srun interactive step: echo back 100 lines through PTY integration test
WAL replay after launch: controld restart, job in Running resumes tracking replay fixture

6.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected
launch_basic.toml 1-node /bin/true job Completed, exit_code=0
launch_fail.toml /bin/false Failed, exit_code=1
launch_oom.toml process allocates > mem limit Failed, OOM signal captured
launch_cancel.toml cancel Running job Completed (cancelled), all cgroups removed
launch_prolog_fail.toml prolog exits 1 job stays Configuring, transitions Failed without reaching Running
launch_epilog_timeout.toml epilog hangs > epilog_timeout noded kills epilog, reports Failed
launch_multinode.toml 3-node MPI-like job Running on all 3; completion waits for all 3
launch_srun_interactive.toml srun step over PTY stdout bytes match expected
launch_noded_crash.toml / launch_replay.toml noded SIGKILL mid-job; controld restart after JobLaunched Requeued via heartbeat timeout (Phase 10); Running jobs re-tracked on replay

6.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root Cause Signal Recovery
PID reuse after step exit wrong process monitored delay between waitpid and pidfd poll exit_code mismatch in tests use pidfd_open immediately after fork; never store raw PID without fd
Cgroup not cleaned up on noded crash resource accounting drift, new jobs get wrong limits epilog skipped free_mem_bytes drift; stale cgroup dirs controld detects heartbeat timeout, marks node Down, noded restarts and cleans residual cgroups at startup
Job stuck in Completing (epilog hangs) resources not freed, queue starves epilog script with infinite loop job_completing_age_seconds alert per-job epilog timeout (default 120s); kill epilog on timeout, transition Failed
Configuring timeout if noded unreachable job stuck, allocation held network partition between controld and noded launch_ack_timeout_total counter configuring_timeout (default 300s): if no Running ack, requeue job, free allocation
Double-free on concurrent cancel + completion; srun step orphaned after failover I1 violation; step running but untracked cancel RPC races natural completion; no re-association of interactive steps assertion in CoreBitmap::clear_range; step_orphan_total counter on noded restart Allocation.freed: AtomicBool idempotent free; noded re-reports all live steps on reconnect, controld reconciles

6.7 Performance Concerns

  • LaunchMsg serialization: pre-encode CoreBitmap as compact bytes (N_WORDS * 8); avoid per-core JSON fields.
  • Fork-exec cost ~1ms/step on Linux (not a bottleneck for batch; srun interactive latency < 500ms target is the sensitive path); cgroup cpuset.cpus write ~100µs — preallocate hierarchy at node daemon startup for known job slots.
  • waitpid vs pidfd_open: prefer pidfd_open + async I/O to avoid dedicated monitor thread per step.
  • Completing state: collect all node reports before freeing allocation using a JoinSet or counter per job_id (not a blocking wait); epilog is I/O bound (script execution) and runs in a separate thread pool, never blocking the noded RPC handler.

6.8 Edge Cases

  • Job with 0 allocated nodes (pure accounting job): skip LaunchMsg loop, transition directly Configuring→Running→Completing. Prolog exit non-zero: do not exec step, transition to Failed, still run epilog for cleanup.
  • Node reports StepStatus{Running} twice, or controld sends CancelStepMsg after step already completed: both idempotent — noded ignores if already in reported state, or returns StepStatus{Completed} again; controld accepts.
  • LaunchMsg arrives at noded for a JobId already in step table: indicates replay or duplicate send — check WAL for prior StepStarted, respond with current status.
  • Cgroup v1 vs v2: detect at noded startup (/sys/fs/cgroup/cgroup.controllers existence); use appropriate subsystem paths; document minimum kernel version 5.2 for cgroup v2.
  • PTY allocation failure (no PTYs available): return error to srun, do not fail batch step. Exit code 137 (SIGKILL): record as exit_code=137, not as OOM, unless /oom_kill cgroup counter is non-zero.

6.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Containment Recovery
noded loses RPC connection mid-job controld heartbeat timeout jobs on that node stuck Configuring/Running mark node Down (Phase 10), requeue requeueable jobs noded reconnect re-reports live steps; non-requeueable jobs Failed
OOM kill mid-step cgroup OOM event single step fails other steps cancelled via CancelStepMsg job transitions Failed with exit_code=137 and oom_killed=true
controld crash between JobLaunched and JobCompleted restart jobs running without controld tracking WAL replay: JobLaunched present → re-track; query noded for step status step_status_query RPC on startup for all Running jobs found in WAL
Epilog leaves residual files / cgroup creation fails (permission denied) disk fills on compute node / LaunchMsg returns error future jobs fail to start / job cannot start disk usage alert / job transitions Failed immediately, node flagged epilog timeout + forced kill, documented cleanup; verify noded runs with CAP_SYS_ADMIN, alert cgroup_create_error_total

6.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: job_configuring_duration_seconds (Configuring→Running, P99 alert > 10s), job_completing_duration_seconds (Completing→terminal, P99 alert > 300s), step_exit_code{code} histogram (spike in non-zero codes indicates app/environment problem), cgroup_oom_kill_total{node} per-node OOM events.
  • Logging: INFO — JobLaunched{job_id, nodes, step_id}, JobCompleted{job_id, exit_code, duration_s}; WARN — prolog/epilog failures, configuring timeout, duplicate step status; DEBUG — every StepStatusMsg received.
  • Tracing: span job_launch{job_id} wraps LaunchMsg send through Running ack; child spans per node.
  • noded startup reconciliation: on connect to controld, noded lists all live cgroups and reports any step not in controld's Running set (orphan detection).
  • Rolling deploy of noded: drain node (scontrol drain) before noded upgrade; undrain after restart; existing jobs on other nodes unaffected.

Phase 7 — EASY Backfill

7.1 Objectives

  • Compute a reservation (window start + node set) for the highest-priority pending job each scheduling pass.
  • Backfill lower-priority jobs into currently-idle resources whose time-window closes before the reservation starts.
  • Guarantee P4: reservation start never pushed right by any backfilled job.
  • Require time-limit honesty: jobs without a finite time limit cannot be backfilled (no window bound).
  • Depth-limit backfill scan to enforce P9 (bounded pass latency); configurable backfill_depth (default 500).
  • Integrate with hpcsched-sched, reading state from hpcsched-alloc + hpcsched-queue.

7.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Reservation computation — hpcsched-sched::backfill::compute_reservation — applies P2 (Lifka 1995 EASY definition), P3 (Mu'alem 2001 runtime-estimate honesty), I4, ADR-22

  • Input: top pending Job, current Node inventory with alloc_cores/alloc_gpus/alloc_mem.
  • Walk running jobs sorted by (estimated_end = start_time + time_limit) ascending.
  • Simulate resource release: maintain a timeline of node states; at each release event, test whether top_job.spec can be satisfied.
  • Reservation { window: TimeWindow, node_set: NodeSet, job_id: JobId } stored in SchedulerState.
  • Re-compute reservation every pass; invalidate on any topology change (node state, job completion, submit).

Backfill pass — hpcsched-sched::backfill::backfill_pass

  • Iterate pending queue from rank 1 (skip rank 0 = top job) up to backfill_depth.
  • Per candidate job: (a) has finite time_limit; (b) now + time_limit <= reservation.window.start; (c) fits current idle resources.
  • If all pass: allocate (via hpcsched-alloc::allocate), push BackfillPlan entry, transition job to Configuring.
  • After each allocation: update idle resource snapshot; re-check remaining candidates against updated snapshot.
  • Do not re-run main priority pass inside backfill; backfill is additive only.

Data structures

Type Location Role
Reservation hpcsched-core window + node_set for top job
BackfillPlan hpcsched-sched Vec<(JobId, start_time, Allocation)>
ResourceSnapshot hpcsched-sched idle per-node bitmaps at current instant
Timeline hpcsched-sched::backfill sorted Vec<(time, NodeId, freed_resources)>

Persistence: backfill allocations follow same WAL path as main-pass allocations (Phase 3); Reservation is ephemeral — not written to WAL, recomputed on restart.

Observability hooks: emit backfill_jobs_started, backfill_reservation_start_time, backfill_depth_scanned counters each pass.

7.3 Deliverable

  • backfill_pass runs after main priority pass in the scheduling loop; squeue shows backfilled jobs as Running.
  • sinfo --reservation (Phase 13) shows active reservation: start time, node set, for-job-id.
  • Integration test: 4-node cluster, top job needs 4 nodes, smaller jobs fill 2-node gaps before reservation window.

7.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
Top job never delayed Reservation start monotonically non-decreasing once set
Backfill correctness Backfilled job end_time <= reservation.start in 100% of unit tests
No double-alloc I1/I2 hold after every backfill pass (bitmap sum check)
Depth limit Pass with 10k pending jobs completes within scheduling-pass latency SLO
Finite time-limit enforcement Jobs with time_limit == None never appear in BackfillPlan
Replay Controller restart re-derives same Running set from WAL (reservation not in WAL)

7.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture file Purpose Expected output
backfill_basic.yaml 1 top job (4 nodes), 3 small backfill jobs All 3 backfilled; top job starts at reserved time
backfill_no_delay.yaml Backfill candidate would push reservation right Candidate skipped
backfill_no_timelimit.yaml Candidate lacks time_limit Not backfilled
backfill_depth_cap.yaml Queue depth 600, cap 500 Only first 500 scanned
backfill_reservation_update.yaml Running job completes early Reservation recalculated; earlier start
backfill_partial_nodes.yaml Topology with mixed-state nodes Backfill uses only idle nodes
backfill_replay.yaml Crash mid-backfill; replay WAL Allocations recovered; reservation recomputed

7.6 Pitfalls

  • Stale resource snapshot during backfill loop: updating snapshot in-loop after each backfill allocation but forgetting to account for newly Configuring jobs -> over-allocation. Fix: always subtract Configuring allocations from snapshot before each candidate check.
  • Infinite reservation horizon: if the top job can never fit (nodes down + no forecast of release), compute_reservation returns an infinitely-far window, blocking all backfill. Detection: reservation_start_age gauge; alert if > 2x top-job time_limit.
  • Time-limit dishonesty / job exceeding limit: jobs killed by noded at time_limit expiry, but backfill assumed the slot was free. Backfill window must use wall_limit, never user estimate. Mitigation: noded enforces hard kill at time_limit; reconciliation on heartbeat.
  • Backfill thrash: frequent topology changes (node down, new submits) cause reservation to move, previously backfilled slots become invalid mid-pass. Hold a pass-local snapshot; do not re-compute reservation mid-pass.
  • Priority inversion via backfill: a low-priority job backfilled runs while a medium-priority job is still Pending. Acceptable by EASY semantics, but must be logged (backfill_priority_inversion_count) for operator audit.
  • Backfill pass latency blowup: O(depth * nodes) timeline simulation. Cap timeline events to backfill_depth * max_nodes_per_job. Emit backfill_pass_duration_ms histogram.

7.7 Performance Concerns

  • Timeline simulation is O(N_jobs * N_nodes); memoize node release events in a BTreeMap<Instant, Vec<ResourceRelease>>.
  • Resource snapshot clone per candidate: use NodeSnapshot as a compact bitset (not full Node struct) to stay cache-friendly.
  • Depth cap (backfill_depth = 500) is the primary latency guardrail; tune per cluster size.
  • Avoid heap allocation inside inner backfill loop; pre-allocate BackfillPlan with Vec::with_capacity(backfill_depth).
  • Reservation recomputation is O(N_running); skip if no running job changed since last pass (dirty flag on running set).

7.8 Edge Cases

  • Top job has time_limit = None: reservation impossible; treat as infinite start; backfill disabled. Log warning.
  • All nodes idle: top job allocated by main pass; no backfill needed; pass exits early.
  • Two jobs tied at top priority: deterministic tiebreak (submit_time, then job_id) required for reproducibility (P8).
  • Reservation node_set overlaps with nodes mid-drain: exclude Draining nodes from reservation candidate set (I6).
  • Backfill candidate's time_limit > reservation.window.duration: reject even if resources available (P4).
  • Clock skew between controld and noded: use controld monotonic clock for all timeline calculations; noded clocks are reference only.

7.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Recovery
Reservation never computed (bug) backfill_reservation_set gauge = 0 for > 1 pass Backfill disabled; top job unaffected Restart scheduling loop
Double-allocation post-backfill I1 check fails; bitmap assertion Job fails on noded WAL-based requeue; bitmap re-scan
Timeline OOM (huge cluster) backfill_timeline_build_ms spike; allocator panic Scheduling pass skips backfill Reduce depth cap; add node-count guard
Backfilled job evicted by preemption (Phase 9) Job transitions Preempted Slot freed; reservation re-evaluated Normal preemption flow; requeue if requeueable

7.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: backfill_jobs_started_total (counter), backfill_reservation_start_epoch (gauge), backfill_pass_duration_ms (histogram), backfill_depth_scanned (histogram).
  • Log fields on each pass: top_job_id, reservation_start, backfill_count, depth_scanned.
  • sinfo --reservation exposes active Reservation; squeue --start shows estimated start times (derived from reservation or backfill plan).
  • Deployment: backfill is enabled by default; disable via backfill_enabled = false in partition config to fall back to strict FCFS for debugging.
  • Rollback: disabling backfill mid-run leaves running backfilled jobs untouched; only new pass omits backfill step.

Phase 8 — Multifactor Priority + Fair-Share

8.1 Objectives

  • Implement classic Slurm multifactor priority: priority = sum(w_i * factor_i) over age, fair-share, partition, QoS, job_size.
  • Build hierarchical association tree (Account/Association) with decayed usage (half-life configurable).
  • PriorityFactors computed per job each scheduling pass; ranking is deterministic (P8).
  • Fair-share factor = F(j) = shares_j / (2 * usage_j) (Slurm FIFO/classic formula), normalized 0–1.
  • All weights and decay parameters in cluster config; no hard-coded magic numbers.
  • Located in hpcsched-fairshare; consumed by hpcsched-sched main pass + backfill.

8.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Association tree — hpcsched-fairshare::tree — applies P5 (Schwiegelshohn competitive-ratio justification), P15 (Kalyvianaki decay formula), R4 (Slurm multifactor priority source), ADR-12

  • Association { account: AccountId, parent: Option<AccountId>, shares: u32, usage_decayed: f64, children: Vec<AccountId> }
  • Tree loaded from config + updated by hpcsched-acct on job completion.
  • Usage decay: each scheduling pass (or time-based trigger), apply usage_new = usage_old * decay_factor where decay_factor = 0.5^(elapsed / half_life).
  • Effective fair-share normalized up the tree: child's effective shares = (child.shares / parent_total_shares) * parent_effective_shares.

Priority computation — hpcsched-fairshare::priority

fn compute_priority(job: &Job, factors: &PriorityWeights, fs_tree: &FairShareTree, now: Instant) -> u32
  • Age factor: min((now - job.submit_time) / age_reset_factor, 1.0) * w_age.
  • Fair-share factor: (assoc.shares / (2.0 * assoc.usage_decayed + epsilon)) normalized * w_fairshare.
  • Partition factor: partition.priority_tier mapped to [0.0, 1.0] * w_partition.
  • QoS factor: qos.priority normalized * w_qos.
  • Job-size factor: 1.0 - (requested_cpus / cluster_total_cpus) (smaller jobs slightly preferred) * w_size.
  • Sum to u32; ties broken by submit_time then job_id (ascending, i.e., older first).

PriorityFactors struct (in hpcsched-core):

Field Type Description
age f64 normalized age factor
fairshare f64 normalized fair-share factor
partition f64 partition priority tier
qos f64 QoS priority
job_size f64 size-adjusted factor
weights PriorityWeights w_age, w_fairshare, w_partition, w_qos, w_size

Scheduling integration

  • hpcsched-sched::main_pass calls fairshare::compute_priority for all Pending jobs; sorts PendingQueue by descending priority before pass.
  • Priority recomputed every pass; not cached across passes (weights or usage may have changed).
  • PendingQueue::priority_order() returns Vec<JobId> sorted by descending priority.

Decay scheduling: run FairShareTree::apply_decay(elapsed) once per decay_interval (configurable, default 60 s) inside the scheduling event loop; not per pass to avoid over-decay.

Persistence: FairShareTree snapshot written to checkpoint; WAL records UsageUpdated(account, delta_tres_seconds) on job completion. Replay restores usage exactly (I10).

8.3 Deliverable

  • squeue shows jobs in priority order matching computed PriorityFactors.
  • sprio analog command (Phase 13 extension) dumps per-job factor breakdown.
  • Fair-share decay observable: a heavy user's jobs drop in priority over time as decay runs.
  • All weights zero-able individually; setting w_fairshare = 0 degrades to pure FCFS.

8.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
Priority monotonicity I8: same state -> same priority ranking across 1000 deterministic replays
Fair-share decay After 2 * half_life, usage halved twice; test with fixed elapsed
Age factor growth Job submitted 1h ago has higher age factor than job submitted 1s ago
Tie-break determinism Two identical jobs: lower job_id ranked first (submit_time equal)
Weight zeroing w_fairshare = 0 -> priority independent of usage; unit test
Tree normalization Sum of child shares / parent shares <= 1.0 for all non-root nodes
Scheduling pass order priority_order() matches hand-computed ranking for 20-job fixture

8.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected output
priority_age_only.yaml All weights 0 except w_age Oldest pending job ranks first
priority_fairshare.yaml Two accounts, one heavy user Heavy user's jobs rank lower
priority_decay.yaml Advance clock by 3x half_life Usage at 12.5% of original
priority_qos_override.yaml High-QoS job submitted last Ranks first despite young age
priority_size_factor.yaml Small vs large job, equal age Small job higher priority
priority_tree_normalization.yaml 3-level account tree Leaf effective shares computed correctly
priority_weights_zero.yaml All weights = 0 except age Ranking = pure FCFS
priority_replay.yaml Checkpoint + WAL replay Post-replay priority order identical

8.6 Pitfalls

  • Decay over-application: applying decay per scheduling pass rather than per elapsed wall time causes exponential decay proportional to pass frequency. Fix: track last_decay_time; compute elapsed since last application; apply once per decay_interval.
  • Division by zero in fair-share: usage_decayed reaches 0 (new account, no jobs ever). Fix: add epsilon = 1e-9 to denominator; minimum usage floor in config.
  • f64 precision drift: summing TRES-seconds over long runs drifts. Store usage as integer tres_seconds: u64 internally; convert to f64 only for factor computation.
  • Stale priority on topology change: job priority depends on cluster_total_cpus (size factor); if a node is added/removed, all priorities shift. Recompute all factors each pass (already mandated); do not cache.
  • Association tree missing entry: job submitted to unknown account. Reject at submit with AccountNotFound error; do not fall back to root (silent misconfiguration).
  • Weight misconfiguration producing overflow: sum of max factors * weights can exceed u32::MAX. Normalize final sum to [0, u32::MAX] explicitly; document max per-weight range in config schema.

8.7 Performance Concerns

  • Priority computation: O(N_pending) per pass; each job is O(1) factor lookup + arithmetic. Bottleneck at >100k pending jobs.
  • Sort: PendingQueue re-sort each pass is O(N log N); for large queues, use a partial sort (top-K) if backfill depth << N.
  • FairShareTree traversal for normalization: O(N_accounts); pre-compute effective shares at tree load time, update only on structural changes.
  • Decay computation: O(N_accounts) per decay tick; cheap relative to pass cost.
  • Avoid per-pass allocation of PriorityFactors structs; reuse a scratch buffer in the scheduler struct.

8.8 Edge Cases

  • New user / account with zero usage: fair-share factor = max (1.0); epsilon prevents div-by-zero.
  • Account with shares = 0: factor = 0; job still scheduled but at lowest fair-share priority.
  • Clock jump backward (NTP correction): elapsed negative -> skip decay tick (no negative decay). Log warning.
  • Job submitted before association tree loaded at startup: queue with AccountUnresolved state; resolve on first pass after tree loaded.
  • Weights changed at runtime (config reload): apply new weights immediately on next pass; no migration needed (ephemeral per-pass computation).
  • Job in Requeued state (from Phase 9/10): re-enters Pending with original submit_time preserved (age factor not reset); fair-share usage already charged via Phase 11.

8.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Recovery
FairShareTree diverged from checkpoint Post-replay usage != expected Wrong priority ordering Recompute from sacct records; manual reseed
Decay not applied (bug) fairshare_last_decay_age gauge > 2x decay_interval Heavy users never yield Restart controller; decay applied on next tick
Priority all-zero (weight config bug) priority_computed_max gauge = 0 All jobs same priority; FCFS Fix config, reload; re-sort pending queue
Tree normalization bug (shares > 1.0) Factor > 1.0 detected; saturate at 1.0 + counter Priority inflated for affected account Log + alert; fix config

8.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: fairshare_decay_last_applied_epoch (gauge), priority_compute_pass_duration_ms (histogram), fairshare_tree_accounts_total (gauge), priority_recompute_count (counter).
  • Log per-pass: top-5 jobs by priority with factor breakdown at DEBUG level.
  • sprio -l command (Phase 13): table of job_id, age_factor, fairshare_factor, partition_factor, qos_factor, size_factor, total.
  • Config reload: SIGHUP to controld triggers config re-parse; new weights applied on next pass; tree structure updated atomically.
  • Rollback: weight changes are config-only; revert config + SIGHUP restores prior behavior within one scheduling pass.
  • Audit log: record priority_factors_snapshot for each job at transition Pending->Configuring for postmortem reproducibility.

Phase 9 — Preemption + Gang + QoS

9.1 Objectives

  • Higher-QoS or higher-priority jobs preempt running lower-priority jobs via configurable preempt modes: suspend, requeue, checkpoint.
  • Gang scheduling: co-located jobs in the same gang group time-slice together; noded enforces via cgroup freezer.
  • QoS tiers define limits (max running jobs, max TRES) + preempt_mode + which QoS levels a tier can preempt.
  • Preemption grace period: noded receives PreemptMsg with a deadline; job not suspended/requeuable by deadline is forcibly killed.
  • Enforce I6 (no new jobs on Down/Drain) and prevent preemption loops.
  • Builds on Phase 5 (main pass), Phase 6 (lifecycle), Phase 7 (backfill); all preemption decisions are controller-side (P2).

9.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

QoS model — hpcsched-core::qos — applies P6 (Borg preemption tiers), P10 (Srinivasan gang scheduling), P12 (Condor checkpoint/restart), ADR-12, I7

  • QoS { name, priority: u32, max_jobs_running, max_tres: TresLimit, preempt_mode: PreemptMode, preemptible_by: Vec<QosName>, grp_tres: TresLimit }.
  • PreemptMode enum: { Suspend, Requeue, Checkpoint, Off }.
  • Jobs carry spec.qos; default QoS applied if unset.
  • QoS limits enforced at two gates: submit-time (reject if max_jobs_per_user exceeded) and schedule-time (skip if grp_tres exhausted).

Preemption trigger — hpcsched-sched::preemption

  • In main scheduling pass: if a high-priority job fails try_allocate, enter preemption mode:
    1. Collect candidate victims: Running jobs with qos in preemptor.qos.preemptible_by, lowest priority first.
    2. Simulate: would freeing victims (by PreemptMode) yield enough resources?
    3. If yes: issue PreemptMsg { job_id, mode, grace_deadline } to relevant noded instances via hpcsched-proto.
    4. Reserve resources for preemptor until grace_deadline + fence_buffer.
    5. Transition victims: Running -> Completing (requeue/checkpoint) or Running -> Preempted (suspend).
  • Anti-loop: once a job is marked Preempted, it cannot be immediately preempted again by same QoS tier; cooldown counter.

Gang scheduling — hpcsched-sched::gang + hpcsched-noded::gang

  • Gang group assigned at job-submit if spec.gang_id set or partition has gang_scheduling = true.
  • GangGroup { id, members: Vec<JobId>, timeslice_ms, phase: Enum{Running, Suspended} } in controller state.
  • controld sends GangSliceMsg to noded on timeslice boundary; noded toggles cgroup freezer state for group members.
  • Timeslice period: configurable per partition (default 30 s); controld drives the tick.
  • Only jobs on the same node(s) can be in the same gang group (allocation constraint enforced at Phase 4).

PreemptMode behaviors

Mode Controller action Noded action State transition
Suspend Set victim.state = Preempted; reservation held Freeze cgroup Running -> Preempted
Requeue Free allocation; push back to Pending Kill step processes Running -> Requeued -> Pending
Checkpoint Await checkpoint-complete signal Run checkpoint hook Running -> Completing -> Requeued
Off No preemption of this QoS level — —

Persistence: JobPreempted(job_id, mode, preemptor_id) WAL record before sending PreemptMsg. On replay: reconstruct victim state correctly; preemptor reservation rebuilt from WAL.

Grace period enforcement: controld starts a timer per victim at PreemptMsg send. If noded does not confirm StepExited or CgroupFrozen by grace_deadline, controld marks victim Down-equivalent and forces allocation release.

9.3 Deliverable

  • High-QoS job successfully starts by preempting lower-QoS running jobs.
  • Gang groups time-slice visibly (toggle Running/Preempted per gang phase in squeue).
  • squeue -j <victim_id> shows state = Preempted or Requeued with reason Preempted.
  • Requeued jobs re-enter Pending queue with original submit_time; re-prioritized normally.

9.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
Preemption correctness Preemptor starts within grace_deadline + 2s of preemption trigger
No double-alloc across preemption I1 holds throughout: victim allocation freed before preemptor allocation set
Requeue idempotency Victim requeued; job_id unchanged; submit_time preserved
Gang timeslice Both gang members alternate Running/Suspended at configured timeslice ± 10%
QoS limit enforcement Job exceeding grp_tres rejected at schedule gate; counter incremented
Preemption loop prevention Job not preempted twice by same QoS tier within cooldown window
Grace period expiry Victim not responding: allocation freed within grace_deadline + fence_buffer
WAL replay with preemption Post-crash replay produces identical victim + preemptor states

9.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected output
preempt_suspend_basic.yaml High-QoS job preempts low-QoS job via Suspend Victim Preempted; preemptor Configuring
preempt_requeue.yaml Requeue mode; victim re-enters pending Victim state = Pending; submit_time unchanged
preempt_checkpoint.yaml Checkpoint mode; hook called before requeue checkpoint_hook invoked; then Requeued
preempt_grace_expiry.yaml Noded silent; grace expires Controller forces release; victim -> Failed
gang_timeslice.yaml 2-job gang, 30s timeslice State toggles at 30s intervals
qos_grp_tres_limit.yaml Account at GrpTRES limit Next job stays Pending with reason QosGrpTresLimit
preempt_loop_guard.yaml Victim preempted twice by same QoS Second preemption blocked; cooldown logged
preempt_replay.yaml Crash during grace period Replay: reservation held; preemption re-sent to noded

9.6 Pitfalls

  • TOCTOU in victim selection: victim completes between victim selection and PreemptMsg send. Fix: check victim state is still Running when freeing allocation; if already completed, skip and re-try victim selection.
  • Checkpoint hook hangs: noded checkpoint process blocks indefinitely. Fix: checkpoint operation has independent timeout (configurable); noded kills checkpoint process at timeout; reports CheckpointFailed to controld.
  • Gang starvation: gang group with one large job and one tiny job; tiny job starves because it always shares timeslice. Fix: gang membership requires same-size or proportional resource claim; document as invariant.
  • Preemption of job with dependencies: if job B depends on job A (external dependency tracking), preempting A while B runs causes B to fail. Current scope: no job-dependency tracking; document as limitation; do not preempt jobs that have dependents in Running (check dependents field if present).
  • Suspend + node failure race: job suspended (Preempted), node goes Down. Controller must treat Preempted job on Down node as Requeued, not Suspended. Detect in Phase 10 node-fault path; special-case Preempted -> Requeued on node-down.
  • Grace period too short for checkpointing: large memory job checkpoint takes minutes. Config must allow per-QoS grace_period_s; default 60 s inappropriate for Checkpoint mode. Document minimum per mode.

9.7 Performance Concerns

  • Victim selection: O(N_running) per preemption trigger; cache running jobs by QoS tier for O(1) tier lookup.
  • Preemption is rare (not on every pass); cost is amortized. But grace-period timer per victim adds N_victims timer entries to event loop; use a BinaryHeap<(deadline, JobId)> for O(log N) timer management.
  • Gang timeslice message fan-out: |gang_members| RPCs per tick; batch via BroadcastGangSliceMsg to reduce per-noded RTT.
  • cgroup freeze/thaw on noded: kernel operation, ~1 ms per group; not on the controld critical path.
  • QoS limit checks at schedule gate: maintain running counters per QoS (not recount from job list); O(1) gate check.

9.8 Edge Cases
