  • Preemptor job cancelled while grace period in flight: cancel PreemptMsg effect; restore victim if Suspend mode (un-freeze). Requeue mode victim already in Pending; preemptor cancellation does not re-run it.
  • Multiple preemptors competing for same victim: only one preemptor wins (first to commit to WAL); second must re-enter main pass.
  • Gang member fails during Running phase: gang group dissolved; remaining members run normally (no timeslicing of one job).
  • QoS with preempt_mode = Checkpoint but no checkpoint hook configured: treat as Requeue; log warning per-job.
  • Job with time_limit = None as preemption victim: valid to preempt; no time-limit constraint on victim.
  • Suspend of MPI job: MPI communicator hangs on freeze; document that Suspend mode is unsupported for MPI jobs; check spec.flags for mpi = true and fall back to Requeue.

9.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Recovery
Grace period not enforced (timer bug) preempt_grace_overrun_total counter; victim stays Running Resource not freed; preemptor stuck Configuring Manual scancel victim; audit timer loop
cgroup freeze fails on noded CgroupFreezeError in noded log; StepState still Running Gang members not isolated noded retries 3x; reports FreezeFailure; controld marks node Drain
Preemption WAL write fails Write error before PreemptMsg send Victim not preempted; preemptor stays Pending Scheduling pass retries; WAL error triggers controller failover alarm
Double-alloc post-preemption I1 assertion fails Two jobs share a core Bitmap scan at reconciliation; kill both jobs; re-derive from WAL
Checkpoint hook data loss Hook exits non-zero; no checkpoint file Job cannot resume; re-run from scratch Mark job Requeued with CheckpointFailed annotation in sacct

9.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: preemption_events_total{mode} (counter), preemption_grace_overruns_total (counter), gang_timeslice_events_total (counter), qos_grp_tres_limit_hits_total (counter), preempt_victim_selection_duration_ms (histogram).
  • Log fields per preemption event: preemptor_job_id, victim_job_id, mode, grace_deadline, qos_tier.
  • squeue --reason exposes Preempted + preemptor job_id as reason code.
  • scontrol show job <id> (Phase 13) shows PreemptTime, SuspendTime, RequeueCount.
  • Deployment: preemption disabled by default per QoS (all preempt_mode = Off); operator opts in per QoS config entry.
  • Rollback: set preempt_mode = Off in QoS config + SIGHUP; in-flight preemptions complete; no new ones triggered.
  • Audit: every preemption writes a structured record to the accounting log (hpcsched-acct) for compliance review.

Phase 10 — Node Fault Handling

10.1 Objectives

  • Detect node loss via heartbeat-timeout; transition NodeState to Down without operator intervention.
  • Requeue requeueable jobs stranded on downed nodes; transition non-requeueable jobs to Failed.
  • Support Drain / Draining lifecycle for maintenance windows: block new allocations; allow running jobs to finish.
  • Support forced Down (immediate) via admin RPC distinct from natural timeout.
  • Reconcile CoreBitmap, GpuSet, and Allocation records when a downed node returns to service.
  • Propagate all node-state changes to WAL before mutating in-memory state; comply with P6.
  • Expose node-fault events as structured log records and metrics; operator must not need gdb to diagnose.

10.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Heartbeat tracking — hpcsched-controld: — applies P11 (Barroso graceful degradation), P13 (Fuxi fault tolerance), I6, P7 (graceful degradation principle), ADR-08

Field Type Description
last_heartbeat Instant Controller-local monotonic timestamp of last HeartbeatMsg from node
consecutive_misses u8 Incremented each tick without receipt; reset on arrival
heartbeat_timeout_secs u64 Config: default = 3 × heartbeat_interval_secs
NodeTracker HashMap<NodeId, NodeEntry> Per-node liveness record
FaultEventTx mpsc::Sender<FaultEvent> Channel to scheduling loop for immediate notification
  • Async tick task fires every heartbeat_interval_secs; iterates NodeTracker; for each node where last_heartbeat.elapsed() > heartbeat_timeout_secs and state ∉ {Down, Drain}: trigger fault handler.
  • Fault handler is a separate async task per node (bounded tokio::task::spawn); does not block the tick loop.
  • Use NodeState as an AtomicU8 with compare_exchange to gate idempotent transition; double-fire is harmless.
  • On HeartbeatMsg receipt: update last_heartbeat; reset consecutive_misses; update reported cpus_load, mem_free, gpu_util in node record for scheduler heuristics.
  • NodeEntry also tracks registered_at: Instant and version: String (noded binary version) for operational visibility.

WAL records emitted in fault handling (all in hpcsched-state): NodeDown{node_id, detected_at, method: Timeout|Admin}, JobRequeued{job_id, reason: NodeFault{node_id}, requeued_at}, JobFailed{job_id, reason: NodeFault{node_id}, failed_at}, BitmapFreed{node_id} (confirms forced bitmap clear committed), NodeDrain{node_id, reason, drained_at}, NodeResume{node_id, capacity: CapacitySnapshot, resumed_at}.

Requeue / fail sweep: query jobs_on_node(node_id) (O(running_jobs) scan; index HashMap<NodeId, Vec<JobId>> maintained by allocator). For each affected job: spec.requeue == true → emit JobRequeued, PendingQueue::reinsert(job) with original priority (recomputed next pass via age-factor), release bitmaps on remaining live nodes, alloc = None; spec.requeue == false → emit JobFailed, release bitmaps, update accounting partial record. Multi-node jobs release bitmaps on all other nodes in the allocation even if Alive (job cannot continue without the faulted node). Bitmap free uses CoreBitmap::force_clear_all(node_id) / GpuSet::force_clear_all(node_id) — sets all bits zero unconditionally, logs warning if already zero; alloc_mem reset to 0. Emit FaultEvent{node_id, affected_jobs} to scheduling loop channel after WAL flush.

Drain lifecycle (hpcsched-controld + hpcsched-noded): Idle/Mixed/Allocated --[DrainRPC]--> Draining (if jobs running) or Drain (if idle); Draining --[last job epilog completes]--> Drain; Drain --[ResumeRPC]--> Idle (after capacity check); Drain/Down --[AdminForceDown]--> Down (immediate). Scheduling pass eligible_nodes filter excludes Drain/Draining (documented in hpcsched-alloc::allocate() contract). scontrol update NodeName=X State=DRAIN → DrainNode RPC → controld; State=RESUME → ResumeNode RPC → controld validates capacity, sets Idle.

Node return reconciliation: on ResumeNode RPC, compare incoming CapacitySnapshot (from noded re-register) to stored Node capacity; if reduced (hot-plug removal), emit WARN and keep the reduced value (never silently use old value). Assert alloc_cores.count_ones() == 0 before setting NodeState::Idle; reject resume if non-zero. Emit NodeResume WAL record; update Node in state map.

Integration with scheduling loop: fault handler notifies scheduling loop via a bounded mpsc::Sender<FaultEvent>; scheduling loop drains all pending FaultEvents at the top of each pass and rebuilds eligible_nodes: Vec<NodeId> (filtered on Idle | Mixed, the sole point of truth for allocation eligibility) before computing allocations — ensuring no allocation lands on a node that faulted within the same pass. BackfillPlan reservations on downed nodes are invalidated: Phase 7's reservation set is scrubbed of any Reservation.node_set intersecting the downed node before the next pass.

10.3 Deliverable

  • Controller detects missing heartbeats; transitions nodes to Down; affected jobs appear in squeue with REQUEUED or FAILED state within 2 × heartbeat_timeout_secs.
  • drain + resume workflow operable via scontrol; draining node accepts no new allocations; existing jobs finish.
  • WAL contains NodeDown + JobRequeued/JobFailed + BitmapFreed for every fault event; replay restores correct node and job states after controller restart.
  • sinfo shows per-node STATE column reflecting current NodeState.
  • squeue -R shows reason code for requeued jobs: NodeFault(node_id).
  • Backfill reservations involving downed nodes are cleared; next scheduling pass recomputes clean reservation.

10.4 Acceptance Criteria

Test Pass condition
test_heartbeat_timeout_requeues_jobs Job returns to Pending within 3 × heartbeat_timeout after heartbeat stops
test_non_requeueable_job_fails_on_fault Job.state == Failed; alloc == None; alloc_cores.count_ones() == 0
test_drain_blocks_new_allocs No job allocated to Drain node in 10 consecutive scheduling passes
test_draining_to_drain_on_last_completion Node transitions Draining → Drain after last job epilog; WAL record present
test_node_return_bitmap_assert alloc_cores.count_ones() == 0 asserted; failed assertion rejects resume
test_wal_replay_mid_fault Simulate crash after NodeDown WAL record; replay produces NodeState::Down; jobs in correct terminal state
test_admin_force_down_immediate AdminForceDown RPC transitions node immediately; does not wait for timeout
test_double_fault_idempotent Fault handler fires twice for same node; second invocation is no-op
test_multinode_job_full_release Multi-node job on 4 nodes; 1 node faults; all 4 nodes' bitmaps released
test_backfill_reservation_invalidated Active backfill reservation on faulted node cleared before next pass

10.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture file Purpose Expected output
fault/node_timeout_requeue.yaml Node heartbeat stops; requeue=true job JobRequeued WAL; job in Pending; bitmap zeroed
fault/node_timeout_fail_nonrequeue.yaml Node heartbeat stops; requeue=false job JobFailed WAL; job in Failed; bitmap zeroed
fault/drain_no_new_alloc.yaml Drain node; submit jobs exceeding remaining capacity Drained node not selected; other nodes used
fault/draining_to_drain_transition.yaml Drain issued mid-job; job completes Draining → Drain transition; WAL record
fault/node_return_reconcile_reduced_capacity.yaml Node returns with fewer CPUs WARN logged; capacity updated to reduced value; Idle
fault/wal_replay_mid_fault.yaml WAL snapshot at NodeDown; replay State identical to pre-crash; no phantom allocs
fault/multi_node_simultaneous_fault.yaml 3 nodes down simultaneously All jobs requeued; no scheduling-loop stall

10.6 Pitfalls

  • Bitmap leak on forced free: force_clear_all must be called unconditionally even if noded never confirmed step exit; omitting violates I1; manifests as a node appearing permanently over-allocated after return.
  • Double-requeue race: heartbeat scan fires twice before NodeState committed; use compare_exchange(Allocated, Down) as a gate; second attempt sees Down and exits.
  • Clock skew in timeout computation: heartbeat timestamps are controller-local Instant; never use node-reported time; NTP jumps on the controller node can cause spurious timeouts — use monotonic clock.
  • Drain races with allocator: scheduling pass reads NodeState::Idle before drain RPC writes NodeState::Drain; protect NodeState reads in allocate() with a per-node RwLock; drain write acquires write-lock.
  • Cascade fault storm: 100+ nodes die simultaneously; requeue loop O(nodes × jobs/node) runs synchronously; bound requeue batch size at 256 jobs/tick; emit a single batched WAL record per node to reduce fsync count.
  • Draining stuck on zombie processes: noded must send StepExited even when job process is forcibly killed; without it Draining → Drain never fires; test with SIGKILL to job processes.
  • Capacity mismatch on return not flagged: silent acceptance of wrong capacity violates I2; always compare and emit WARN + operator action required. Requeueable job loses progress: expected in Slurm semantics; checkpoint/restart is application responsibility; document in user guide.

10.7 Performance Concerns

  • Heartbeat map scan: O(n_nodes) per tick; 64k nodes in 1 ms at 1 ns/entry — negligible; HashMap sufficient.
  • Requeue sweep: O(jobs_on_dead_node); typical ≤ 512 jobs/node; pipeline WAL writes as one batch append per node.
  • Bitmap free: O(cores_per_node / 64) word-clear operations; < 1 µs for 2048 cores.
  • Fault handler spawned per node: use a bounded semaphore to prevent spawning > 32 concurrent fault tasks under cascade.
  • Drain check in scheduling pass: eligible_nodes is a pre-filtered Vec<NodeId> rebuilt once per pass, O(n_nodes) amortized over all jobs. Lock contention: per-node RwLock<NodeState> preferred over a single global lock; reduces contention on heartbeat tick vs scheduling pass.

10.8 Edge Cases

  • Node rejoins before timeout fires: HeartbeatMsg received; last_heartbeat updated; consecutive_misses = 0; no state change; if fault handler already started, compare_exchange in handler sees Idle/Allocated (not Down) and aborts.
  • Multi-fault in one tick: all processed sequentially within fault event loop; ordered WAL records; no interleaving.
  • Node returns with increased capacity (hot-add): allowed; update Node.cpus/gpus/mem; emit INFO; no assert failure.
  • Requeueable job with per-submit --no-requeue flag: spec.requeue = false overrides partition default; treated as non-requeueable.
  • Drain node with zero running jobs when drain issued: immediately transitions to Drain (not Draining); must not receive new allocs.
  • Controller restart while node is in Draining: WAL records NodeDrain; replay restores Draining; jobs continue until they complete; then Drain.
  • Repeated drain/resume cycles: each cycle emits WAL records; bitmap assert fires each resume; no state accumulation.

10.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Containment Recovery
Heartbeat task panic heartbeat_scan_last_run metric stale > 2× interval All fault detection blind Supervisor restarts task; controld health-check Restart controld; WAL replay
WAL write fails during fault NodeDown not persisted; bitmap freed in memory After restart: phantom node Idle with missing jobs WAL fsync error → controlled panic Standby promotes (Phase 12)
Double-requeue due to bug Two PendingQueue entries for same JobId Double-launch on next pass; I1 violated PendingQueue::insert deduplicates by JobId Remove duplicate; audit log
Drain RPC lost (network) sinfo still shows Idle; node gets new allocs Maintenance unsafe Operator re-issues; idempotent drain RPC Re-issue scontrol
Draining stuck (zombie process) Draining metric non-zero for > max job time_limit Node unavailable for maintenance Alert; operator forcibly marks Down Resolve zombie; scontrol resume

10.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: node_state_gauge{node_id, state} per NodeState variant; heartbeat_last_seen_seconds{node_id} gauge; node_down_total counter; jobs_requeued_on_fault_total; jobs_failed_on_fault_total; node_drain_active gauge.
  • Alert rule: node_down_total rate > 5/min over 10-min window → critical alert; node_drain_active > 0.1 × n_nodes → warning.
  • Structured log: WARN node_id=X state=Down jobs_requeued=N jobs_failed=M detected_at=T method=Timeout on each fault; INFO node_id=X state=Drain reason=R on drain.
  • Tracing: span node_fault_handler with fields node_id, detected_at, method, jobs_requeued, jobs_failed, bitmap_bits_freed.
  • sinfo output columns: NODELIST, STATE, CPUS, MEMORY, GRES, REASON, SLURMD_START_TIME (noded registration timestamp), LAST_HEARTBEAT.
  • Config: heartbeat_timeout_secs (default 90), heartbeat_interval_secs (default 30), requeue_on_node_fail (partition-level boolean, default true), max_concurrent_fault_handlers (default 32).
  • Rollback: drain is operator-reversible via scontrol update NodeName=X State=RESUME; Down requires node to re-register heartbeat.
  • Migration: NodeState enum variants are WAL-serialized by name; adding a new variant (e.g., Unknown) requires forward-compatible WAL reader with #[serde(other)] fallback to avoid replay failures on older WAL files.

Phase 11 — Accounting + Limits

11.1 Objectives

  • Record per-job TRES (cpu, mem, GPU, node, walltime) usage as authoritative, append-only accounting data.
  • Enforce association / QoS limits at submit time and at each scheduling pass; cover: max_submit_jobs, max_running_jobs, max_tres_per_job, GrpTRES, GrpTRESRunMins, max_wall_duration_per_job.
  • Expose sacct-style query interface with field selection, time-range filter, user/account/state filter.
  • Feed completed-job TRES usage into hpcsched-fairshare decay path.
  • Persist accounting ledger independently of WAL; survive controller restart; support archive/rotation.
  • Enforce invariant I10: TRES-seconds = allocated TRES × elapsed duration within rounding tolerance.

11.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

TRES dimensions and accounting model (hpcsched-acct): — applies P1 (I10: TRES-seconds accounting), P15 (Kalyvianaki decay for fair-share usage), B1 (Feitelson workload characterization), ADR-12 (single-threaded accounting pass), I8 (limit enforcement before scheduling), I10 (TRES-seconds correctness invariant)

TRES = Trackable Resource; every schedulable unit has a TRES type, enabling heterogeneous limit enforcement and fair-share computation across resource types: cpu (core, alloc_cpus × elapsed_ms), mem (MB, alloc_mem_mb × elapsed_ms), gpu (device, alloc_gpus × elapsed_ms), node (node, alloc_nodes × elapsed_ms), billing (weighted sum Σ(tres_weight_i × tres_i)).

AcctRecord (hpcsched-acct): job_id: JobId (primary key), user/account/qos: String, submit_time: i64 (Unix ms), start_time/end_time: Option<i64> (set on Running / terminal state), final_state: JobState (Completed/Failed/Cancelled/Preempted), alloc_cpus: u32, alloc_mem_mb: u64, alloc_gpus: u32, alloc_nodes: u32, tres_usage: TresUsage (computed on completion). TresUsage{cpu_ms, mem_mb_ms, gpu_ms, node_ms: u64} uses millisecond precision (avoids I10 rounding failure for short jobs) = alloc_tres × (end_time − start_time); preempted jobs accumulate segments. AcctRecord events Submitted/Started/Completed/Failed/Cancelled/Preempted/Requeued each append a JSON-lines entry to acct.log.

Accounting ledger persistence: append-only $state_dir/acct.log, one JSON-lines record per event; independent of WAL (no cross-file transactions, reconciled on startup — see 11.6); rotated daily or by size (max_acct_log_mb) to acct-YYYYMMDD.log.gz (archived records need --archive); batch-fsync every acct_fsync_interval_ms (default 1000 ms, 1 s loss window — WAL remains the authoritative durability source).

In-memory indexes (rebuilt on startup from ledger): user_index: HashMap<String, BTreeSet<JobId>>, account_index: HashMap<String, BTreeSet<JobId>>, time_index: BTreeMap<i64 /* submit_hour */, Vec<JobId>>, job_index: HashMap<JobId, AcctRecord>.

Association / QoS limit enforcement:

Limit Storage Enforcement point Action on violation
max_submit_jobs Association.max_submit_jobs submit_handler Reject; LimitExceeded { reason } RPC error
max_running_jobs Association.max_running_jobs Scheduling pass eligible-set filter Skip job; mark eligible = false for this pass
max_tres_per_job / min_tres_per_job (cpu/mem/gpu) Association + QoS Submit + pass Reject at submit (over max or under min); skip at pass
max_wall_duration_per_job QoS.max_wall_duration_secs Submit Reject if spec.time_limit > limit
GrpTRES / GrpTRESRunMins (group ceilings) Association.grp_tres / grp_tres_run_mins Pass, per-account aggregation Skip all jobs in assoc until under ceiling; run-mins computed from running-job remaining time
QoS priority boost QoS.priority_factor Priority computation Add qos_factor × weight_qos to PriorityFactors.qos
max_nodes_per_job Partition + Association Submit + pass Reject / skip

AssociationTree mirrors Account/Association from hpcsched-core; GrpUsage traversal is O(depth), max depth ≤ 8 in practice — computed once at scheduling-pass start (sum Allocation.tres per node in tree) and cached for the pass, giving O(1) per-job checks after initial aggregation. QoS record: { name, max_jobs: Option<u32>, max_tres: Option<TresLimit>, max_wall_secs: Option<u64>, priority_factor: f32, preempt_mode: PreemptMode, grp_tres: Option<TresLimit> }. Precedence: QoS > Association > Partition defaults. Admin sreport tabulates TRES usage by user/account/partition over a time range, backed by an aggregating sacct query.

Fair-share feed: on job_completed/job_failed, call hpcsched-fairshare::record_usage(association, tres: TresUsage); hpcsched-fairshare holds Association.usage_raw and applies half-life decay on its own schedule (fairshare_decay_interval_secs) — the two crates stay decoupled via this single function call.

sacct RPC and CLI: SacctQuery{user, account, start_time, end_time: Option, states: Vec<JobState>, job_ids: Vec<JobId>, format: Vec<SacctField>, partition: Option<String>, archive: bool}; handler intersects indexes, streams matching AcctRecords with field projection (SacctField: JobID,User,Account,State,Submit,Start,End,Elapsed,AllocCPUS,ReqMem,ReqGRES,CPUTime,TresUsage,Partition,QOS,NodeList). CLI formats as aligned text or --parsable2 pipe-delimited; --archive additionally scans monthly archive files; responses paginate in chunks of 1000 via a SacctContinuation token.

11.3 Deliverable

  • Every completed / failed / cancelled job has an AcctRecord with correct TresUsage; I10 holds within 1-second rounding.
  • Submit RPC rejects jobs violating max_tres_per_job, max_wall_duration_per_job, max_submit_jobs with structured error.
  • Scheduling pass skips jobs over max_running_jobs and GrpTRES limits.
  • sacct -u <user> --starttime <T> returns formatted records.
  • hpcsched-fairshare tree receives usage updates after each job terminal event.
  • sreport cluster utilization computes TRES utilization ratio per partition over a time window from accounting records.
  • Ledger survives controller restart; sacct returns identical records post-replay.

11.4 Acceptance Criteria

Test Pass condition
test_tres_usage_cpu_ms_accuracy cpu_ms == alloc_cpus × elapsed_ms; I10 holds ≤ 1000 ms rounding
test_tres_usage_preempted_segments Sum of segment durations equals total usage
test_submit_rejected_max_jobs LimitExceeded error; job absent from queue
test_submit_rejected_max_tres LimitExceeded on cpu/mem/gpu over-request
test_grptres_blocks_account All jobs in account skipped when GrpTRES exceeded; other accounts unaffected
test_grptresmins_limit Scheduling pass skips when accumulated run-mins exceed limit
test_sacct_query_filters User + time-range filter returns correct subset; field projection correct
test_acct_ledger_replay Restart; replay ledger; sacct returns identical records
test_fairshare_usage_updated Association.usage_raw increments after job completion
test_i10_invariant Fuzz: random job durations / allocs; I10 holds in all cases

11.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected output
acct/tres_usage_simple.yaml Single CPU job 60 s; 4 CPUs cpu_ms = 240000; node_ms = 60000
acct/tres_usage_gpu_job.yaml 2 GPUs, 120 s gpu_ms = 240000
acct/max_submit_jobs_reject.yaml User at max_submit_jobs=5; submit 6th 6th submit returns LimitExceeded
acct/max_tres_per_job_reject.yaml QoS max_cpus_per_job=32; submit 64-cpu job Rejected at submit
acct/grptres_running_limit.yaml Account at GrpCPUs=128; 130-cpu running Next job in account skipped; other account proceeds
acct/qos_wall_limit_reject.yaml QoS max_wall_duration=24h; submit 48h job Rejected at submit
acct/sacct_time_range.yaml 10 jobs over 3 days; query middle day Exactly the middle-day jobs returned
acct/ledger_replay_idempotent.yaml Replay acct.log twice Record count unchanged; no duplicates
acct/fairshare_feed.yaml Job completes; check usage_raw in association usage_raw incremented by job TRES

11.6 Pitfalls

  • GrpUsage stale mid-pass: aggregate at pass start; a job completing mid-pass does not reduce the group limit within that pass (conservative; correct; next pass adjusts). Document this one-pass lag.
  • Millisecond precision skipped: u64 seconds truncates sub-60-second jobs to zero cpu_seconds; use u64 milliseconds internally, convert to seconds only for display.
  • Association not found at schedule time: user submits --account not in tree; accept at submit (warn) but treat as no-limits; or reject (policy choice — document in config). Ledger / WAL divergence after crash: crash between WAL JobCompleted append and ledger append; on startup assert ledger has a corresponding terminal record for each WAL JobCompleted, append if absent (idempotent reconcile).
  • Index rebuild time on large history: millions of records; partition by year/month; load only recent N months on startup; serve archive queries with on-demand decompression.
  • Preempted job TRES double-count: each requeue cycle opens a new Started record; ensure final TRES = sum of segment durations only, not alloc × total_wall_since_first_submit. GrpTRESRunMins over-conservative: remaining minutes for a job = time_limit - elapsed; if time_limit is unset, default to partition max_time; never use infinity.

11.7 Performance Concerns

  • Ledger append: one write per event; batch-fsync every 1 s; per-event fsync is 100× slower — avoid.
  • GrpUsage aggregation: O(n_assoc × n_running); memoize per pass; invalidate only on JobStarted/JobCompleted events between passes.
  • sacct query: O(matching_records) with index lookup; streaming response avoids allocation of full result set in memory; max 100k records per query page.
  • Limit checks in scheduling pass: precompute per-account/per-user running counters once per pass; O(1) check per job amortized.
  • Association tree depth ≤ 8 levels typical (O(8) traversal negligible, cache GrpUsage per level for the pass); ledger index rebuild O(n_records), ≤10M records/month typical, target < 5 s for 1M records.

11.8 Edge Cases

  • Job cancelled before start: TresUsage = {0, 0, 0, 0}; ledger record still appended for audit trail.
  • Job preempted and requeued multiple times: each run segment appended; final TresUsage = sum of all segments; verify with I10 per segment, not total.
  • Association deleted while jobs pending: cache association snapshot at submit time; do not orphan accounting; log warning.
  • GrpTRESRunMins with heterogeneous time limits: use min remaining time across jobs in account as conservative estimate; not the average.
  • TRES overflow: cpu_ms for 65536 CPUs × 30 days × 86400 × 1000 ≈ 1.7 × 10^14; fits u64 (max ≈ 1.8 × 10^19).
  • Clock skew between noded exit-time and controld receipt: use controld-local elapsed time (start_time also set by controld); immune to noded clock drift. QoS deleted while jobs running: jobs retain their QoS label in AcctRecord; limits no longer enforced; log warning.

11.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Containment Recovery
Ledger disk full write returns ENOSPC; metric acct_write_errors_total New accounting records lost Alert; rotate/archive; resume appending Rotate log; hpcsched-admin acct-reconcile
GrpUsage bug over-counts Jobs unfairly blocked; sacct aggregation diverges from live counter Account starvation Add assertion: live counter ≤ sacct aggregate Fix bug; restart; rebuild index
WAL / ledger divergence Startup reconciliation reports gaps Accounting undercount Reconcile loop fills gaps on startup Automatic on restart
Limit enforcement gap (submit vs schedule); Index rebuild OOM GrpTRES exceeded, jobs running over limit; startup OOM on large history Overcommit (soft); controld fails to start Bitmap check (I1/I2) is independent hard guard; limit startup load to recent N months Scheduling pass corrects next tick; configure acct_max_startup_months

11.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: accounting_records_total{event_type}; limit_exceeded_total{limit_type}; acct_log_size_bytes; grptres_utilization{account} gauge (running TRES / GrpTRES ceiling); sacct_query_duration_ms histogram; acct_ledger_lag_secs (time since last fsync).
  • Alert: grptres_utilization{account} > 0.95 → warn; limit_exceeded_total rate spike → investigate submit pattern.
  • Log: INFO job_id=X tres_usage={cpu_ms=Y,gpu_ms=Z} account=A elapsed_ms=E on each terminal event; WARN account=X grptres_limit_hit=true on scheduling skip; ERROR acct_ledger_write_failed on I/O error.
  • Tracing: span acct_record_append with job_id, event_type, tres_usage; span limit_check with limit_type, account, result; span sacct_query with filter_summary, records_returned, latency_ms.
  • sacct flags: --starttime, --endtime, --user, --account, --state, --format, --parsable2, --archive, --noheader, --delimiter.
  • Ledger rotation config: max_acct_log_mb (default 1024), acct_rotate_interval (default daily), acct_archive_dir, acct_archive_compress (default zstd).
  • Deployment: no schema migration on new TRES dimensions (old records lack the new field → zero-default in deserialization); AcctRecord uses #[serde(default)] on all optional fields, unknown fields ignored via #[serde(deny_unknown_fields = false)].

Phase 12 — Controller HA / Failover

12.1 Objectives

  • Primary controld writes WAL + checkpoints to a shared durable store visible to standby.
  • Standby continuously tails WAL; maintains shadow state within a bounded lag.
  • Failover promotes standby to primary within a target RTO (≤ 30 s); no manual repair required.
  • Fencing ensures at most one controller allocates resources at any instant; I9 holds across failover.
  • No job double-launched across failover; epoch numbering enforced by node daemons.
  • Operator-visible failover events; cluster continues scheduling after promotion.

12.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Shared durable store abstraction (hpcsched-state): — applies P13 (Fuxi WAL-based recovery), B7 (Kleppmann Ch.9 consensus), B9 (Gray&Reuter Ch.9 force rule), P10 (crash-only), ADR-06, ADR-30, I9

DurableStore: Send + Sync trait exposes: append_wal(&[WalRecord]) -> Result<WalOffset>, write_checkpoint(&Checkpoint, WalOffset) -> Result<()>, read_since(WalOffset) -> Result<impl Iterator<Item=WalRecord>>, committed_offset() -> Result<WalOffset>, acquire_lock(name, ttl_secs) -> Result<LockGuard> / release_lock(LockGuard), write_epoch(u64) / read_epoch() -> Result<u64>. Default implementation: POSIX shared filesystem (NFS/GPFS); WAL segments wal-NNNNNN.log (fixed-size, e.g. 64 MB); checkpoint checkpoint-NNNNNN.snap (temp-file + rename for atomicity); lock is a lease file controld.lock (O_CREAT|O_EXCL, primary writes PID + expiry, renews every lock_ttl/3); committed_offset lives in a small wal.offset file written after each segment fsync, so standby never reads past committed data.

Primary role and WAL write path: on startup, attempt acquire_lock("controld-primary", lock_ttl_secs); if acquired, read current epoch, increment, write_epoch + fsync, then begin accepting RPCs. Every mutating operation serializes a WalRecord, buffers in a 64 KB segment, and on segment-full or wal_flush_interval_ms timeout calls append_wal + fsync before acking the RPC caller (P6: ack only after fsync). Checkpoint fires every checkpoint_interval_records (atomic rename, updates wal.offset post-checkpoint); lock renewal runs every lock_ttl/3 — 3 consecutive failures trigger a controlled panic and standby promotion.

Standby role: on startup with lock acquire failed, enters StandbyMode{lag_records: AtomicU64}; polls committed_offset() every standby_poll_interval_ms (default 500 ms), and on new data calls read_since(last_offset) and applies via StateLog::replay_record() — the identical code path used for crash recovery (P8). Standby never accepts client RPCs (returns NotPrimary{primary_addr}); lag_records = committed_offset − standby_offset is exposed as a metric.

Fencing protocol: (1) primary lock TTL expires (crash or partition); (2) standby detects the expired lock via acquire_lock polling but waits one full TTL before acquiring — the fence window ensures the old primary's in-flight RPCs time out and it stops issuing commands; (3) standby then acquire_locks, atomically increments the epoch (write_epoch + fsync), and broadcasts EpochUpdate{new_epoch} to all registered nodeds (async fan-out); (4) standby transitions to primary role and begins accepting RPCs; (5) noded, on reconnect, receives HelloAck{controller_epoch}, updates known_epoch, and drops any incoming LaunchMsg/AllocMsg whose epoch < known_epoch.

Epoch propagation (hpcsched-proto): controller_epoch: u64 is carried on LaunchMsg and CancelMsg (noded rejects if epoch < known_epoch), HeartbeatAck and HelloAck (noded updates known_epoch if higher), and EpochUpdate{new_epoch} (pushed by the new primary on promotion). Noded's known_epoch: AtomicU64 only ever increments via compare_and_swap — never decrements; the epoch is embedded in WAL records for auditability.

State transfer correctness: standby applies WAL records through the identical replay_record function used in crash recovery — no separate standby-apply path (P8). On promotion, standby verifies standby_offset == committed_offset (applying any remaining gap before accepting RPCs), then schedules an async, non-blocking re-inventory of all nodeds (HelloMsg fan-out) to refresh heartbeat timestamps and capacity — the scheduler begins accepting submits before re-inventory completes, using the promotion Instant as the new heartbeat-timeout baseline. ShadowState reuses the same ControllerState struct (no separate type, minimizing divergence risk); standby exposes read-only squeue/sinfo RPCs from shadow state, labeled with lag_records.

Checkpoint format: Checkpoint{version: u16, wal_offset: WalOffset, jobs: Vec<Job>, nodes: Vec<Node>, allocations: Vec<Allocation>, pending_queue: SerializedQueue, fairshare_tree: SerializedTree, epoch: u64}, zstd-compressed, target < 100 MB for 10k jobs + 1k nodes. Standby loads the latest checkpoint on startup (if available) then tails WAL from the checkpoint offset, avoiding full replay; a 32-bit CRC of the compressed payload is stored in the file header and verified on load; a version mismatch falls back to full WAL replay from offset 0 (WARN checkpoint_version_mismatch). Naming: checkpoint-{wal_offset}.snap.zst, with checkpoint-latest.snap.zst symlinked atomically after write.

12.3 Deliverable

  • Primary crash → standby promotes within lock_ttl + promotion_latency; scheduling resumes; squeue reflects correct state.
  • No job launched twice across failover (epoch check visible in noded log as StaleEpoch drops).
  • WAL and checkpoint on shared store are readable by standby without copying to local disk.
  • sinfo --ha shows primary/standby addresses, epoch, standby lag in records and estimated seconds.
  • Standby serves read-only squeue / sinfo RPCs during normal operation; lag_records in response header.
  • hpcsched-admin failover --force triggers controlled failover for maintenance (graceful primary handoff without TTL wait).

12.4 Acceptance Criteria

Test Pass condition
test_failover_no_double_launch Kill primary mid-schedule; each job launched ≤ 1 time total across failover
test_epoch_fence_rejects_stale_launch Old primary sends LaunchMsg with old epoch; noded drops with StaleEpoch log
test_standby_lag_within_sla Standby shadow-state lag ≤ 2 s behind primary under 1000 jobs/s submission load
test_promotion_state_identical Standby state after checkpoint + tail equals primary state at point of failure (I5)
test_rto_under_30s Failover detected and new primary serving RPCs within 30 s
test_split_brain_prevention Two standby candidates race; exactly one acquires lock; other stays standby
test_epoch_persisted_before_rpc Simulate promotion + crash before epoch fsync; next promotion uses higher epoch
test_wal_segment_boundary Standby follows across WAL segment rotation; no gap in applied records
test_controlled_failover hpcsched-admin failover --force; primary shuts down cleanly; standby acquires immediately
test_readonly_standby_squeue squeue RPC to standby returns correct shadow state with lag_records in response

12.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected output
ha/primary_crash_mid_alloc.yaml Primary crashes after WAL JobAllocated but before noded LaunchMsg Standby promotes; job transitions to Configuring → Running; not double-launched
ha/split_brain_epoch.yaml Old + new primary both send LaunchMsg; different epochs Noded logs StaleEpoch; drops old-epoch message; I9 holds
ha/standby_promotion_state.yaml Snapshot primary state; kill primary; compare standby post-promotion States identical; no phantom allocations
ha/lock_renewal_failure.yaml Primary lock renewal times out 3×; primary panics Standby acquires lock after TTL; epoch incremented
ha/noded_reconnect_epoch.yaml Noded reconnects to promoted standby Noded known_epoch updated; normal heartbeat resumes
ha/wal_segment_rotation.yaml WAL rotates to new segment during standby tail Standby reads across boundary; no gap; lag bounded
ha/checkpoint_load_tail.yaml Standby loads checkpoint + short WAL tail State identical to primary; startup faster than full replay

12.6 Pitfalls

  • TTL sizing: lock_ttl_secs must be > worst-case NFS fsync latency × max-in-flight-RPCs; too short → spurious failovers; too long → high RTO; recommend 90 s for typical HPC NFS.
  • Fence window too short: standby acquires lock before old primary's in-flight RPC fsync completes; old primary may still issue a LaunchMsg after promotion; always wait one full TTL.
  • Epoch not persisted before accepting RPCs: standby increments epoch in memory, crashes before write_epoch fsync, next standby uses same epoch; fencing fails; I9 violated. Always write_epoch + fsync before accepting first RPC.
  • committed_offset file not atomic: primary writes WAL segment, then updates wal.offset; standby reads wal.offset before segment fsync completes; use segment fsync + wal.offset write as an atomic pair (write offset only after segment fsync).
  • Standby diverges via separate apply path: if standby-apply code differs from crash-replay code, state diverges silently; enforce single replay_record function used by both paths.
  • Noded known_epoch resets on reconnect: reconnect must send current known_epoch to new primary; new primary responds with its epoch; noded takes max; never resets to 0.
  • WAL tail read past uncommitted data: standby reads a WAL segment partially written by old primary; use committed_offset sentinel; never read past it.
  • POSIX rename not atomic across some NFS implementations: use O_CREAT|O_EXCL for lock file (atomic on POSIX); for checkpoint rename, verify NFS mount option noac or use etcd for lock if NFS rename atomicity untrustworthy.
  • Multiple standbys fighting for lock: TTL-based backoff with jitter prevents livelock; recommend single standby per deployment.

12.7 Performance Concerns

  • WAL buffering strategy: accumulate records in a VecDeque<WalRecord> up to 64 KB or wal_flush_interval_ms; batch flush reduces NFS fsync calls from O(RPCs) to O(time / interval).
  • WAL append (primary): one 64 KB buffered write + fsync to NFS per batch; NFS fsync latency typically 5–20 ms; this is the dominant scheduling throughput limit; pipeline: accept RPCs, batch WAL records, fsync, ack.
  • Standby poll loop: 500 ms poll interval; NFS read of wal.offset = 1 RTT; use inotify/kqueue on local NFS mount where supported to eliminate polling.
  • Checkpoint write: zstd-compressed snapshot; target < 100 MB; write time < 2 s; do not block scheduling loop — write on a dedicated background thread.
  • Epoch broadcast on promotion: fan-out to n_nodes × 1 RPC each; use tokio::spawn fan-out; bounded by noded reconnect backoff, not broadcast serialization.
  • Lock renewal: must complete within lock_ttl / 3; alert if round-trip > lock_ttl / 6; use a watchdog timer.
  • Checkpoint load on standby startup: single zstd decompression + deserialization; target < 5 s for 100 MB snapshot.

12.8 Edge Cases

  • Network partition heals during fence window: old primary re-acquires lock before standby; both believe they are primary; second epoch write wins; noded epoch check resolves; only one epoch value can hold the lock at any time.
  • Primary partitioned (not crashed): holds lock; standby blocked for full TTL; all client RPCs time out; RTO = lock_ttl; size TTL appropriately (default 90 s).
  • WAL corruption in shared store: standby detects checksum mismatch on record; halts promotion with CorruptWal error; does not promote on corrupted state (availability sacrificed for correctness).
  • Checkpoint absent (first boot or after compaction gap): standby replays full WAL from offset 0; may be slow for large history; enforce WAL compaction policy.
  • Promoted standby crashes before serving any RPC: lock TTL expires; second standby (if configured) acquires; epoch incremented again; correct.
  • JobId sequence across failover: max_job_id persisted in WAL; replay reconstructs max; new primary issues max + 1; no ID collision.
  • Dual-homed noded: noded connects to new primary on second NIC; EpochUpdate arrives on first NIC; known_epoch updated regardless of NIC; epoch is per-daemon, not per-connection.
  • Standby clock skew vs primary: standby uses its own Instant for heartbeat timeout tracking after promotion; re-inventory all nodeds on promotion to reset heartbeat clocks.
  • WAL segment mid-write on primary crash: last segment may be partially written; standby detects truncated record via CRC; replays up to last valid record; discards partial tail.

12.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Containment Recovery
Shared store unreachable (NFS failure) WAL append returns I/O error; alert All RPCs fail (P6: ack after fsync) Controller enters DegradedMode; no new allocs Restore NFS; controller resumes; standby catches up
Epoch not written before promotion write_epoch error on promotion attempt Fencing incomplete; I9 at risk Treat as promotion failure; retry after TTL Investigate store write failure; retry
Standby state divergence Post-failover: job state mismatch in squeue Wrong scheduling decisions Compare standby to WAL replay from checkpoint Reload from checkpoint; re-replay; alert on divergence
Lock starvation (two standbys) Neither acquires; primary gone; cluster offline Full scheduling outage TTL + jitter backoff; recommend single standby Operator clears stale lock file; standby acquires
WAL segment gap (primary skipped rotation record) Standby committed_offset discontinuity Standby stuck at gap Halt standby; alert operator hpcsched-admin wal-repair --fill-gap
Checkpoint write too slow Write time > checkpoint_interval Checkpoint age grows; recovery time increases Move checkpoint write off hot path Tune checkpoint_interval_records; faster NFS

12.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: controller_role{role="primary"|"standby"} gauge; wal_append_latency_ms histogram; standby_lag_records gauge; standby_lag_seconds gauge (derived); failover_total counter; lock_renewal_failures_total; checkpoint_write_duration_ms; wal_committed_offset; wal_segment_count.
  • Log: INFO promoted to primary epoch=N lag_records_applied=M duration_ms=T on failover; WARN standby_lag_records=X when lag > max_lag_records_warn (default 1000); ERROR wal_append_failed error=... on store write failure; INFO checkpoint_written offset=N size_mb=M duration_ms=T.
  • Tracing: span wal_append with record_count, bytes, latency_ms, segment_id; span failover_promotion with epoch, lag_records_applied, duration_ms; span checkpoint_write with offset, size_bytes, compress_ratio.
  • sinfo --ha columns: PRIMARY_ADDR, STANDBY_ADDR, EPOCH, STANDBY_LAG_RECORDS, STANDBY_LAG_SECS, LAST_CHECKPOINT_OFFSET, LOCK_EXPIRES_IN.
  • Config: lock_ttl_secs (default 90), standby_poll_interval_ms (default 500), checkpoint_interval_records (default 10000), max_wal_segment_bytes (default 64 MB), wal_flush_interval_ms (default 100), max_lag_records_warn (default 1000).
  • Deployment: primary + one standby; shared NFS must have sync export option; no_root_squash if controld runs as root; document NFS rename atomicity requirement.
  • WAL retention: WAL segments older than the latest checkpoint + wal_retention_extra_segments (default 2) are eligible for deletion; hpcsched-admin wal-compact performs cleanup.
  • WAL format versioning: each record has version: u8; reader skips unknown record types (forward compatibility); old reader refusing new version → block promotion until upgraded (backward incompatibility).
  • Rolling upgrade: drain cluster; upgrade standby binary; fail over to standby (now new version); upgrade old primary; return to primary role; failover again if desired.
  • Rollback: if new version introduces incompatible WAL records, rollback requires restoring from last pre-upgrade checkpoint; document per release.
  • Controlled failover: hpcsched-admin failover --force sends GracefulHandoff RPC to primary; primary flushes WAL, releases lock, shuts down; standby acquires immediately (no TTL wait); epoch still incremented.

Phase 13 — CLI Suite + Observability

13.1 Objectives

  • Deliver all user-facing binaries in hpcsched-cli: sbatch, srun, squeue, scancel, sinfo, sacct.
  • Expose Prometheus-compatible metrics endpoint from hpcsched-controld.
  • Structured tracing (OpenTelemetry spans) across submit → schedule → launch → complete path.
  • squeue pending/running views with column filtering and format strings.
  • sinfo node and partition state tables with reason/drain annotation.
  • sacct queries over accounting records with time-range, user, account, state filters.
  • All CLI binaries connect to controld via hpcsched-proto RPCs; no direct DB access.

13.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

hpcsched-cli crate layout: — applies P1 (R1: Slurm CLI taxonomy as reference), ADR-04 (MessagePack RPC encoding over CLI boundary), ADR-09 (TLS client auth for CLI→controld), B3 (Kleppmann CLI usability for ops tooling), I7 (job state transitions observable via CLI without bypassing FSM)

hpcsched-cli/src/
  bin/
    sbatch.rs    — parse #SBATCH directives + CLI flags → SubmitReq RPC
    srun.rs      — interactive step: allocate or attach, launch step, stream stdio
    squeue.rs    — ListJobs RPC → formatted table; --format, --user, --partition, --state filters
    scancel.rs   — CancelJob RPC; --user, --partition, --state wildcard cancel
    sinfo.rs     — ListNodes + ListPartitions RPCs → node/partition table
    sacct.rs     — QueryAcct RPC → TRES usage table; --starttime, --endtime, --user, --state
  lib.rs         — shared: RPC client construction, output formatting, error rendering
  format.rs      — column spec parser; printf-style `%j %u %T %R` substitution
  table.rs       — columnar output: fixed-width, separator, header, sort

Metrics (controld): /metrics HTTP endpoint (port configurable, default 9100) via axum/hyper, registered through the prometheus crate at startup and updated from the scheduling loop and job lifecycle hooks. Families: hpcsched_queue_depth (gauge, pending jobs by partition), hpcsched_node_utilization_cpu/_gpu (gauge, allocated/total per node), hpcsched_sched_pass_latency_seconds / hpcsched_backfill_pass_latency_seconds (histogram, wall time per pass), hpcsched_backfill_jobs_filled (counter), hpcsched_fairshare_gini (gauge, Gini over active users' shares), hpcsched_job_wait_seconds (histogram, submit→start latency by partition), hpcsched_failover_events_total (counter), hpcsched_wal_write_latency_seconds (histogram).

Tracing: tracing + tracing-opentelemetry; spans submit_job, schedule_pass, backfill_pass, alloc_job, launch_step, requeue_job carry attributes job.id, job.user, job.partition, job.state, node.id. OTLP exporter configurable via OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT; falls back to tracing-subscriber JSON logs.

squeue output format:

JOBID  PARTITION  NAME   USER   ST   TIME    NODES  NODELIST(REASON)
12345  gpu        train  alice  R    2:14:03 4      n[001-004]
12346  cpu        prep   bob    PD   0:00    0      (Resources)
  • --format overrides columns using %j %P %j %u %T %M %D %R tokens.
  • --sort by field; default: state then priority desc.
  • --noheader, --json output modes.

sinfo output:

PARTITION  AVAIL  TIMELIMIT  NODES  STATE  NODELIST
gpu        up     7-00:00    8      idle   n[001-008]
cpu        up     1-00:00    24     mix    n[009-032]
  • --nodes for per-node view: NODE STATE CPUS MEMORY GRES REASON.

sacct columns: JobID | User | Account | State | Elapsed | AllocCPUS | AllocGPUS | ReqMem | CPUTime | Start | End.

13.3 Deliverable

  • All six CLI binaries compile and connect to a running controld.
  • squeue renders live queue state; sinfo renders node/partition state.
  • sacct queries completed job records with filter combinations.
  • sbatch submits jobs, returns job ID.
  • /metrics endpoint scraped by Prometheus; Grafana dashboard skeleton included as dashboards/hpcsched.json.
  • OpenTelemetry traces visible in Jaeger with full submit→complete span tree.

13.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
squeue --json output parses as valid JSON all fields present
sinfo reflects node drain reason within 1 heartbeat interval <10 s
sacct --starttime filter returns correct subset 100% accuracy vs. DB
/metrics responds in <5 ms p99
hpcsched_sched_pass_latency_seconds histogram populated after 100 passes nonzero counts in all buckets

13.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Purpose Expected Output
fixtures/cli/squeue_basic.toml 3 jobs: 1 running, 2 pending; verify column order exact table string
fixtures/cli/squeue_format.toml custom --format "%j %u %T" 3-column output

fixtures/cli/scancel_user.toml cancel all jobs for user; verify state transitions all → Cancelled
fixtures/cli/metrics_scrape.txt Prometheus text-format snapshot hpcsched_queue_depth line present
fixtures/cli/sbatch_bad_time.toml --time exceeds partition max error, no job created

13.6 Pitfalls

  • Stale squeue output: CLI caches RPC response; if connection reuse races with controld restart, old data served. Mitigation: no client-side caching; each invocation makes a fresh RPC.
  • Metric cardinality explosion: per-job labels on histograms create unbounded series. Fix: only partition/state labels on job metrics; no job-id labels.
  • Column truncation masking reasons: NODELIST(REASON) field truncated at 20 chars hides drain reason. Signal: truncated strings in sinfo; fix: --long mode disables truncation.
  • sacct slow scan on large DB: full-table scan for time range without index. Mitigation: index on (submit_time, user_id); query plan verified in fixture.
  • Trace sampling at high throughput: 100% sampling at 10k jobs/min floods OTLP collector. Fix: configurable sampling rate; default 1% for schedule_pass spans. srun stdio hang on noded failure: interactive step blocks waiting for EOF on stdio pipe if noded crashes mid-step. Detection: timeout on stdio read; noded sends EOS on step termination regardless of process exit.

13.7 Performance Concerns

  • squeue RPC serializes entire job list; at 10k jobs, response can reach ~5 MB. Mitigation: server-side filtering by state/user/partition before serialization; pagination cursor.
  • Metrics endpoint computation (Gini coefficient for fairshare) is O(N users); cap at O(N log N) with sorted array; compute lazily on scrape, not in scheduling loop.
  • sacct DB queries block on hpcsched-acct read lock; use read-only snapshot transaction to avoid blocking scheduling writes.
  • Histogram bucket boundaries for sched_pass_latency_seconds: use exponential buckets from 1 ms to 10 s to capture tail accurately.

13.8 Edge Cases

  • squeue called during controller failover: RPC returns transient error; CLI retries 3x with 500 ms backoff before exiting non-zero.
  • scancel on already-completed job: idempotent; returns success with "job already terminal" message.
  • sinfo when all nodes are down: table renders with down state; not an error.
  • sacct with future --starttime: returns empty result, exit 0.
  • sbatch #SBATCH directives conflict with CLI flags: CLI flags take precedence; warning printed.
  • Metrics scrape during scheduling pass: metrics registry uses atomic counters; no lock taken during scrape. OTLP endpoint unreachable: tracing degrades to local log output; no impact on job submission.

13.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Recovery
CLI binary cannot reach controld RPC timeout user-visible only; no scheduler impact check controld health, retry
Metrics endpoint panics HTTP 500 / no scrape alerting gap; no scheduler impact restart metrics server thread
sacct DB corruption / OTLP exporter deadlocks query returns error / trace buffer full acct queries fail (scheduling unaffected) / submit latency increase restore from checkpoint / disable OTLP, use log fallback

13.10 Operational Notes

  • All CLI binaries read HPCSCHED_CONTROL_ADDR env var; fallback to /etc/hpcsched/hpcsched.conf ControlMachine field.
  • squeue --iterate N refreshes display every N seconds (curses-free; ANSI escape clear).
  • Log level controlled by RUST_LOG; structured JSON logs to stderr; human-readable default.
  • Metrics port configurable in controld.toml under [metrics] port = 9100; rolling log rotation via tracing-appender with 100 MB / 7-day retention default.
  • Deployment: CLI binaries statically linked (musl target); no runtime deps; copy to compute nodes for local squeue/sinfo.
  • Dashboard hpcsched.json imports into Grafana 9+ via grafana-cli; panels: queue depth, utilization, pass latency, backfill rate, fairshare Gini trend.

Phase 14 — Scale + Soak + Conformance

14.1 Objectives

  • Validate scheduling-pass latency at 10k+ pending jobs meets target (≤ 500 ms p99).
  • Soak test: continuous submit/cancel/complete churn for ≥ 24 hours with zero crash, no double-allocation, no accounting drift.
  • Protocol fuzz: hpcsched-proto message parser rejects all malformed inputs without panic.
  • Conformance fixtures encode Slurm semantics for backfill, fair-share, preemption, node-fault, QoS limits, WAL replay — regression-safe across refactors.
  • Performance regression CI gate: fail build if pass latency or memory regresses > 10%.

14.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Scale harness (benches/ and tests/scale/): — applies P8 (B1 Feitelson workload model for fixture generation), P9 (depth-limit enforcement regression), P2 (Lifka 1995 EASY invariant conformance fixtures), ADR-22 (backfill depth constant under test), Back-of-Envelope CM9 (pass latency prediction guides regression budget)

  • bench_sched_pass (populate in-memory state with N = {100, 1k, 5k, 10k, 50k} jobs + M nodes, invoke single scheduling pass, record wall time via criterion) and bench_backfill_pass (10k pending, 200 nodes half-allocated, full backfill, record depth-limited scan time).
  • bench_alloc_bitmap (1k concurrent alloc/free on CoreBitmap/GpuSet, throughput + cache-miss rate via perf stat), bench_fairshare_recompute (5k users in deep association tree, target < 50 ms), bench_wal_throughput (append 100k WAL records, measure fsync batching, target > 50k records/s on NVMe).

Soak harness (tests/soak/soak_runner.rs): launches controld + N simulated noded in-process (hpcsched-noded library API) with worker threads submit_worker (random ResourceSpec, random user/account), cancel_worker (random Pending jobs), complete_worker (simulated completion after random duration). Invariant checkers run every 10 s: I1/I2 (sum node bitmaps vs. live allocation records), I3 (every Running job has a valid Allocation), I5 (crash + replay compared to pre-crash snapshot), I10 (accounting TRES-seconds vs. alloc × elapsed within 1%). Failure injection kills random noded, partitions noded from controld for 30 s, and kills+restarts controld; duration is configurable (CI 1 hour, release gate 24 hours); output is a structured JSON log of all invariant checks, failures, restarts.

Protocol fuzz (fuzz/ using cargo-fuzz/libfuzzer): fuzz_proto_deserialize (arbitrary bytes into every hpcsched-proto message type via prost decode, assert no panic), fuzz_submit_rpc (random SubmitReq fields including zero nodes, u64::MAX time limit, empty partition name), fuzz_wal_replay (random WAL byte stream must not panic, may Err), fuzz_cli_args (arbitrary argv to each CLI binary, assert no panic, only exit codes 0/1/2). Corpus seeds from real workload captures with structured mutation for valid-but-boundary inputs.

Conformance fixture suite (fixtures/conformance/):

Fixture file Slurm semantic encoded Pass condition
backfill_basic.yaml / backfill_no_delay.yaml top job reserved, lower fits before reservation; delaying candidate rejected I4: completion ≤ reservation start; top job start time unchanged
fairshare_decay.yaml / fairshare_tree.yaml heavy user yields priority after decay period; child account usage rolls up to parent ranking inversion after T_decay; parent usage = sum of children
preempt_requeue.yaml / preempt_grace.yaml higher-QoS job preempts running lower-QoS; grace period before SIGKILL lower transitions Preempted → Requeued; noded receives SIGTERM first
node_down_requeue.yaml / node_drain_finish.yaml heartbeat timeout → node Down → job Requeued; Drain lets existing jobs finish, no new allocs job re-enters Pending; post-drain alloc count = 0
qos_limit_max_jobs.yaml user at QoS max_jobs limit → submit rejected error: QoS limit
wal_replay_idempotent.yaml replay same WAL twice → identical state state hash equals
partition_time_limit.yaml job time_limit > partition.max_time → rejected error at submit
gang_timeslice.yaml two co-scheduled jobs alternate on same nodes both Running; timeslice events
failover_no_double_alloc.yaml controld failover mid-allocation; no resource double-alloc I9 holds
acct_tres_accounting.yaml cpu-seconds = alloc_cpus × elapsed ± 1% I10 holds

Conformance runner (tests/conformance/runner.rs): loads each YAML fixture (initial cluster state + event sequence + expected final state), drives in-process controld, applies events, and compares the state snapshot to expected — on failure, diffs expected vs. actual and emits the failing event trace. CI requires all conformance fixtures to pass on every PR.

Performance regression gate (.cargo/ci.toml/Makefile): criterion stores baseline in target/criterion/, CI compares with --load-baseline main; gate fails the build if sched_pass_10k p99 > 500 ms, backfill_10k p99 > 1 s, or any benchmark regresses > 10%.

  • cargo bench suite runs and records results for all scale benchmarks.
  • Soak harness runs 1+ hour in CI with all invariant checks green.
  • All 10 conformance fixtures pass.
  • Fuzz targets compile; 10-minute fuzz run finds zero panics.
  • Performance regression gate integrated in CI pipeline.
  • CONFORMANCE.md documents each fixture's Slurm semantic and expected behavior.

14.5 Conformance Fixtures

Covered in 14.2 table. Additional fixture metadata:

Field Convention
Format YAML with sections: cluster, events, expected_state
Cluster section nodes list, partitions, accounts, QoS definitions
Events section ordered list of {type, payload, at_tick}
Expected state job states, node states, allocation maps, accounting totals
Malformed cases fixtures/conformance/malformed/: bad YAML, unknown event types, negative TRES values — runner must reject with parse error, not panic
Regression tags each fixture tagged with the Slurm version it encodes (e.g., slurm: 23.x); no separate metadata file

14.6 Pitfalls

  • Criterion baseline drift: baseline committed to repo becomes stale after hardware change; gate flags false regressions. Mitigation: re-baseline explicitly on infra change; document in CI notes.
  • Soak worker starvation: submit_worker outpaces complete_worker → queue depth grows unboundedly → OOM. Fix: token-bucket submit rate; soak config specifies max_queue_depth.
  • Fuzz corpus explosion: unbounded corpus growth slows fuzz CI. Mitigation: libfuzzer corpus minimization step before commit; max corpus size 10k inputs.
  • Conformance fixture fragility: exact timestamp comparison fails on clock jitter. Fix: compare state topology (job states, allocations) not absolute timestamps; use tick-based simulation.
  • In-process noded simulation divergence: simulated noded skips real cgroup enforcement, hiding resource-limit bugs. Mitigation: integration test variant that spawns real noded binaries in a network namespace. Double-free in bitmap under concurrent soak: race in CoreBitmap::free if called from two threads for same job. Detection: invariant checker I1/I2 triggers; fix: bitmap mutation always under per-node lock.

14.7 Performance Concerns

  • sched_pass at 10k jobs: O(N) job iteration + O(N × M) alloc attempts in worst case. Must short-circuit on first infeasible node; cache eligible-node sets per partition.
  • backfill_pass depth limit: cap scan at 500 jobs by default; configurable BACKFILL_MAX_JOBS. Without cap, O(N²) time-forward simulation degrades to minutes.
  • Fair-share recompute: tree traversal with decay is O(users × depth); memoize subtree usage sums; do not recompute unchanged subtrees.
  • Bitmap operations: CoreBitmap on 1024-core nodes = 16 × u64; SIMD popcount via popcnt instruction; avoid heap allocation in hot alloc path.
  • WAL write batching: group multiple job events into one fdatasync; target 10 ms batch window; measure with bench_wal_throughput.
  • Soak memory growth: per-job Allocation held in HashMap; completed jobs must be evicted to hpcsched-acct within 60 s of completion (check RSS every 5 min in soak). Scheduling loop lock: single RwLock over controller state blocks all RPCs during pass; Phase 14 validates this is acceptable at target pass latency, else split into per-partition locks.

14.8 Edge Cases

  • 10k jobs all for the same single node: scheduling pass must still complete within target; eligible-set cache prevents redundant bitmap probes.
  • Soak: node comes back up mid-backfill-plan: backfill plan references stale node state; alloc must re-validate node availability atomically before committing.
  • WAL replay of truncated record (crash mid-write): replay detects partial record via checksum; truncates to last complete record; logs warning; continues.
  • Conformance fixture with zero nodes: runner rejects as invalid cluster spec; not a scheduling edge case.
  • Fuzz input that decodes to valid protobuf but semantically invalid (e.g., nodes: 0, time_limit: u64::MAX): validation layer in controld rejects before scheduling; fuzz verifies no panic.
  • Soak: controld failover during active backfill plan: standby resumes from WAL; backfill plan is not persisted (ephemeral); standby recomputes on next pass, no double-allocation because bitmaps are in WAL state. criterion run on machine with no stable TSC: timing noise causes spurious failures; gate uses 3-run median, not single sample.

14.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Recovery
Soak: invariant I1 violated (double-alloc) checker fires, soak aborts test-only; not production bisect event log to offending allocation; fix bitmap lock
Bench regression > 10% CI gate fails PR blocked profile with perf/flamegraph; identify O(N²) path
Conformance fixture failure on refactor CI fails PR blocked diff expected vs. actual state; trace event sequence
Fuzz: panic in proto deserialize cargo fuzz reports crash potential controld crash on malformed RPC add input to regression corpus; fix parser
Soak OOM; Failover fencing failure (I9 violation) process killed by OOM killer; failover_no_double_alloc.yaml fails test-only; critical production risk reduce submit rate, check job eviction to acct; audit fencing implementation in Phase 12, add fencing integration test

14.10 Operational Notes

  • Soak harness configurable via tests/soak/config.toml: duration_secs, num_nodes, submit_rate_hz, cancel_fraction, node_failure_rate.
  • CI pipeline stages: (1) unit tests, (2) conformance fixtures, (3) criterion benchmarks + regression gate, (4) 1-hour soak, (5) fuzz 10-min corpus run.
  • Fuzz corpora stored in fuzz/corpus/; committed to repo; cargo fuzz run resumes from corpus.
  • Benchmark baselines stored in benches/baselines/; updated via make update-baselines on intentional performance changes; PR must justify regression.
  • CONFORMANCE.md maps each fixture to the Slurm source file or paper section that defines the semantic (e.g., backfill_basic.yaml → sched/backfill/backfill.c:_attempt_backfill()).
  • Soak logs are structured JSON; post-soak analysis script scripts/analyze_soak.py computes invariant-check pass rate, restart count, queue-depth time-series.
  • Release gate: all four CI stages must pass on main before tagging a release; soak duration increased to 24 hours for release candidates.

Threat Model

Trust Boundaries

Reference the canonical architecture diagram. Four trust zones:

Zone Components Trust Level Enforcement
T1: Admin plane controld, config files, WAL/checkpoint store Fully trusted; root-equivalent Filesystem DAC; mTLS server cert
T2: Node daemons noded per compute node Trusted-but-compartmentalized; authenticated by mutual TLS Per-node client cert; cert CN must match registered NodeId
T3: CLI clients sbatch/srun/squeue/scancel/sacct User-trusted; authenticated by PAM/mTLS; authorization by uid/account UID propagated in RPC header; controld verifies against association table
T4: External users, network, job processes inside cgroups Untrusted; minimal surface cgroup v2 isolation; no direct socket access; read-only proc view

Data flows and trust crossings:

  • T3→T1 (submit/cancel/query): authenticated RPC; authorization at controller; rate-limited.
  • T2→T1 (heartbeat/resource report): mTLS; noded cannot initiate allocation changes — only controld does.
  • T1→T2 (launch/cancel step): controld-initiated over mTLS; noded executes, does not validate allocation correctness (controller is authoritative, per P2).
  • T4 job processes have no path to T1 or T2 sockets; cgroup net_cls optionally isolates network.

A compromised noded cannot forge allocations; it can only report resource usage and launch steps it was explicitly instructed to launch. Controld validates all resource reports against its own bitmap state before updating.

Threat Table

ID Threat Attack Vector Impact Mitigation Detection Telemetry Recovery
TH-01 Malicious job submission — job spec crafted to cause integer overflow in ResourceSpec fields (nodes/cpus/mem/time) CLI -> submit RPC Controller OOM / wrong allocation Saturating arithmetic + validated bounds (max_nodes, max_time from partition config); reject at deserialization RPC error rate spike rpc_submit_rejected_total{reason} counter Auto: reject; none needed
TH-02 Resource spec inflation — user requests 0 nodes or maxu64 GPUs to corrupt CoreBitmap/GpuSet submit RPC payload Double-allocation (I1 violation) Validate ResourceSpec at ingress: nodes∈[1, partition.max_nodes], time∈[1, partition.max_time], gpus≤node.gpus Structured log WARN on reject spec_validation_failures_total Auto: reject with descriptive error
TH-03 Rogue node daemon — attacker controls a noded and sends false heartbeat (reports idle when actually broken) HeartbeatMsg forgery Jobs dispatched to broken node → fail mTLS client certificates per node; controld verifies cert CN == NodeId Jobs on node fail/timeout; heartbeat content sanity checks noded_heartbeat_anomaly_total Auto: mark node Down; requeue jobs (Phase 10); revoke cert
TH-04 Replay attack — re-send a captured LaunchMsg to launch a step twice RPC replay Double process spawn; resource double-use Message nonces + monotonic sequence per session; noded rejects duplicate StepId Duplicate StepId log duplicate_launch_attempts_total Auto: idempotent reject by noded
TH-05 WAL/checkpoint tampering — operator or rogue process corrupts state files Filesystem access to WAL store Incorrect state replay; phantom jobs WAL records have CRC32C checksum; replay aborts on bad checksum; checkpoint has SHA-256 hash in header WAL replay error logged at CRITICAL wal_checksum_errors_total Manual: restore from last clean checkpoint; escalate
TH-06 Denial of service via job flood — one user submits 10k jobs/sec to exhaust scheduler memory and loop time sbatch in a tight loop Scheduling loop latency blows SLO (P9) Per-user submit rate limit (configurable max_submit_rate); pending queue depth cap per association; reject with 429-equivalent Queue depth gauge spike; latency histogram pending_queue_depth, submit_rate_limit_drops_total Auto: rate-limit; shed load
TH-07 Priority inversion via fake fair-share tree — crafted account hierarchy to inflate own fairshare score Account create API One user monopolizes cluster Association/account creation restricted to admin uid; fairshare formula inputs validated; weights are read-only config Audit log of account creation fairshare_score_outlier alert Manual: audit accounts; reset usage
TH-08 Preemption abuse — submit high-QoS job to preempt all running jobs, then cancel it Legitimate submit + cancel Cluster churn; other users evicted QoS preempt tier assignment is admin-only; preemption grace period (P9); rate-limit preemption events per user High preemption rate alert preemption_events_total{reason} Auto: grace period dampens; manual: audit QoS assignments
TH-09 Memory exhaustion via large job arrays — pending queue unbounded growth submit array of 1M jobs OOM kill of controld Array expansion lazily; global pending cap; queue eviction of lowest-priority if cap hit Heap allocation metrics heap_allocated_bytes, oom_events_total Auto: reject submissions above cap; manual: raise memory or reduce cap
TH-10 Supply-chain — malicious Rust crate injected via Cargo dependency Build-time Backdoor in scheduler binary Cargo.lock pinned; cargo deny checks advisories + licenses; reproducible builds; minimal external dependencies (prefer std) CI advisory scan CI audit log Manual: bump/patch crate; re-pin
TH-11 Privilege escalation via job process — job escapes cgroup and accesses controld socket Cgroup breakout Read/write scheduler state noded runs as unprivileged user; controld socket protected by DAC (mode 0600, root); cgroup v2 mandatory cgroup_escape_detected (kernel audit) Kernel audit events Manual: isolate node, drain, reimimage
TH-12 HA fencing failure — split-brain: both primary and standby believe they are active Network partition during failover Double-allocation (I9 violation) Lease-based fencing: only lease-holder writes allocations; lease has TTL; standby waits for TTL+δ before promoting Split-brain detection: duplicate allocation in WAL fencing_violations_total Manual: force-terminate one controller; reconcile WAL
TH-13 Operator misconfiguration — node declared with inflated CPU/mem; scheduler over-allocates Config file Node OOM; job failure Config parsed with strict type validation; sanity checks (mem_per_node ≤ physical RAM reported by noded); warn on mismatch noded reports actual vs configured divergence node_capacity_mismatch_total Auto: use noded-reported capacity (conservative); alert operator
TH-14 Protocol fuzzing — malformed protobuf frames crash controld RPC input Scheduler crash; jobs stranded prost/protobuf deserialization bounded by frame-length prefix; unrecognized fields ignored per proto3 semantics; AFL/cargo-fuzz in CI (Phase 14) Panic/structured error log rpc_deserialization_errors_total Auto: per-connection error; no global crash
